B11 (boundary semantics — strict `<` documented for `HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD`, `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW`, `DAMAGE_RECENT_WINDOW`; aligned in C.5.3, G.6, entities.yaml). IMPORTANT cluster: V/A.3 line 706 antecedent disambiguated; C.5.3 30s first-pass design intent + Scenario C false-negative class acknowledged; D.4 `t_lastGracePulse[axis]` anchored-at-`t_0` semantics clarified; H.11f/H.11g Light-axis cooldown ACs added.
>
> **Round-2/3/4 specialists consulted (cumulative)**: game-designer, systems-designer, network-programmer, ai-programmer, audio-director, qa-lead, creative-director (round-4 senior synthesis). Round-4 patch is single-session-applied per `.claude/docs/context-management.md` "one theme per session" guidance. **Round-5 design-review must run in a fresh session** before advancing to Theme 2 (Roblox API verification).

## Overview

**Player Controller** is the player-side input and movement surface for Terranova: a thin customization layer over Roblox `Humanoid` that owns first-person locomotion (walk, sprint, jump, crouch-equivalent), the **lantern raise/lower** state, the **squad coordination affordances** (quick-ping and emote wheel), and the death/respawn input flow. From the player's seat, it is the body — every loud action (sprinting, raising the lantern) and every quiet action (walking, pinging, emoting) is routed through this controller. From the system seat, it is the **publisher of `Sprint` and `Light` emissions** to the Ecological Disturbance service (per `ED.C.3.2`), the **owner of the movement-delta and state-entry-grace timers** that gate stationary-attenuation (per `ED.C.1.1` / `ED.G.5`), and the **cross-platform input router** that makes touch, mouse, and gamepad first-class. Because Pillar 1 (Quiet Is Power) is enforced by what counts as loud, and because Pillar 2 (The Squad Is the Experience) is enforced by what coordination affordances exist on every platform, Player Controller is where two of the four game pillars are mechanically realised. The rest of the game watches the controller's outputs.

## Player Fantasy

**The Loud Choice.**

You walk. Walking is the default because walking is survival — every footfall the predator doesn't hear is a footfall you bought. But your friend is bleeding out two ridges away and the oxygen pool is shrinking, so you sprint. The sprint is loud — **not because each pulse booms, but because sprinting hands the world a faster drumbeat**: walking publishes nothing, while a sprint publishes a pulse every two seconds, each one barely louder than a raised lantern's glow (0.10 vs 0.08 — and even sprinting in place still publishes 0.09, per the [R13-F1] hold-floor). What the predator gets from your sprint is *more frequent information*, not a single shout — a steady stream of fixes on where your body is working. You imagine it leaving you: the rhythm your body is making is now somewhere on the map, and you can't help picturing something out there catching it, pulse after pulse — the game never tells you whether anything actually heard, and that unanswered dread is the point. You ping your friend's location for the squad. You flash the "follow me" emote. Nobody has spoken. The lantern stays down until you absolutely need it. Loudness is a transaction billed by the pulse, not a movement option, and you are the one signing — every two seconds. *(Round-16 prose reconciliation, R15 game NEW-1: "loud" here is pulse-RATE, not per-pulse magnitude — the 0.08/0.09/0.10 magnitude gradient is deliberately near-flat; the mechanical loudness of sprint is its cadence. R13-F1 is NOT reopened. **Round-18 R17-1 (ludonarrative honesty, user-ruled): the prior "you feel it leave you… something is listening" wording was rewritten to inference. The controller emits the pulse (C.8.1) but delivers NO real-time predator-response feedback channel — there is no signal that tells the player a predator turned its head — so the Player-Fantasy prose must frame the dread as the player's own inference under uncertainty, never as a delivered feedback the round can't build.**)*

**Anchor moment** — the 2–5 seconds this controller must deliver: you're crouched at chest-high cover, lantern lowered, friend three meters ahead in the dark. You've both been moving loud for a while now — two sprints to cross the last ridge, the lantern up longer than it should have been — and as you settle the world *stirs*: a low, place-less swell with no direction to it, the world telling you the heat it's been holding has just **tipped over a line**. It doesn't tell you where anything is, and it doesn't pin the blame on any one footfall — it tells you the squad's noise has been adding up and just crossed into worse territory. You can't help picturing something out there working with more than it had a minute ago — but the game never confirms it, and that unanswered dread is the point. You tap a quick-ping on the cave mouth ahead, mash the "quiet" emote, and watch your friend freeze mid-step. Ninety seconds without anyone speaking aloud. *(Round-22 R21-1 reconciliation, user-ruled 2026-06-14: the world-response cue (V/A.3) fires on a **cumulative Ecological-Disturbance band-crossing** — hysteresis-gated, ~2–4 per run — NOT on a single sprint pulse, so the prose must read it as accumulated-squad-noise crossing a threshold, never as an immediate per-sprint response. The prior round-20 wording "the noise you just made" / "a fix your sprint put on the board" overclaimed per-sprint traceability the cue cannot deliver at the 2-player floor; corrected to honest cumulative-threshold framing. Round-20 had already retracted the earlier "hear the predator drag itself across stone" positional-audio promise V/A.3's anti-pillar forbids — that retraction stands. The cue stays **non-positional/non-directional** — "the heat the squad raised crossed a line," never "the predator is over there" — with the same inference-under-uncertainty framing as the Loud Choice prose above. **Round-24 GD-1 (R23-1(c), user-confirmed 2026-06-15): dropped the residual "you're part of why" per-actor-attribution tail** — at the 2-player floor a cumulative band-crossing cannot honestly assign blame to *this* player's own noise vs the squad's accumulated noise, so the prose now claims only that the squad's noise crossed a line, never that you personally caused it.)*

**Pillars served**

- **Pillar 1 — Quiet Is Power**: *"Stealth and restraint are mechanically rewarded. Loud play accelerates the predator."* Player Controller is where the contest between quiet and loud is fought every input. Walk vs. sprint, lantern down vs. lantern up, gather here vs. one zone over — every decision routes through this controller, and the controller publishes the consequence. **The proactive-quiet in-run signal is the always-on disturbance bar (HUD/ED-owned) plus the OQ.9 onboarding teaching beat — NOT a PC cue (round-24 R23-1(a) / GD-2/GD-3, user-confirmed 2026-06-15):** the world-response cue (V/A.3) closes only the **reactive** half of this loop and fires **one-directionally, on an upward band-crossing only**. Silence-as-reward is the Pillar-1 grammar; a symmetric proactive-quiet cue would convert dread into a meter for players to probe and optimize (the GD-5 threshold-probing degenerate, OQ.10). The disturbance bar already shows the squad its standing in real time — the cue's job is to make a *worsening* crossing perceptible, never to reward quiet with its own positive chime.
- **Pillar 2 — The Squad Is the Experience**: *"Multiplayer is core. Communication, role-splitting, and shared risk define the fantasy."* Quick-ping and the emote wheel are not accessories to the body — they *are* the body when voice chat is not assumed. Coordination on every platform (touch, mouse, gamepad) lives here, or it lives nowhere.
- **Pillar 3 — The World Watches**: every Sprint and Light emission this controller publishes is the world watching. The controller is the seam where player intent becomes ecological consequence.

**Reference grounding**

| Reference | What this controller takes |
|---|---|
| *Alien: Isolation* | The body is a liability that publishes you to a listener; sound radius is a survival statistic, not a feature. |
| *Doors* (Roblox) | Restraint over reflex; the input pace the player chooses is the survival pace. |
| *Apeirophobia* (Roblox) | Co-op fragility expressed through the *absence* of action — walking together, lantern down, no one spending silence carelessly. |
| *Subnautica* | The single-intelligent-predator philosophy means every input is evaluated against a watcher you respect. |

**What the player should feel** — that the controller is not a power fantasy. The body is slow. Sprinting and lighting are deliberate transactions paid in disturbance. The squad coordinates without voice and trusts the controller to make that coordination first-class on whatever device they're on. The horror, when it comes, is not random — *you summoned it.*

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

#### C.1 — Locomotion

1. **Walk speed** (default move) = `WALK_SPEED = 12 studs/s`. The player's quiet floor; **walk publishes no Sprint emission**. Roblox `Humanoid` default of 16 was rejected as too brisk for the "fragile body" fantasy.
2. **Sprint speed** = `SPRINT_SPEED = 20 studs/s` (1.67× walk). Engages on sprint-state entry; **publishes Sprint emissions** to Ecological Disturbance per `ED.C.3.2`.
3. **Sprint binding** is **per-platform**:
   - **Touch**: toggle (single tap of sprint button starts; tap again to stop). Avoids two-thumb hold fatigue over 5–20 min sessions on iPhone SE-class hardware.
   - **Keyboard/Mouse**: hold (`Left Shift`). Genre-universal convention.
   - **Gamepad**: hold (`L3` press-and-hold, NOT click-toggle). L3-click-as-toggle misfires under grip stress in predator encounters.
   *Rationale*: same intent (sprint costs effort), different friction per platform.
4. **Jump**: enabled (Roblox `Humanoid` default). **Allowed mid-sprint**. Jump does not suppress, reset, or rate-limit Sprint or Light pulse intervals — pulse timers are server-clock interval-based, not reset-on-interrupt. Bunny-hop exploits are precluded by the interval timer's authority.
5. **Fall damage**: **none**. Oxygen and predator contact are the only health/death vectors. Adding fall damage would create "unfair death" friction conflicting with Pillar 4 (Rounds Not Saves — every death must feel earned).
6. **Crouch**: **not in MVP**. Walk is the quiet floor; a third locomotion tier is deferred to v2.

#### C.2 — Stamina

1. **Stamina is server-authoritative**. Maximum = `STAMINA_MAX = 100` units; abstract units (not seconds) preserve drain/regen tuning independence.
2. **Drain** while sprinting = `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE = 12.5 units/s` → 8 seconds of full sprint at maximum stamina.
3. **Regen** while not sprinting = `STAMINA_REGEN_RATE = 10 units/s`. Regen is intentionally slower than drain (ratio 0.8) — an 8-second sprint requires 10 seconds of walking to fully restore, which makes "sprint everywhere, walk on empty" a losing strategy.
4. **Regen delay** after sprint stops = `STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY = 1.5 s`. Prevents bar-twitch on partial-sprint inputs and makes sprint-burst micro-management feel committed.
5. **At 0 stamina**: forced walk (server reverts `Humanoid.WalkSpeed` to `WALK_SPEED`; sprint input is ignored until stamina > 0). No additional cooldown — slow regen is the natural drain-floor; a cooldown on top of slow regen would be double-punishment.
6. **No final pulse on depletion**: when sprint state exits because stamina hit 0 mid-frame, the next pulse interval does not fire. Per `ED.C.1.1`, pulses fire while sprint state is *active*; exiting state stops the timer.
7. **HUD coupling**: server pushes `OnStaminaChanged` (current stamina, normalized 0.0–1.0) at ≤5 Hz to the owning client only — for the HUD bar. The client never uses this value to gate input; the server gates everything.

#### C.3 — Lantern

1. **Toggle binding**: single press/tap toggles between `LanternLowered` and `LanternRaised`. Defaults: `F` (PC); `D-pad Down` (gamepad); dedicated lantern button in HUD upper-left zone (touch — physically separated from sprint+stick zone to defend against predator-encounter mis-tap, per pitfall flagged in `Section E.6`).
2. **Raised state**: server publishes `LightEmission` to Ecological Disturbance per `ED.C.3.2` (`magnitude = MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE = 0.08`, interval `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL = 4 s`). Visual rendering: `LANTERN_VISIBILITY_RADIUS = 20 studs` (player-side rendering only — not a gameplay mechanic; HUD/art-bible owns visuals).
3. **Lowered state**: silent. No emissions.
4. **Lantern is orthogonal to sprint state** — both can be active simultaneously. Sprint and Light pulses fire on independent intervals with independent movement-delta trackers and independent grace-window timers. **Concurrent use is the maximum-loud state by design** — the cost is additive; the squad has a reason to say "lantern down" while sprinting.
5. **No fuel mechanic**. The lantern is a binary state owner, not a resource. Two simultaneous resources (oxygen + lantern) would exceed MVP complexity ceiling.

#### C.4 — Squad Coordination

1. **Quick-ping**:
   - **Input**: two-finger simultaneous tap (touch); `G` (PC); `L1 / LB` (gamepad).
   - **What is pinged**: client raycasts from camera center on input. If the ray hits a tagged entity (`enemy`, `resource`, `door`, `exit`) within `PING_RAYCAST_RANGE = 80 studs`, ping carries location + entity type. Otherwise location only.
   - **Server validation**: server validates the ping ray *origin* against the player's server-tracked `HumanoidRootPart.Position` within `PING_ORIGIN_TOLERANCE` before broadcasting, then re-runs the raycast server-side and uses the server's result (the client's `candidateTargetId` is advisory only — per E.F). **The server does NOT validate camera angle** (camera orientation is not server-tracked, so a camera-angle plausibility check is unenforceable — R6-B4); ping legitimacy rests on origin-position plausibility plus the server-authoritative raycast (per game-concept's "server validates plausibility" rule).
   - **Persistence**: `PING_DISPLAY_DURATION = 8 s`.
   - **Rate limit**: per-player `PING_COOLDOWN_PER_PLAYER = 1.0 s`; burst cap `PING_BURST_CAP = 3` pings per `PING_BURST_WINDOW = 1.5 s`.
   - **Categories**: Danger (red, enemy tag), Resource (green, resource tag), Navigate (yellow, terrain/door/exit), Untagged (white, location-only).
   - **Not a disturbance source**: pings publish nothing to Ecological Disturbance. **Player-mental-model note (round-16 game/UX)**: the ping's world marker is squad-screen UI only — nothing about a ping exists in any channel the predator perceives (no emission, no positional audio, no world object). HUD/teaching copy MUST NOT imply otherwise: a player who builds the false "pings attract the predator" correlation becomes too scared to ping, a Pillar-2 coordination regression. The marker renders only on squad clients.
2. **Emote wheel**:
   - **Input**: `T` hold (PC); `D-pad Up` hold ≥0.3 s (gamepad); dedicated emote button (touch, lower-right HUD zone — **tap-target ≥ `EMOTE_TOUCH_TARGET_MIN = 44 px` with ≥ `EMOTE_TOUCH_SEPARATION_MIN = 12 px` minimum separation from BOTH the Jump button AND the right-thumb gather / tap-hold zone edge (C.7 / T9), round-28 T-3 ux NEW-1**, parity with the Sprint button's target. **Measurement convention (round-28 ux-B1): `EMOTE_TOUCH_SEPARATION_MIN` is measured EDGE-TO-EDGE — the gap between the nearer edges of the emote-button hit region and the adjacent control's hit region — NOT center-to-center (a center-to-center reading would let the targets overlap and collapse the guard on iPhone-SE-class screens).** **Unit (round-28 ux narrow-gate fix): `px` = Roblox `UDim2` `Offset` — device-independent pixels resolved through `UIScale`, NOT CSS/iOS points; 44 is the Apple-HIG 44 dp accessible-minimum mapped onto Roblox's offset layer.** The **gather/tap-hold zone is the higher adjacency risk** (not Jump): a mis-tap there converts a gather *intent* into a wheel-open + T9 lockout — the exact failure this knob bounds; the separation floor therefore applies to the gather-zone edge as well as Jump. Without a sized/separated target a mis-tap on iPhone-SE-class screens opens the wheel and engages the T9 gather lockout for the full `EMOTE_WHEEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT`. **Close behavior (round-28 T-3 ux NEW-3 — resolves the "closes on release" vs "hold-Up-then-cycle" contradiction):** for the **press-and-hold radial gesture** (PC `T`-hold; gamepad hold-then-stick-select) the wheel **closes on release**; for **touch** it closes on a **second tap**; for the **D-pad accessibility fallback** (below) the wheel **LATCHES open** and closes only on commit (`A`/`Cross`), cancel (`D-pad Down`), `EMOTE_WHEEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT`, or the **C.5.4 T5 death force-close** (round-28 ux-B2 — the fourth close-path, applicable to every latch/hold mode; see the death-while-latched note below) — it does NOT close on release of `D-pad Up` (else releasing `D-pad Up` to press `D-pad Left/Right` would close the wheel before a slot could be cycled).
   - **Slot count**: 6. Slot ordering by predicted use frequency: (1) Quiet, (2) Follow me, (3) Danger, (4) All clear, (5) Wait, (6) Help/SOS. Slots 1–3 are functionally locked; slots 4–6 are cosmetically replaceable via Robux purchases (animation-swap with semantic preservation — cosmetic-boundary compliant per game-concept's anti-pillars).
   - **Rate limit**: per-player `EMOTE_COOLDOWN_PER_PLAYER = 3.0 s`; burst cap `EMOTE_BURST_CAP = 5` emotes per `EMOTE_BURST_WINDOW = 15.0 s`.
   - **Display**: `EMOTE_DISPLAY_DURATION = 4 s` on-screen indicator; animation plays to completion (~1.5–2.5 s).
   - **Accidental-open lockout (round-16 game/UX — the wheel previously had no timeout)**: an open emote wheel with no slot selection auto-closes after `EMOTE_WHEEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT = 4.0 s` (G.4; range 2–8 s). On auto-close: no emote fires, no cooldown is charged, and the T9 touch gather lock releases. Without this, an accidental open (palm brush on the touch emote button, a held D-pad Up under grip stress) left the wheel open indefinitely — locking out tap-hold gather on touch (T9) until the player noticed.
   - **D-pad fallback (accessibility)**: `D-pad Up` (hold 0.3 s) opens and **LATCHES the wheel open** (round-28 T-3 ux NEW-3 — it does NOT close on release of `D-pad Up`, so the player releases and then cycles); `D-pad Left/Right` cycles slots; `A / Cross` commits (and closes); `D-pad Down` cancels (and closes); the wheel also closes on `EMOTE_WHEEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT`; and the wheel **force-closes on the C.5.4 T5 death input-lock** (the fourth close-path — enumerated here so an implementer building the latch state machine from this list does not miss it, round-28 ux-B2). **Death-while-latched (round-28 ux gate IMPORTANT):** if the player enters S4 (death) with the wheel latched open, the wheel **force-closes and the T9 client gather lock releases as part of the C.5.4 hard input-lock at T5** — so dying with the wheel open cannot trap touch input on respawn (the idle-timeout would also release it within ≤ 4 s, but the T5 force-close makes the release immediate and explicit). Fully operable without right stick. (The latch-open close-rule is the same one stated in the Input close-behavior clause above — the two are now consistent, closing the round-23/round-27 ux spec contradiction.)
   - **Emotes generate ZERO disturbance**. Server-validated. (Per game-concept anti-pillar: emote audio uses fixed UI-only channels, never positional 3D audio.)
3. **Mutual exclusion**: while emote wheel is open on touch, tap-hold gather is **locked out** on the right thumb side (gesture-conflict mitigation). Player must close the wheel before gathering. *This is a state-machine rule, not a feel knob — see C.7 transition T9.* **Touch-path-only scope (round-28 game-I1):** this lockout is gated on the `isTouch` input context, NOT on the bare "wheel is open" state — there is no tap-hold gather gesture on gamepad or PC, so an open emote wheel (including the gamepad D-pad latch) MUST NOT suppress gather on those platforms (an implementer wiring the lockout to wheel-open state alone would block gamepad gather unnecessarily).

#### C.5 — Death and Respawn

1. **Trigger conditions** (authoritative list — any one fires the death flow):
   - `Humanoid.Health` reaches 0 (predator kill — primary mechanism in MVP)
   - Squad oxygen pool reaches 0 *and* the per-player oxygen-grace timer expires (the grace timer is owned by the Resource Management GDD — Player Controller subscribes to an `OnPlayerOxygenExpired` signal from that service, when authored)
2. **Server-authoritative respawn timer**: 30 s (`RESPAWN_DELAY = 30 s`). Timer begins on the server at the moment `Humanoid.Died` fires. **Idempotent guard**: the death handler MUST refuse re-entry — `Humanoid.Died` can fire twice in pathological replication races, and the timer must not reset.
3. **Squad oxygen cost**: 1 shared-oxygen-pool unit (exact unit defined by Resource Management GDD). Cost is triggered by **two server-side paths** that converge on the same idempotent T5 death procedure:

   - **Path A — `Humanoid.Died`**: deducted at the moment `Humanoid.Died` fires, not at respawn arrival. Rationale: cost is the *act* of dying; deducting at death rather than at respawn prevents the disconnect-mid-respawn-timer exploit.
   - **Path B — `Players.PlayerRemoving` while imminent-death predicate satisfied**: server evaluates the predicate when an alive player disconnects. If satisfied, server invokes the same T5 procedure on behalf of the disconnecting player: 1 squad oxygen deducted, `OnPlayerDied(playerId, deathCause="disconnect-while-damaged")` broadcast, sprint state cleared. The player is removed immediately after T5 completes — **no respawn timer is started** (they are no longer present to receive it).

     **Read-and-cache discipline (load-bearing for predicate validity under cross-service teardown)**: PC's `Players.PlayerRemoving` handler MUST read `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player]` into a local variable **AS THE FIRST STATEMENT of the handler, before any yield-capable call** (no `task.wait`, no `RemoteFunction:InvokeServer`, no `Knit:GetService(...)` round-trip, no `Signal:Wait`). The cached local — not the live table read — is what the predicate evaluates. Knit-service `PlayerRemoving` connection ordering across services is **non-deterministic by Knit framework contract**; if PredatorService's own `PlayerRemoving` handler runs before PC's and clears the entry (per F.4 belt-and-braces clearance rule), a naive live-read predicate would observe `nil` and silently fail to fire on a legitimate predator-induced disconnect. The cache-first ordering closes this race independent of the cross-service teardown order.

     ```luau
     -- C.5.3 Path B handler skeleton (illustrative, not normative implementation):
     Players.PlayerRemoving:Connect(function(player)
         -- FIRST STATEMENT BLOCK — no yield-capable call may appear before the snapshot is complete.
         local cachedDamageTs = lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player]
         local cachedPredatorCausedImminent = predatorCausedImminent[player]  -- B5 arm-3 latch (PredatorService-owned)
         local cachedLatchSetTs = predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[player]  -- R10-1 arm-3 outer-ceiling stamp (cache-first, same race discipline)
         local humanoid = player.Character and player.Character:FindFirstChild("Humanoid")
         local cachedHealth = humanoid and humanoid.Health or nil
         local hrp = player.Character and player.Character:FindFirstChild("HumanoidRootPart")
         local cachedNow = getServerTime()  -- C.11 seam — NOT inline workspace:GetServerTimeNow() (R6-B1)
         -- deathContext snapshot (CONV-3): EVERY field captured before any yield, with an explicit
         -- nil-coalesce on the position read so a nil HumanoidRootPart never propagates into the
         -- world-anchored Death SFX (V/A.3). If the position resolves nil, V/A.3 suppresses the
         -- positional remote cue rather than playing it at the world origin.
         local deathContext = {
             deathEventId      = nextDeathEventId(),                         -- monotonic server-session counter
             userId            = player.UserId,
             playerName        = player.Name,                                -- snapshot (R6-B10 destroyed-instance log safety)
             lastKnownPosition = (hrp and hrp.Position) or lastServerTrackedPosition[player],  -- nil-coalesced; may be nil → V/A.3 suppresses positional cue
             deathTimestamp    = cachedNow,                                  -- round-16 B2: death-time stamp from the C.11 getServerTime() seam (the cachedNow first-statement read); carried in OnPlayerDied and ordering the V/A.4a caption queue (H.58)
         }
         -- Now safe to evaluate the predicate against cached locals
         -- and to yield within the T5 procedure (RequestSquadOxygenSpend, etc.)
         if isImminentDeath_cached(cachedHealth, cachedDamageTs, cachedPredatorCausedImminent, cachedLatchSetTs, cachedNow) then
             invokeT5(player, "disconnect-while-damaged", deathContext)
         end
     end)
     ```

     This is normative for the **ordering only**; the implementation is free to refactor the cache into a single struct, a Knit-service helper, or a per-player snapshot table. The invariant is: **no yield-capable Luau call may sit between handler entry and the cache reads.**

   **Imminent-death predicate** (server-evaluated at `PlayerRemoving` time, no client involvement):
   ```
   isImminentDeath(player) =
       (Humanoid.Health < HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD                              -- arm 1: recent graze + currently low HP
        AND lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player] ~= nil
        AND (getServerTime() - lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player]) < HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW)
       OR
       (lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player] ~= nil                           -- arm 2: lethal combo in flight from high HP
        AND (getServerTime() - lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player]) < DAMAGE_RECENT_WINDOW)
       OR
       (predatorCausedImminent[player] == true                               -- arm 3 (B5): predator-caused sub-threshold state
        AND Humanoid.Health < HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD
        AND predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[player] ~= nil                 -- arm 3 outer ceiling (R10-1)
        AND (getServerTime() - predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[player]) < LATCH_MAX_AGE)
   ```
   where `HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD = 25` (default; range 10–50), `DAMAGE_RECENT_WINDOW = 3.0 s` (default; range 1.5–5.0), `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW = 30.0 s` (default; range 15–60), and `LATCH_MAX_AGE = 120.0 s` (default; range 60–180 — the arm-3 outer staleness ceiling, R10-1). **`predatorCausedImminent[player]`** is a PredatorService-owned boolean latch (lifecycle in F.4, same ownership pattern as `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp`): set `true` when a predator damage application either crosses the player from `≥ HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` to `< HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` or lands while the player is already sub-threshold; cleared on HP-recovery to `≥ HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` or on T6 (respawn). It is `nil`/`false` for any player the predator has not driven sub-threshold this life — the arm short-circuits on `== true` before the health read.

   **Cached-read form is the normative form (R12-AI-3 — the formal predicate must not be Race-2-vulnerable as written)**: every term of the predicate above is evaluated against the **handler-entry snapshot** captured in the Path B skeleton's first-statement block — never against the live tables. The notation `predatorCausedImminent[player]`, `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player]`, `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[player]`, `Humanoid.Health`, and `getServerTime()` in the formal block above are shorthand for the cached locals `cachedPredatorCausedImminent`, `cachedDamageTs`, `cachedLatchSetTs`, `cachedHealth`, and `cachedNow` respectively; an implementation that conforms to the formal spec by re-reading the live tables inside the predicate body is **non-conforming** (it re-opens the Race-1 mid-yield teardown class the cache-first discipline closes). **Explicit parameter mapping (R12-build-I1)** — the skeleton's `isImminentDeath_cached(cachedHealth, cachedDamageTs, cachedPredatorCausedImminent, cachedLatchSetTs, cachedNow)` binds to the predicate's terms as: `cachedHealth` ↔ `Humanoid.Health` (arms 1 and 3); `cachedDamageTs` ↔ `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player]` (arms 1 and 2); `cachedPredatorCausedImminent` ↔ `predatorCausedImminent[player]` (arm 3); `cachedLatchSetTs` ↔ `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[player]` (arm 3 outer ceiling); `cachedNow` ↔ the single `getServerTime()` read all three elapsed-time subtractions use.

   **Arm-3 outer staleness ceiling — `LATCH_MAX_AGE` (R10-1, user-ruled 2026-06-09: outer-ceiling over pure-permanent)**: arm 3 is deliberately recency-*independent* of the `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW`/`DAMAGE_RECENT_WINDOW` arms (that is the whole point — it catches the fled-past-30 s Scenario-C evasion), but it is NOT *unbounded*. PredatorService maintains a paired **`predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[player]`** — the `getServerTime()` at the moment the latch was set, **renewed on each subsequent predator damage application that re-sets the latch while the player is sub-threshold** (so a still-hunted player's ceiling keeps refreshing). Arm 3 fires only while `(getServerTime() − predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[player]) < LATCH_MAX_AGE`. **Why a ceiling, not pure-permanent**: without it, a player the predator drove sub-threshold *once*, who then survives in a sub-threshold oxygen-starved state for many minutes with no further predator contact, would still be charged on an eventual disconnect — a weak-attribution over-charge tail (the predator's causal contribution has decayed but the latch never expires). The 120 s ceiling bounds that tail while sitting far above the 30 s `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW`, so it does **not** re-open Scenario C (the fled-30+s evasion path the latch closes). Default 120 s is first-pass design intent per G.8.1; the 60–180 s envelope is the band between "too short → Scenario C reopens" (must stay `> HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW` with margin) and "too long → the over-charge tail returns." **The margin is machine-checkable, not prose ([R13-F2], user-ruled 2026-06-10 — `LATCH_MAX_AGE` stays 120 s): `LATCH_MAX_AGE > HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW + 30 s`** — the G.6 coupled-knob invariant that closes the degenerate 60/60 corner where both knobs sit at their range extremes, the margin is zero, and Scenario C reopens (R12-SD-7/AI-8). `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp` is `nil`-guarded in the predicate (the `~= nil` conjunct short-circuits before the subtraction, identical to the arm-1/arm-2 timestamp discipline), is cleared with the latch on HP-recovery and on T6 (paired reset, F.4), and is read — never written — by PC. **Trust boundary (R12-network)**: `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp` is **server-internal state, written only by PredatorService** (the F.4 sole-setter rule); it is **never settable, directly or indirectly, via any RemoteEvent** — no client payload reaches it, so the arm-3 ceiling cannot be forged or refreshed from the client.

   **Boundary semantics (B11 — comparators are strict `<`, not `<=`)**: the HP comparison is `Humanoid.Health < HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD`, NOT `<=`. A player at exactly `Humanoid.Health = 25.0` does NOT satisfy arm 1 (the HP value must be strictly less than 25). The same strict-less-than semantics apply to both window comparators: `(now - lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player]) < HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW` and `(now - lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player]) < DAMAGE_RECENT_WINDOW`. A player whose predator-damage timestamp is exactly 30.0 s ago does NOT satisfy arm 1's recency check (the elapsed time must be strictly less than 30 s); a player whose predator-damage timestamp is exactly 3.0 s ago does NOT satisfy arm 2 (the elapsed time must be strictly less than 3 s). **The strict comparators are intentional** — they bias the predicate toward false-negatives at exact boundaries (favoring "no oxygen deducted" when the inputs are at the precise edge), consistent with E.F's precedent at line 515 where the ping cooldown uses `>=` for the inclusive direction (acceptance at exact boundary). The asymmetry across these two cases is principled: ping-cooldown UX favors *acceptance* at the boundary (off-by-one denials are bad UX); imminent-death cost attribution favors *rejection* at the boundary (off-by-one over-charges are worse than off-by-one under-charges for Pillar 2 cost legitimacy).

   **Practical implication for tuning**: when authoring an AC or playtest scenario at a boundary value (HP=25, 30s elapsed, 3s elapsed), use HP=24.99 or 25.01 / 29.99 s or 30.01 s / 2.99 s or 3.01 s to exercise the actual branch; testing at the exact boundary value is testing the edge case where neither side fires, which is a separate edge-case test class. G.6 and `entities.yaml` mirror this strict-less-than annotation.

   **Pillar 2 (cost attribution) — both arms are predator-scoped AND time-bounded**: each arm of the OR requires `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player] ~= nil` AND a recency check against the predator timestamp. A player whose HP fell below `HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` from non-predator damage (oxygen-depletion ticks, environmental hazard, fall damage) and who then disconnects pays NO squad oxygen. The predicate fires only when the predator demonstrably and recently contributed to the player's near-death state. This closes the Theme-1-round-2 ai-programmer Pillar 2 finding ("a player at HP=24 from oxygen-depletion who disconnects pays squad oxygen even though the predator was never near") AND the round-3 stale-graze defect (a 600 s-old predator brush followed by oxygen-depletion no longer triggers Path B). The OR is intentional given the predator-scope and time-scope: arm 1 catches "predator hit me within the last `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW = 30 s` AND I'm disconnecting at low HP" (the recent graze plus current low HP makes the predator the credible proximate cause of the imminent death); arm 2 catches "lethal predator combo in flight from high HP within `DAMAGE_RECENT_WINDOW = 3 s`" (predator damage recent enough that HP may not yet reflect it). The 30 s upper bound on arm 1 is the round-3 patch: too low (<15 s) misclassifies legitimate "lethal combo in flight" disconnects (player took heavy graze, ran 20 s, disconnected at low HP — predator IS proximate cause but window already expired); too high (>60 s) re-opens the stale-graze false-positive class for slow oxygen-depletion deaths after a long-ago predator brush.

   **Design-intent note (round-4 IMPORTANT, game-designer F1)**: the 30 s value of `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW` is **first-pass design intent**, not playtest-validated. The 15–60 s range in G.6 is the analytical envelope between the two failure modes above; the precise default within that envelope will be revisited after first-prototype playtest data per G.8.1 ("Default values shipped here are first-pass design intent, not playtest-validated"). Tuning this knob without playtest evidence is premature.

   **"Scenario C" — CLOSED by arm 3 (round-9 B5, user-ruled 2026-06-08; was a round-4-acknowledged false-negative, escalated to BLOCKING at round-8)**: the case is — a player takes a predator graze that drives them below `HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD`, panic-flees for 30+ seconds (escaping arm 1's `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW` recency window), enters slow oxygen-depletion while still sub-threshold, and disconnects. The predator was the *originating* cause of the sub-threshold state but is no longer the *recent* cause, so arms 1 and 2 both miss. Round-8 game-designer B5 escalated this to BLOCKING **specifically at the 2-player floor** — it is a systematic Pillar-2 cost-evasion path (wait out the 30 s window, then disconnect cost-free), most exploitable when the squad is smallest. **Arm 3 closes it**: the PredatorService `predatorCausedImminent` latch records that the predator *caused* the current sub-threshold state, independent of recency. While the latch holds AND the player is still sub-threshold, a disconnect pays squad oxygen. **The round-3 stale-graze false-positive class stays closed** — the latch is set ONLY on a predator damage application that crosses or strikes below the threshold, so a minor graze that never dropped the player sub-threshold never sets it, and a player who recovered above threshold (latch cleared) then slowly oxygen-depleted pays nothing. **Arm 3 sidesteps the damage-magnitude ADR the round-4 prose feared**: it keys on threshold-crossing causality, not on a damage-magnitude classifier. The latch's full set/clear lifecycle is specified in F.4 (PredatorService obligations) with the same rigor as `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp`; edge case E.E documents the now-closed scenario. **Residual false-negative (accepted):** a predator graze that drops the player to *exactly* `HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` (not strictly below) does not set the latch — consistent with the strict-`<` boundary bias toward under-charge at the exact edge (see boundary-semantics block above).

   **Init-order safety — nil-guard explicit**: `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player]` is `nil` for any player who has not yet received predator damage this life, including (a) players who joined before `PredatorService` fired any damage event (or before its bootstrap completed — see F.4 PredatorService init mandate), and (b) freshly-respawned players whose entry was cleared on T6. The predicate handles `nil` explicitly: both arms short-circuit on `~= nil` BEFORE any subtraction. **No `nil - number` arithmetic ever evaluates** — the AND in arm 2 left-evaluates the nil-check first, and Luau's short-circuit semantics guarantee the subtraction runs only when the timestamp is non-nil. F.4 separately mandates that `PredatorService` initializes the table to `{}` (not `nil`) before any player can join, so even table-level `nil` indexing is safe.

   **Idempotency — the `DeathCostReconciliation` state record (round-9 structural refactor)**: the three scattered death-cost booleans of prior rounds (`_deathCostPaid`, `deathDeductionCommitted`, and the reconciliation in-flight marker) are replaced by **a single per-death-event record** carrying one enumerated `state` field. This dissolves the recurring "flag SET specified, CLEAR-ON-FAILURE omitted" defect class (four reproductions across four rounds: R4-B6 `_deathCostPaid`-on-T6, R6-B5 `deathDeductionCommitted`, R8 CONV-1 in-flight marker): there is no longer a free boolean to add with a missing clear path — a new lifecycle concern is a new *state or transition* in the table below, which forces its set/clear contract to be written.

   **The record.** Every death event — Path A (`Humanoid.Died`) or Path B (`PlayerRemoving`-while-imminent) — mints a unique `deathEventId` from a monotonic server-session counter (`nextDeathEventId()`) and creates exactly one record:

   ```
   reconcileRows[deathEventId] = {
       deathEventId, userId, playerName, lastKnownPosition, deathTimestamp,  -- snapshots, all taken before any yield (deathTimestamp: round-16 B2 — see below)
       pathOrigin    = "A" | "B",                             -- which path created it (governs which T5 side-effects apply)
       state         = "Pending",                             -- the single lifecycle field
       inFlightSince = nil,                                   -- getServerTime() stamp set at Pending → InFlight (step 2); read by the staleness sweep (step 4). nil while Pending.
   }
   ```

   **`inFlightSince` (build-F2 — declared schema field for the staleness sweep)**: the belt-and-braces staleness sweep in step 4 re-arms any `InFlight` row "older than `2 × RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL`." That predicate reads a per-row dispatch timestamp, which MUST be a declared field rather than an implied one. `inFlightSince` is written **only** in the synchronous `Pending → InFlight` transition (step 2: `row.inFlightSince = getServerTime()` via the C.11 seam) and is cleared back to `nil` on **every** `InFlight → Pending` transition — the step-4 failure rollback, the step-4 error-guard rollback, and the staleness-sweep re-arm itself (AC H.56). The staleness sweep compares `getServerTime() − row.inFlightSince > 2 × RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL`; it never reads an undeclared field. (A sweep-re-armed row's original coroutine is discarded at step 5 — see the R12-net-B2 ownership re-check.)

   **`deathTimestamp` (round-16 B2 — closes the consumed-never-produced gap)**: the `OnPlayerDied` payload (C.9) and the V/A.4a caption-queue ordering both consume `deathTimestamp`, but no prior round specified its production. It is a **death-time snapshot, captured exactly once per death event into the `deathContext` snapshot (and thence the `reconcileRows` record), read from the C.11 injectable clock `getServerTime()` — never inline `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`**:
   - **Path A (`Humanoid.Died`)**: `deathTimestamp = getServerTime()` read at handler entry, in the same synchronous span as the rest of the deathContext snapshot, before any yield.
   - **Path B (`PlayerRemoving`)**: `deathTimestamp = cachedNow` — the first-statement-block clock read the skeleton above already captures (one clock read serves the predicate AND the stamp; no second read, so the stamp can never disagree with the predicate's `now`).
   - **Reconciliation-recovery broadcasts carry the ORIGINAL stamp**: a delayed `OnPlayerDied(deathCause="reconciliation-recovery")` carries the record's snapshotted `deathTimestamp` (the moment the player actually died), NOT the tick-commit time — this is what lets the V/A.4a queue order a late-arriving broadcast by when the death happened rather than when the network delivered it. The stamp is immutable after record creation (snapshots are never re-written). Verified by H.58, which also grounds H.52's ordering assertion (previously the ordering key existed in no producer).

   A `Player`-instance pointer `currentDeathRecord[player] = deathEventId` exists **only** to (i) converge the two racing paths onto the same record for a still-present player and (ii) let T6 find the live record. Path B sets it too (the `Player` instance is valid for the synchronous span of `PlayerRemoving`); it is cleared when the player is removed or at T6.

   **The four states and their transitions** — this is the complete lifecycle; no state is entered or left except as listed:

   | State | Meaning | Entered by | Exits to |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | **Pending** | Charge owed, not in flight. **The only reconcile-eligible state.** | record creation; rollback from InFlight on failure; **staleness-sweep re-arm of an aged InFlight row (R12-net-B2)** | → InFlight; → Abandoned (`BindToClose`) |
   | **InFlight** | A deduction attempt is dispatched and yielding. **The cross-path idempotency guard AND the no-double-dispatch guard** — replaces both `_deathCostPaid == true` and the in-flight marker. | a dispatcher (initial T5 or a reconciliation tick) sets it **synchronously, before** the yielding `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` | → Committed (success, **gated by the step-5 `state == "InFlight"` re-check**); → Pending (failure rollback); → Pending (**error-guard rollback — any error thrown while still `InFlight`, per the step-4 whole-span guard, R13-build-G2**); → Pending (staleness-sweep re-arm — the re-armed row's late-resolving original coroutine **discards** its result at step 5, R12-net-B2); → Abandoned (`BindToClose`) |
   | **Committed** | `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` AND `OnPlayerDied` both succeeded; squad oxygen charged. **Terminal-success.** | the step-5 success path (only if the row is **still `InFlight`** when the yield resolves — the sweep-re-arm late-success path is discarded, never Committed) | (row removed from `reconcileRows` — **NOT on the success path; retained until the player's T6 / `PlayerRemoving` per N-2, or removed immediately only at an orphaned tick-commit, R13-build-G2**) |
   | **Abandoned** | Closed without a charge. **Terminal-accepted-loss.** | `BindToClose` fires while the row is Pending or InFlight (logged) | (none) |

   **Reconcile-eligibility collapses to `state == "Pending"`** — no S4-membership clause, no second flag. This is what closes round-8 CONV-4 / systems Finding 4: a Pending record is keyed by `deathEventId` and is **independent of the player's S4 membership and instance lifecycle**, so it survives T6 (respawn) and reconciles afterward. **T6 detaches `currentDeathRecord[player]` and starts the next life's record; it never deletes an uncommitted prior-life record.**

   **R6-B5 is now structurally impossible**: there are no per-player sticky booleans to leak across lives. Life 2's death mints a fresh `deathEventId` with a fresh `Pending` record regardless of life 1's record state, so "the second death of a session pays zero" cannot occur (H.41 verifies this directly). **The legacy `_deathCostPaid[player]` / `deathDeductionCommitted[player]` per-player flags and their `Players.PlayerAdded` init (former R6-I11) and T6 dual-clearance (former R6-B5) are RETIRED — superseded by the record's `state` and the `currentDeathRecord` pointer.**

   **Dispatch discipline (atomicity rule — load-bearing for the entire idempotency claim)**: Luau is cooperatively scheduled — any function that may yield (`RemoteFunction:InvokeServer`, `Signal:Wait`, `task.wait`, `pcall(:GetAsync)`, a `Knit:GetService(...):Method()` that performs a remote call internally) creates a yield boundary across which other coroutines (the OTHER death path's handler, or the next reconciliation tick) can run. Every deduction — the initial T5 OR a reconciliation retry — is dispatched through exactly one mandatory sequence, expressed as transitions of the record's `state`:

   1. **Resolve the record (no yield in this step).** For a present player, read `currentDeathRecord[player]`; if it points to a record whose `state ∈ {InFlight, Committed}`, **return immediately** (no-op — the other path or a prior tick already owns this death event). If no record exists for this death event, mint `deathEventId = nextDeathEventId()`, create `reconcileRows[deathEventId]` in `state = "Pending"`, and set `currentDeathRecord[player] = deathEventId` (Path B sets the pointer too, synchronously, while the instance is valid). For a reconciliation tick, the record is the `Pending` row the scan selected. **`invokeT5` / `deathContext`-vs-record relationship (R12-build-I2 — no double-mint)**: `invokeT5(player, deathCause, deathContext)` *resolves-or-mints* the record via this step — the caller-supplied `deathContext` snapshot feeds record creation **only when a new record is minted here**; if an existing record short-circuits the resolve (the `state ∈ {InFlight, Committed}` return, or a `Pending` record already minted by the other path), the fresh `deathContext` is **discarded** — the record's original snapshots remain authoritative and no second record is ever created for the same death event.
   2. **Pending → InFlight, synchronously, BEFORE any yield.** Setting `state = "InFlight"` (and stamping `row.inFlightSince = getServerTime()` in the same synchronous step) is the single idempotency-and-no-double-dispatch guard: a concurrent path resolving the record at step 1 reads `InFlight` and returns; the next tick's scan skips a non-`Pending` row. **This one transition replaces both the old `_deathCostPaid = true` set and the separate in-flight marker** (round-8 CONV-1) — InFlight has exactly one entry (here), three mandatory exits (step 4 success, step 4 failure, and the whole-span error-guard in step 4 below), **plus one optional exit: the belt-and-braces staleness-sweep re-arm (InFlight → Pending), whose late-resolving original coroutine is discarded at step 5 — it must neither Commit nor re-roll-back (R12-net-B2)**. The "a stuck-in-flight row cannot exist" guarantee holds **only because of the whole-span error guard** — see step 4.

      **Pre-yield ordering of the commit-independent run-fact side-effects (round-18 [R16-2] / ai Finding 2 — the S4 no-re-acquire gate must be IMMEDIATE, not commit-gated and not cross-service-teardown-order-dependent).** Three of the T5 side-effects enumerated in step 5 are facts of the **death/disconnect itself**, NOT of the squad-oxygen charge: **(g) `PredatorService:ReleasePredatorLock(player)`** [both paths], **(h) `PredatorService:OnPlayerS4Entered(player)`** [Path A only], and **(i) `OnSquadMemberAliveChanged(player.UserId, isAlive=false)`** [both paths]. On the **initial** T5 dispatch these three MUST fire **synchronously in the same pre-yield span as the step-2 `Pending → InFlight` transition — BEFORE the step-3 `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` yield** — guarded once-only by that same transition (a second racing path returns at step 1 reading `InFlight`/`Committed` before it can reach step 2, so each fires exactly once per death event). This makes the predator no-re-acquire gate (h) and lock-release (g) immediate and independent of: (1) **whether the charge commits** (a dead/removed player is not a valid target regardless of the oxygen outcome — and on a failure-rollback the gate must already be armed); (2) **the cross-service `Players.PlayerRemoving` teardown ordering**, which is non-deterministic by Knit framework contract — this is the SAME ordering the round-17 panel rejected for Race-2, so the gate must NOT lean on "PredatorService's own teardown covers it"; and (3) for a **Path-B** player, the **pre-removal yield window** inside the dispatch — the lock is released and the alive-edge published synchronously before the player can be removed mid-yield (closing the round-17 ai Finding 1 unguarded window). The post-`Committed` step-5 block therefore runs ONLY the **commit-gated** side-effects — the `OnPlayerDied` broadcast and (a)/(b)/(c)–(f) — and never re-runs (g)/(h)/(i) on the initial dispatch. **Reconciliation-tick dispatches do NOT re-fire (h) or (i)** (the S4-entry and the `isAlive=false` alive-edge are one-time facts that already fired at the initial T5 — H.70) and re-fire only the idempotent (a)/(b)/(g) per the R6-B8 subset (re-releasing an already-released lock is a no-op; (g) firing pre-yield at the initial T5 means a reconciliation re-fire is redundant-but-harmless).
      **Dispatch-origin discriminator (round-20 SD-19-B2 — disentangles "mid-yield orphaned" from "reconciliation dispatch"; CD: fifth-flag bar PASSES, a seam fix not a re-architecture).** The post-yield commit (step 5) selects its side-effect SET from a **coroutine-local `dispatchOrigin ∈ {"initial", "reconciliation"}` captured at dispatch time (in this step-1/step-2 synchronous span, before the step-3 yield)** — NOT from whether a live `currentDeathRecord` pointer exists at commit. The two axes are independent: an **initial** Path-A/Path-B dispatch whose player departs *mid-yield* (between the step-3 yield and the step-5 resolve) arrives at commit with **no live pointer — exactly like a reconciliation orphan — yet it is still an *initial* dispatch**, and its commit MUST run the initial set (the `OnPlayerDied` broadcast + (a)/(b)/(c)–(f); (g)/(h)/(i) already fired pre-yield per the ordering rule above). Reading live-pointer-presence alone would mis-route this case to the reconciliation subset and wrongly drop (c)–(f). Therefore: **`dispatchOrigin` selects the side-effect SET** (`"initial"` → the full set minus the pre-yield trio; `"reconciliation"` → the R6-B8 player-removal-safe subset (a)/(b)/(g) only); **live-pointer-presence-at-commit selects only the RETAIN-vs-REMOVE decision** (orphan → remove the `Committed` row immediately, N-2 / R13-build-G2; live pointer → retain until T6 / `PlayerRemoving`). `dispatchOrigin` is a **dispatch-scoped coroutine-local, not a new record field and not a per-player sticky flag** (the record already carries the orthogonal `pathOrigin` A/B axis; this is the initial-vs-reconciliation axis the side-effect dispatcher needs) — it is immutable for the life of the dispatch, which is why the fifth-flag bar passes. Verified by H.75.
   3. **Invoke `RequestSquadOxygenSpend(amount=1, reason="death", deathEventId=row.deathEventId)` wrapped in `pcall`.** **Transport (round-16 — carried network N-2 rewrite): `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` is a SERVER-INTERNAL Knit service-to-service call (`PlayerControllerService` → ResourceManagement's service method on the same server), NOT a RemoteFunction, never registered on any `Service.Client` surface, never network-exposed, never client-invokable** — no client payload can reach the squad-oxygen ledger through it (the prior "RemoteFunction" wording at this site and H.22f actively steered implementers toward a network-exposed remote; that wording is retired). It remains a **yield-capable** call (RM's handler may yield internally on its own ledger work), so the step-2 set-before-yield and step-4 outcome discipline apply unchanged. This is the yield boundary; the record is already `InFlight` from step 2, so a concurrent path's resolve at step 1 returns. **The `deathEventId` is the cross-service idempotency key (R13-net-I1)**: every spend attempt for a death event — initial T5, reconciliation retry, or a sweep-raced duplicate — carries the SAME `deathEventId`, so RM can deduplicate at its own ledger (see the F.2 RM contract row).
   4. **Resolve the attempt — one of exactly two outcome transitions, both mandatory, AND a whole-span error guard:**
      - **Failure → InFlight → Pending (clear-on-failure rollback).** If `pcall` returned `(false, errMsg)` OR the resolved result indicates failure (per RM's `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` contract — e.g. `{success=false, reason}`), set `state = "Pending"`, clear `inFlightSince = nil`, surface the failure to a server log channel, and do **NOT** broadcast `OnPlayerDied`. The squad-oxygen ledger is unchanged; the row is now eligible for the next reconciliation tick (the rollback is a *handoff* to the tick, not the end of recovery).
      - **Success → InFlight → Committed (clear-on-success), conditioned on the row still being `InFlight` (R12-net-B2)** — the success transition is taken only if the post-yield `state == "InFlight"` re-check passes; a row the staleness sweep re-armed to `Pending` mid-yield is **discarded** (log-and-exit, no commit, no rollback). See the Success-path block below.
      - **Error guard, scoped to the InFlight window (MANDATORY — closes the stuck-InFlight hazard; SD-B4 ordering correction).** The dispatch body MUST execute under an outer `pcall`/`xpcall` (or equivalent), and the rollback is **conditioned on the record still being `InFlight`**: **any error thrown while `state == "InFlight"` (i.e., after the `Pending → InFlight` transition and before the `InFlight → Committed` transition) transitions the record `InFlight → Pending`** (clearing `inFlightSince = nil`, logging via the snapshot, never the live instance), not just a `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` failure *result*. Without this, an error between step 2 and the outcome classification (e.g. the success/failure classifier itself throwing, or any non-pcall'd line in the `task.spawn`'d retry coroutine) would strand the row in `InFlight` forever — the tick scan only selects `state == "Pending"` and would never re-select it.

        **Why the rollback is scoped to InFlight and NOT the whole span (SD-B4).** The prior wording said "ANY error after `Pending → InFlight` rolls back to `Pending`," but the success path commits the charge (sets `Committed`) before running the broadcast + side-effect block. Once `state == "Committed"` the squad oxygen has provably been charged; rolling that back to `Pending` on a *post-commit* side-effect error (e.g. the `OnPlayerDied` broadcast or a pulse-timer stop throwing) would make the row reconcile-eligible again and **re-deduct a second oxygen unit on the next tick** — exactly the double-charge the record exists to prevent. Therefore: **an error thrown in the post-`Committed` broadcast/side-effect block is caught and logged but does NOT roll back** — the record stays `Committed`, owes no further charge, and the (idempotent) side-effects (a)/(b)/(g) may simply be retried out-of-band if needed. **This guard is what makes "a stuck-in-flight row cannot exist" true**: `InFlight`'s only outcomes are `Committed` (success), `Pending` (failure *or* any error thrown while still `InFlight`), or `Abandoned` (`BindToClose`); and a `Committed` row is never silently re-deducted. As a belt-and-braces backstop, the tick MAY additionally re-arm any `InFlight` row whose declared `inFlightSince` stamp is older than `2 × RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL` (the staleness sweep: `getServerTime() − row.inFlightSince > 2 × RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL` → set `state = "Pending"`, clear `inFlightSince = nil`) — recommended but secondary to the error guard. **Sweep-vs-slow-coroutine race (R12-net-B2 / R12-SD-4 — why the step-5 re-check is mandatory if the sweep is implemented)**: a slow-but-legitimate in-flight coroutine can outlive the sweep window; the sweep re-arms its row → `Pending`, the next tick dispatches a SECOND coroutine, and without the step-5 `state == "InFlight"` re-check **both** `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` calls would commit — a double squad-oxygen charge. With the re-check, the late coroutine discards its result (log-and-exit). **Accepted residual**: the sweep can therefore cause a benign duplicate `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` *attempt* whose result PC discards — the discard means PC never transitions to `Committed` twice and never broadcasts `OnPlayerDied` twice. **The duplicate attempt is harmless at RM's ledger only because both attempts carry the same `deathEventId` idempotency key and the F.2 RM contract row obliges RM to deduplicate per `(userId, deathEventId)` — without that obligation, two concurrently-successful spends would debit RM's ledger twice even though PC commits once (R13-net-I1; the prior "RM's spend is the single charge point" wording was an ungrounded cross-GDD assumption).** To keep this residual window rare, **the sweep threshold MUST stay `≥ 2 × RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL`** (never tightened below it). AC H.54 verifies the discard path.

      **Log-name safety (R6-B10 — destroyed-instance crash)**: the failure log MUST NOT dereference `player.Name` directly. On a Path-B reconciliation retry the `Player` instance has been removed and may be destroyed by tick time; `player.Name` then raises a *nil-parent runtime error* that **crashes the reconciliation handler and kills every subsequent retry for the entire server session**. The record carries `playerName` snapshotted at creation, and the log reads that snapshot, never the live instance: `warn("[PlayerController] RequestSquadOxygenSpend failed for " .. row.playerName .. " (userId=" .. tostring(row.userId) .. "): " .. tostring(errMsg))`. The same snapshot rule applies to any other log/telemetry line in the reconciliation path.

      **Why the rollback alone is not sufficient (R5-B2 round-5 closure)**: in the concurrent-path race, by the time the `pcall` resolves both `Humanoid.Died` and `Players.PlayerRemoving` may have already returned. Whichever path fires first (Path X) transitions the record to `InFlight` at step 2 and yields at step 3; while Path X is yielded, the OTHER path (Path Y) resolves the record at step 1, reads `InFlight`, and returns synchronously. Path X's `pcall` then fails at step 4 and rolls back to `Pending` — but no in-line retry is reachable because Path Y has already exited and Path X is mid-rollback with no remaining synchronous work. **The reconciliation tick is the authoritative retry surface** (R5-B2 option (a) — CD-recommended, user-confirmed 2026-05-04): `PlayerControllerService` owns a server-side **death-cost reconciliation tick** that runs every `RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL = 5.0 s` on `RunService.Heartbeat`. The tick scans `reconcileRows` for the eligibility predicate and re-dispatches each match through the same step 1–5 sequence.

      **Reconcile-eligibility (single clause)**:

      ```
      reconcile_eligible(row) = (row.state == "Pending")
      ```

      No S4-membership clause, no second flag, no `userId` key (the ambiguous identifier across rejoins is never used). Because eligibility is decoupled from the player's S4 membership and `Player`-instance lifecycle, a `Pending` row reconciles whether the player is still in S4, has already respawned (T6), or has left the server entirely. This is the round-8 CONV-4 / systems-Finding-4 closure: the cost is **never** lost merely because the player respawned before a failed tick-interval recovered.

      **Rejoin-during-reconciliation race (closed by `deathEventId` keying)**: if a player rejoins under the same `userId` while a prior death's `reconcileRows[deathEventId]` row is still `Pending`, the rejoin is a fresh `Player` instance with a fresh `currentDeathRecord[newPlayer] = nil` (no active record). The pending `deathEventId` row is keyed independently — not collided with, not overwritten, not satisfied by the rejoin — and reconciles on its own `deathEventId`. Exactly one squad-oxygen unit is charged per death event; the new life's eventual death is a separate `deathEventId`.

      **Per-retry execution off the Heartbeat handler (R6-I5; transport corrected round-16)**: a retry invokes `RequestSquadOxygenSpend`, a yield-capable server-internal RM service call (NOT a RemoteFunction — see step 3; RM's handler may yield internally, budget 50–150 ms worst case). With up to 4 eligible rows in a 4-player squad, inline retries would block the Heartbeat callback 200–600 ms — falsifying H.28's per-frame budget. **The Heartbeat body only scans, performs the synchronous Pending → InFlight transition (step 2) for each selected row, then `task.spawn`s the yielding step 3–5 remainder off the handler.** Because the `Pending → InFlight` transition is synchronous and completes inside the Heartbeat span before the spawn yields, a row selected this tick is `InFlight` by the time the next tick scans — it cannot be double-dispatched. (This is precisely why InFlight is a first-class state rather than a side marker.)

      **Reconciliation-success T5 side-effects (R6-B8 — phantom-emission closure for the rollback-then-reconcile path)**: when the *initial* T5 `pcall` failed before the step-5 side-effect block ran, the dying player's sprint/light pulse-interval timers were never stopped. A successful reconciliation MUST therefore re-invoke the **player-removal-safe subset** of the T5 side-effects — **(a) sprint pulse-timer stop / force-T2 if S2 was active**, **(b) lantern force-T4 / Light pulse-timer stop if S3b was active**, and **(g) `PredatorService:ReleasePredatorLock(player)`** — and MUST **explicitly suppress (c)–(f)** (stamina-freeze, grace-slate reset, spectator-camera, client-input-lock — already settled or inapplicable to a removed player). Without (a)/(b), a Path-B player who disconnected in S2/S3b before their initial deduction succeeded would keep emitting Sprint/Light pulses for up to `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL = 4 s` on a `HumanoidRootPart` being destroyed — the R5-B1 Pillar-1 phantom-emission class. (a), (b), and (g) are idempotent (re-stopping a stopped timer / re-releasing a released lock is a no-op), so invoking them on both the initial success path and the reconciliation path is safe.

      **Bound on retry latency (round-9 CONV-4 correction)**: if RM is available, a rolled-back row is recovered within at most `2 × RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL = 10.0 s` of the original death event (worst case: a tick fired immediately before the failure; the next tick re-dispatches and succeeds). **If RM remains unavailable, the row stays `Pending` and re-dispatches every tick interval until RM recovers OR `BindToClose` transitions it to `Abandoned`** — i.e. the 10 s figure is the nominal single-retry bound, not a guarantee under sustained RM outage. Correctness no longer depends on the recovery completing before `RESPAWN_DELAY`: the `Pending` row survives T6 (it is `deathEventId`-keyed, not S4-keyed), so reconciliation-before-respawn is now only a **UX** nicety (the surviving squad gets the delayed notification sooner), governed by the *advisory* coupled-knob note in G.6 — not a correctness invariant.

      **Reconciliation tick lifecycle**:

      - **Bootstrap**: `PlayerControllerService` starts the tick connection inside `Knit:Start()`, after all dependency tables are initialized. Before the first tick can fire, bootstrap-initialize: `reconcileRows = {}`, `currentDeathRecord = {}`, `lastServerTrackedPosition = {}`, the monotonic death-event counter `_nextDeathEventId = 0`, **the run-lifecycle latch `_beaconActivated = false` and its companion set-time scalar `_beaconActivatedAt = nil` (round-20 [R16-2] bootstrap-fanout closure, build-B2 / SD-19-I4 / NET-I6 — the C.6 latch table asserts "bootstrap with the other PC tables, C.5.3"; this is that enumeration site, previously a half-finished round-18 fanout that named the obligation in C.6 but never listed it here)**, **AND the tick-interval scalar `lastTickTime = getServerTime()`** (the server-start time captured through the C.11 seam). `_beaconActivated` is a plain server-internal scalar, not a per-player table, so it has no per-player init or teardown; it is set once at PC's `OnEscapeBeaconActivated` subscription (recording `_beaconActivatedAt = getServerTime()` at the same instant) and cleared by the inbound `RunEnded` handler OR the `BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` self-heal (below). H.61b asserts both the `false` start AND a fresh run begins with the latch `false` after a prior run cleared it (N+1-run start — the latch never ratchets across run boundaries). **`nextDeathEventId()` is defined as**: `_nextDeathEventId += 1; return _nextDeathEventId` — a server-session-monotonic integer, never reused within a session, never derived from any client value (server-authoritative identity). The first tick fires no earlier than `RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL` after server start.

        **`lastTickTime` is mandatory, not optional (R11 Tier-0 show-stopper — build-F1 / SD-B1 / N-4)**: the per-tick interval gate is `(getServerTime() − lastTickTime) > RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL` (C.11, G.6, H.40). If `lastTickTime` is left uninitialized, the **first** Heartbeat evaluates `now − nil`, which raises a runtime error inside the Heartbeat callback and **kills the reconciliation tick for the entire server session** — silently defeating every other reconciliation guarantee in this section (a rolled-back `Pending` row would never be re-dispatched). Seeding it to `getServerTime()` at bootstrap makes the first scan fire exactly one interval after server start (H.40), with no `nil` arithmetic on the first frame. **The Heartbeat body MUST set `lastTickTime = getServerTime()` at the moment a scan fires** (i.e., when the interval gate passes), before performing the scan, so the next interval is measured from this scan rather than drifting. **Second-scan drift note (round-16 qa)**: because the gate is strict `>` and `lastTickTime` is re-anchored to the gate-pass time, each scan fires up to one Heartbeat frame (~16.7 ms at 60 Hz) *after* the exact interval boundary, and subsequent intervals measure from the actual fire time — so scan N fires at approximately `N × (RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL + ε_frame)`, a slow bounded-per-interval drift relative to wall-clock multiples of the knob. This is **accepted**: the tick is a retry surface, not a metronome — nothing consumes tick-fire times as a schedule, and the staleness-sweep threshold (`2 × RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL`) has ≥ one full interval of headroom over any single-frame overshoot. H.40 asserts the re-anchor write itself, not wall-clock multiples.
      - **`lastServerTrackedPosition` upkeep**: `PlayerControllerService` updates `lastServerTrackedPosition[player] = HumanoidRootPart.Position` each server Heartbeat from the server-authoritative character position it already tracks (PC "is the position" per F.2/C.10 — this is a cache of that existing value, not a new perception path). It is the nil-coalesce fallback for `deathContext.lastKnownPosition` when the `HumanoidRootPart` is already gone at Path-B snapshot time (C.5.3 skeleton). If BOTH the live HRP and this cache are nil (player never fully spawned), `lastKnownPosition` resolves `nil` and V/A.3 suppresses the positional Death SFX. **Bootstrap + nil-guard (R12-build-I3)**: the table is bootstrap-initialized to `{}` (see the Bootstrap bullet above), so the table itself never `nil`-indexes; the per-player *entry* MAY still be `nil` (never-fully-spawned player, or cleared at `PlayerRemoving`), and **every read of `lastServerTrackedPosition[player]` — including reads while the player is in S4, where the HRP is gone and the cache holds the death-point value until the T6 anchor-reset — MUST nil-guard the entry** (the C.5.3 snapshot's `or`-coalesce is the canonical pattern); a `nil` entry propagates to a `nil` `lastKnownPosition`, never to an error.
      - **Per-player init**: on `Players.PlayerAdded`, set `currentDeathRecord[player] = nil` (no active death record for a fresh player). **No per-player death-cost booleans are initialized — they no longer exist** (the former R6-I11 `_deathCostPaid`/`deathDeductionCommitted` init is retired). A never-died player simply has no `reconcileRows` entry and a `nil` pointer. **Mid-session join (round-16 qa — initialization completeness rule)**: a player who joins DURING an active run receives exactly the same per-player init as a lobby join — `currentDeathRecord[player] = nil`; grace anchors `t_lastGracePulse_sprint`/`t_lastGracePulse_light` reset to `nil` (the E.E B6 `PlayerAdded` reset); NO `_lastHeartbeatTime` write (lazy T1 seed per E.D); `lastServerTrackedPosition[player]` populated by the next Heartbeat upkeep pass (and nil-guarded at every read until then). Every per-player table is bootstrap-initialized to `{}` and every per-player *entry* read path nil-guards, so a mid-run joiner can sprint, die, ping, or disconnect on their first frame with **no `nil`-arithmetic on any code path** (H.69). PC does not gate joining itself — whether mid-run join is permitted is a run-lifecycle/matchmaking rule owned outside PC; PC's obligation is that its tables are join-time-independent.
      - **Per-player teardown (`Players.PlayerRemoving`)**: after the Path-B dispatch returns (success, rollback, or no-fire), explicitly set `currentDeathRecord[player] = nil` and `lastServerTrackedPosition[player] = nil` to release the destroyed `Player`-instance keys. **Additionally, if the record this pointer referenced is `Committed`, remove it from `reconcileRows` now** — the player is departing, so the replay window that the retained `Committed` row guarded (N-2) is closed (a removed instance cannot replay `Humanoid.Died`), and the charge is already final. **A `Pending` or `InFlight` row is NOT removed here** — it persists by `deathEventId` and continues to reconcile independently of the player's departure (the round-8 CONV-4 closure). **InFlight-at-`PlayerRemoving` narration (R12-network)**: an `InFlight` row whose coroutine is still mid-yield when the player departs simply keeps running — the coroutine operates exclusively on the record's snapshots (`playerName`/`userId`/`lastKnownPosition`), so the `Player` instance's destruction is safe; on failure it rolls back to `Pending` and reconciles by `deathEventId` like any other row. **This is the named implementation site for the "cleared when the player is removed" rule (C.5.3) — only the convergence pointer, the position cache, and any *Committed* record are cleared here; an uncommitted record outlives the instance by `deathEventId`.**
      - **Per-tick scan**: O(pending `reconcileRows` entries this session) — a single linear pass; <0.01 ms per tick at H.28's 4-player budget (the yielding server-internal `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` retries are `task.spawn`'d off the handler per R6-I5 and not counted in the scan cost).
      - **Termination**: a row leaves the working-set in one of two ways — a **Committed** row is removed at the player's next **T6 (respawn)** or **`Players.PlayerRemoving` (departure)** (retained until then as the replayed-`Died` short-circuit, N-2; NOT removed on the success path), and an **Abandoned** row is removed at `BindToClose`. A `Pending` or `InFlight` row is retained for the next tick; so is a `Committed` row (harmlessly skipped — the scan selects only `state == "Pending"`) until its T6/`PlayerRemoving` removal. No row is dispatched more than once per tick interval (the synchronous `Pending → InFlight` transition guards this; see above).
      - **Persistence boundary → Abandoned (R6-B11 F3h invariant)**: reconciliation state is in-memory per server session — it does NOT cross `BindToClose` or server-instance boundaries. When `BindToClose` fires, every `Pending`/`InFlight` row transitions **`→ Abandoned`** (logged, with the snapshotted `playerName`/`userId`) and the squad-oxygen decrement for that death event is not applied this session. This is an accepted bound, not a silent drop: making the loss an explicit, logged terminal transition (rather than a row that just vanishes) is exactly the discipline the state record buys. Resource Management owns the durable ledger via ProfileStore; `BindToClose` is a shutdown path where the run is already ending. AC H.42 verifies the bound.

      **HUD forward obligation**: the gap between the original death event and the reconciliation broadcast (up to 10 s nominal, longer under sustained RM outage) is a player-experience gap — a surviving squad member observes a teammate vanish without an `OnPlayerDied`, then receives a delayed `OnPlayerDied(deathCause="reconciliation-recovery")`. HUD GDD owes the player-experience prose for this delayed-notification class (see F.4 row).

   5. **Success path (InFlight → Committed) — gated by a post-yield ownership re-check (R12-net-B2 / R12-SD-4)**: if `pcall` returned `(true, ...)` AND the resolved result indicates success, the coroutine MUST first **re-check `if row.state == "InFlight"`** before any commit-side narration. The yield in step 3 is exactly where the optional staleness sweep can have re-armed the row to `Pending` (and the next tick re-dispatched it); the post-yield state read decides ownership:
      - **Still `InFlight`** → this coroutine owns the row: **set `reconcileRows[deathEventId].state = "Committed"`** — this is the commit point; the charge is now final. Then, **with the record already `Committed` (so a concurrent path or tick resolving it reads `Committed` and no-ops)**, broadcast `OnPlayerDied(...)` and **invoke the commit-gated T5 transition side-effects per the C.6 T5 row** (below) — the `OnPlayerDied` broadcast and (a)/(b)/(c)–(f), normative for both Path A and Path B. **The commit-INDEPENDENT run-fact side-effects (g)/(h)/(i) already fired pre-yield at the step-2 transition (round-18 [R16-2] — see the pre-yield ordering rule above) and are NOT re-run here on the initial dispatch.** **Dispatcher scope (R13-build-G2 — which side-effect set applies)**: the full (a)–(g) set applies only to the **initial** T5 dispatch (Path A or Path B, player still present). A **reconciliation-tick** dispatch — whether the pointer is still live (player in S4 awaiting respawn) or orphaned (player departed/respawned) — invokes only the R6-B8 player-removal-safe subset **(a)/(b)/(g)** and suppresses (c)–(f), per the R6-B8 block below; for an S4-present player (c)–(f) were already settled by the T5 state transition itself, so the R6-B8 rule wins over this sentence's "full" wording in every tick-dispatch case.
      - **No longer `InFlight`** (the sweep re-armed it to `Pending`, or the re-dispatched coroutine has since driven it onward) → **this late coroutine MUST NOT commit and MUST NOT re-roll-back** — it logs (via the row snapshots) and exits; **the row's current owner (the re-dispatched coroutine) is authoritative**. No `Committed` transition, no `OnPlayerDied` broadcast, no side-effects from the late coroutine (AC H.54). The late coroutine's successful `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` result is the accepted residual documented at the step-4 staleness-sweep block.

      **Retain the Committed row until T6 / `PlayerRemoving` (N-2 — closes the replayed-`Died` double-deduct).** The row and its `currentDeathRecord[player]` pointer are **NOT removed on the success path** — they are retained until the player's next **T6 (respawn)** or **`Players.PlayerRemoving` (departure)**, whichever comes first. **Why:** if the row were removed and the pointer cleared here, a replayed `Humanoid.Died` (C.5.2 admits it can fire twice) would resolve step 1 against a `nil` pointer, mint a *fresh* `deathEventId` with a fresh `Pending` record, and deduct a **second** squad-oxygen unit for the same death. Retaining the `Committed` row keeps the step-1 short-circuit (`state ∈ {InFlight, Committed} → return`) armed across the entire window in which a replay can occur, so the second `Died` resolves the retained `Committed` row and no-ops. A retained `Committed` row is harmless to the tick (the scan selects only `state == "Pending"`) and is bounded — it is removed at T6 / `PlayerRemoving` (see those transitions and the Termination bullet). The post-commit side-effects are: **(reorder note, SD-B4)** the broadcast + (a)–(g) run *after* the `Committed` transition, never before, so the error guard's rollback window has already closed by the time any side-effect can throw.

      **Orphaned-row exception (reconciliation-tick commit after the player departed).** When a **reconciliation tick** commits a row for which **no live `currentDeathRecord` pointer exists** — the player already departed (a Path-B row whose `PlayerRemoving` teardown ran while the row was still `Pending`) or already respawned (pointer detached at T6) — **remove the `Committed` row immediately on that commit**. There is no present `Player` instance that can replay `Humanoid.Died` against it, and no future T6/`PlayerRemoving` will arrive to clean it, so the retain-until-T6/`PlayerRemoving` rule has nothing to wait for. (This is why the Path-B reconciliation ACs still observe the row removed in the committing tick.) Equivalently: a `Committed` row is held exactly as long as some live `currentDeathRecord` points at it, and removed the moment nothing does. **Side-effect scope at an orphaned-row commit (R13-net-I2)**: because the player is already removed, only the **player-removal-safe subset (a), (b), (g)** of the T5 side-effects is invoked (per the R6-B8 rule below) and **no live `Player` instance is accessed anywhere in this path** — all logging/broadcast payload fields read the record's snapshots (`playerName`/`userId`/`lastKnownPosition`), never `player.*`. The `pathOrigin` field plus the absence of a live pointer is what selects this subset; an implementation MUST NOT reach the full (a)–(g) block from an orphaned commit.

      - (a) the C.5.5 **sprint hard-reset** (force T2 if S2 active — stop the sprint pulse interval timer; clear sprint authorization);
      - (b) the **lantern force-T4 if S3b active** — stop the Light pulse interval timer (R5-B1 round-5 closure);
      - (c) freeze stamina at current value;
      - (d) reset `t_lastGracePulse_sprint = nil` and `t_lastGracePulse_light = nil` (Pillar 4 fairness — fresh grace slate post-respawn; **Path A only**);
      - (e) activate spectator camera (**Path A only** — Path B player is removed);
      - (f) lock client-side movement / lantern / sprint / gather inputs (**Path A only**);
