### F.3 — Reverse-Cite to ED.F.2a (Predator AI Deferred Contracts)

Per `ED.F.2a`, the Ecological Disturbance GDD lists obligations the Predator AI GDD must reverse-cite when authored. Player Controller has its own row in that table — Q9 movement-delta detection, Q11 Calm-tier first-zone teaching beat, F.3a author-checklist obligations, `EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR` interval-sampling logic, Tier-2 #17 mobile-touch analog-drift threshold (≥ 2 studs), Tier-2 #16 `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW` state-entry timer.

**All of these are resolved in this GDD:**

| ED.F.2a obligation | Resolution in this GDD |
|---|---|
| Q9 movement-delta detection logic | C.7 (mobile drift floor); C.8.1 + C.8.2 (stationary attenuation in emission contract); D.4 (formula); D.6 (sampling) |
| Q11 Calm-tier first-zone teaching beat | F.4 row (level-design dependency, not coded in PC) — see below |
| F.3a author-checklist obligations | This section — F |
| `EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR` interval-sampling logic | D.6; C.7 |
| Tier-2 #17 mobile-touch analog-drift threshold | C.7 (`MOBILE_ANALOG_DRIFT_THRESHOLD = 2 studs`); D.4 platform gate |
| Tier-2 #16 `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW` state-entry timer | C.8.1 + C.8.2; D.4 grace branch |

### F.4 — Open Cross-System Obligations Player Controller Owes Forward

| Receiving System | Obligation Player Controller owes | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Level Design** | Q11 Calm-tier first-zone authoring beat. The first zone of a fresh run MUST be Calm-tier (Disturbance < `TENSE_THRESHOLD = 0.30`). PC does not implement this — PC's role is to ensure the first sprint/light emission in the first zone is *teachable*, i.e., the player can connect "I sprinted" to "the disturbance level rose." | Level-design responsibility; called out here so the obligation is bidirectionally visible. |
| **Predator AI** | Server-tracked player positions are the canonical perception input. Player Controller MUST NOT introduce client-predicted positions into any cross-system perception path. **Explicit prohibition (R12-ai)**: PredatorService tracking MUST NEVER read PC's `lastServerTrackedPosition` cache — that cache is a **death-context fallback only** (the C.5.3 `deathContext.lastKnownPosition` nil-coalesce); live tracking reads `HumanoidRootPart.Position` directly. Reading the cache for tracking would let the predator chase a stale (up to one-Heartbeat-old, or post-death 30 s-stale) phantom position and would silently couple predator perception to PC's reconciliation bookkeeping. | Server-authoritative position is the only valid input for Predator AI; the two position surfaces (`HumanoidRootPart.Position` live vs `lastServerTrackedPosition` death-fallback) are now explicitly disjoint. |
| **Predator AI** | `PredatorService` MUST maintain a server-internal `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp` table — **keyed by `Player` instance object, not `userId`** (a rejoining player under the same userId is a fresh `Player` instance and therefore a fresh key — defends against stale-entry false-positives across a Wi-Fi flap reconnect). *(Keying-scope note, R6-B9 / round-9: this `Player`-instance keying mandate governs `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp`, the `predatorCausedImminent` latch (see the dedicated row below), and PC's `currentDeathRecord[player]` convergence pointer — all for present players ONLY. The `DeathCostReconciliation` records themselves (C.5.3) are keyed by a per-death-event `deathEventId`, NOT by `Player` instance and NOT by `userId`; they survive player removal AND respawn. The two keying domains are disjoint and intentional; see C.5.3 "The `DeathCostReconciliation` state record.")* Readable by Player Controller's `PlayerRemoving` handler for the C.5.3 Path B imminent-death predicate. **PC reads but does not write this table.** Lifecycle is load-bearing for C.5.3 Path B correctness:<br>• **Bootstrap init**: `PredatorService` MUST initialize the table to `{}` (empty, NOT `nil`) before any `Players.PlayerAdded` can fire — i.e., during PredatorService construction, before `Knit:Start()` returns. The C.5.3 predicate's nil-guard depends on the table itself existing; `nil` table indexing would crash the predicate.<br>• **On `Players.PlayerAdded`**: do NOT write — leave the entry as `nil` for new joiners. The C.5.3 predicate treats a `nil` entry as "no recent predator damage" via the short-circuit AND (`lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player] ~= nil`).<br>• **On predator-damage application**: write `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player] = getServerTime()` (via the C.11 clock-injection seam, NOT direct `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`). This is the ONLY writer of the table (Pillar 2 — non-predator damage MUST NOT write here). **Qualifying condition (round-16 ai)**: the application must deal **`damage > 0`** — a 0-damage application (blocked hit, immunity frame, zero-magnitude contact) MUST NOT write or renew the timestamp (it would extend the arm-1/arm-2 recency windows off a no-op contact).<br>• **On T6 (respawn)**: clear the entry — `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player] = nil`. Each new life starts with no predator damage history. *(Trigger channel, round-16 AI-I1: PredatorService executes this clearance in its `OnPlayerT6Respawned(player)` handler ([R16-2]) — the C.6 T6 row is the NORMATIVE single trigger-site enumeration; this bullet documents the obligation, it is not a second trigger.)* Without this, a player respawning after a death where the predator hit them within `DAMAGE_RECENT_WINDOW` would still satisfy the predicate's time arm, double-charging on a subsequent disconnect-while-low-HP from non-predator causes.<br>• **On `Players.PlayerRemoving`**: clear the entry — `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player] = nil`. Belt-and-braces against any indirect retention path; the `Player` keying already prevents userId-level rejoin staleness, but explicit clearance is mandatory.<br>• **Cross-service teardown ordering contract (load-bearing for C.5.3 Path B; race-scope corrected per R6-I2)**: Knit's `PlayerRemoving` connection ordering across services is non-deterministic. PredatorService's own `PlayerRemoving` handler may run before, concurrently with, or after PC's `PlayerRemoving` handler. There are **two distinct races, and PC's cache-first read closes only one of them** — the prior round's prose over-claimed:<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;— **Race 1 (mid-handler yield) — CLOSED by PC's cache-first read.** If PredatorService clears the entry *while PC's handler is mid-yield* (after PC entered but during a `RemoteFunction`/`task.wait` suspension), PC's first-statement read-and-cache of `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player]` has already captured the live value into a local before any yield, so the predicate evaluates the cached value, not the cleared table. This is the race the C.5.3 read-and-cache discipline guarantees against.<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;— **Race 2 (PredatorService clears BEFORE PC's handler entry) — NOT closed by cache-first.** If PredatorService's `PlayerRemoving` connection runs to completion *before* PC's connection is even invoked, the entry is already `nil` when PC's first statement reads it. PC's cache-first read faithfully caches `nil`; the predicate produces a **false-negative** (a legitimately predator-induced disconnect is classified as non-predator and the squad is **not** charged). This is the *Pillar-2-safe* direction (under-charge, never over-charge), which is why round-6 held this IMPORTANT, not BLOCKING — but it is still a correctness gap against design intent.<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;**Resolution (round-9 CONV-2 — downgraded from a binding claim to an intent + a robust guard):** Race 2's residual is the *Pillar-2-safe* direction (under-charge, never over-charge), so shipping without a hard close is acceptable. The prior round shipped a **binding** correctness claim resting on `task.defer(function() lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player] = nil end)` resuming *after* all same-frame `PlayerRemoving` connections — but that ordering is an **unverified post-cutoff Roblox scheduler guarantee** on a live auto-updating platform, and a GDD must not ship a binding correctness claim on an unverified API. **It is therefore stated as a design *intent to verify in Studio* (`task.defer`-vs-Knit-`PlayerRemoving` resumption ordering), NOT a guarantee.** The mechanism PC actually *relies* on for correctness regardless of scheduler order is the **no-op-if-replaced exact-`Player`-instance-key guard** (closes Race 3): any deferred clear MUST target the exact `Player` instance it captured and MUST no-op if that key has already been removed or replaced by a rejoin — `task.defer(function() local p = capturedPlayer; if lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[p] ~= nil then lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[p] = nil end end)` keyed on the captured instance, never on `userId`. Because the timestamp table, the latch, and the death records are `Player`-instance-/`deathEventId`-keyed (never `userId`), a Wi-Fi-flap rejoin cannot be corrupted by a stale deferred clear whatever the resumption order. **Net:** PC's cache-first read closes Race 1 (robust); the instance-key no-op guard closes Race 3 (robust); Race 2 is an accepted under-charge whose deferred-clear mitigation is a Studio-verify intent, not a binding contract. **Architecture must add the `task.defer` ordering to the Theme-2 post-cutoff API-verification list. Round-16 (ai): this is a named ARCHITECTURE-PHASE PREREQUISITE — `/create-architecture` must resolve the cross-service `PlayerRemoving` teardown-ordering strategy (or formally accept the documented Race-2 under-charge) before any PC/PredatorService story is implemented.**<br>• **Player instance key validity on `PlayerRemoving`**: the `player` argument passed to the `PlayerRemoving` callback is still a valid `Player` instance for the duration of the handler — Roblox guarantees the instance is not destroyed until all `PlayerRemoving` connections have run (one source of the cross-service connection-ordering non-determinism). PC's read of `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[player]` and `player.Character.Humanoid.Health` are both safe within the handler's synchronous span; the `Character` model and `Humanoid` MAY be `nil` (e.g., after `LoadCharacter`/`Destroy` interleaving), so the C.5.3 read-and-cache snippet uses a `FindFirstChild` + nil-coalesce to protect the health read. | Cross-system invariant for cost attribution; lifecycle expanded in Theme-1-round-2 patch + round-4 cross-service ordering contract (closes ai-programmer + performance-analyst convergent finding on stale-rejoin entries, ai-programmer + network-programmer convergent finding on init-order race, AND network-programmer C2 + ai-programmer F2 round-4 convergence on PlayerRemoving teardown-order race). |
| **Predator AI** | **`predatorCausedImminent` latch (round-9 B5 — closes the Scenario-C cost-evasion path)**: `PredatorService` MUST maintain a server-internal `predatorCausedImminent` boolean latch, **`Player`-instance-keyed** (same keying + init discipline as `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp`). **PC reads but does not write it** (read in the C.5.3 Path B handler's pre-yield snapshot block and in arm 3 of the imminent-death predicate). Lifecycle:<br>• **Bootstrap init**: initialize the table to `{}` (NOT `nil`) before any `Players.PlayerAdded`, alongside `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp`.<br>• **On `Players.PlayerAdded`**: leave the entry `nil`/absent (treated as `false` — predator has not driven this player sub-threshold).<br>• **On predator-damage application**: set `predatorCausedImminent[player] = true` AND record/renew `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[player] = getServerTime()` (via the C.11 seam) IF the damage either crosses the player from `Humanoid.Health ≥ HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` to `< HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD`, OR lands while the player is already `< HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD`. **This is the ONLY setter of both** (Pillar 2 — only predator damage may set them). **Qualifying condition (round-16 ai — closes the 0-damage latch renewal)**: only an application with **`damage > 0`** may set or renew the latch or re-stamp the timestamp — without this qualifier a 0-damage contact loop (blocked hits, immunity-frame touches) would renew the arm-3 outer ceiling indefinitely, defeating `LATCH_MAX_AGE`. **Crossing-detection prior-HP cache (round-16 ai — the R15 AI residual)**: the crossing test reads the player's HP into a local **`priorHp` IMMEDIATELY BEFORE PredatorService applies its damage, in the same synchronous span (no yield between the read and the application)**; the test is `priorHp >= HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD AND postHp < HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` (where `postHp` is the post-application `Humanoid.Health` read in that same span). An implementation that detects the crossing via a post-hoc `Humanoid.HealthChanged` re-read is **non-conforming** — it races other same-frame damage/heal writes and can mis-attribute or miss the 25.0 crossing. **Renew-on-each-application is NORMATIVE in this block, not only in C.5.3 prose (R12-AI-1 — split-spec closure)**: `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[player]` is **re-stamped to `getServerTime()` on EVERY qualifying predator damage application while the player is sub-threshold** — a set-once implementation built from this F.4 block alone is non-conforming (it would age a still-hunted player out of arm 3 at `LATCH_MAX_AGE` from the *first* hit, defeating the ceiling-refresh). A still-hunted player's arm-3 outer ceiling (R10-1, `LATCH_MAX_AGE`) therefore keeps refreshing; a player the predator stops touching ages out of arm 3 after `LATCH_MAX_AGE`. Damage that leaves the player at exactly `HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` does NOT set either (strict-`<` boundary parity with C.5.3). `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp` is **`Player`-instance-keyed**, bootstrap-initialized to `{}`, and has the same `PlayerAdded`/T6/`PlayerRemoving` lifecycle as the latch (below).<br>• **On HP recovery to `≥ HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` — deploy-gated + threshold-guarded HP-recovery watcher (network N-1 / ai AI-2)**: clear `predatorCausedImminent[player]` AND `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[player]` to `nil`/`false` — the predator no longer owns the player's sub-threshold state. Two binding qualifiers the prior prose left implicit:<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;— **Threshold-guarded, NOT every-HealthChanged.** The watcher clears the latch ONLY on a transition that brings `Humanoid.Health` from `< HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` to `≥ HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` (an actual recovery across the boundary). A `HealthChanged` that moves HP *within* the sub-threshold band (e.g. 18 → 12, or 12 → 18 while still `< 25`) MUST NOT clear the latch — the player is still sub-threshold and the predator still owns the state. A naive "`HealthChanged` → clear" connection would drop the latch on any heal tick that did not actually cross back, re-opening Scenario C.<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;— **Deploy-gated, NOT live-in-MVP.** In MVP no personal-HP heal/regen/restoration path exists, so this watcher has **no event to fire on** and the latch clears at T6 in practice. The watcher MUST be wired (a `Humanoid.HealthChanged` connection, or a hook on whatever restoration event is added) **at the moment any heal/regen/restoration mechanic is first authored — and is dormant until then.** **Named owner (R12-AI-2): `PredatorService` owns BOTH the HP-recovery watcher AND its deploy-gate** — PredatorService owns the latch, so PredatorService owns its clear-watcher; this F.4 row is the reverse-citation that makes the gate non-skippable (any future heal-feature GDD/story MUST cite this row and land the PredatorService watcher in the same change). **Named forward obligation (network N-F1, escalated round-11):** this is a *deploy gate*, not a free-standing MVP task — shipping a heal path WITHOUT simultaneously deploying the threshold-guarded clear-watcher would let arm 3 over-charge a player the predator drove low but who fully recovered (a reopened false-positive class). The obligation is named here so the heal-feature author cannot silently skip it; whoever adds healing owns deploying this watcher in the same change, and PredatorService is the implementation owner.<br>• **On T6 (respawn)**: clear — `predatorCausedImminent[player] = nil` AND `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[player] = nil` (paired with the `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp` T6 clearance). *(Trigger channel, round-16 AI-I1: executed in PredatorService's `OnPlayerT6Respawned(player)` handler ([R16-2]); the C.6 T6 row is the normative single trigger-site enumeration — this bullet documents, it does not trigger.)*<br>• **On `Players.PlayerRemoving`**: clear both under the same deferred no-op-if-replaced guard as `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp` (CONV-2 resolution above). | Cost-attribution correctness; lifecycle mirrors `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp` so no new lifecycle class is introduced. Closes round-8 game-designer B5 (user-ruled 2026-06-08: close the evasion window) + round-11 R10-1 outer ceiling / N-1+AI-2 watcher gating (user-ruled 2026-06-09). |
| **Predator AI** | **`PredatorService:ReleasePredatorLock(player)` contract (round-8 B-AI-2 — carried-unfulfilled since round-1 A3 / round-5 I.4, CD-escalated to BLOCKING)**: on T5 (death, either path) PC's death procedure MUST trigger release of any predator target-lock / aggro held on the dying player. **PC owns declaring the trigger (the caller owns the contract); `PredatorService` owns the implementation** of `:ReleasePredatorLock(player)` — drop the target lock, clear aggro, return the predator to search/idle target-selection. **`:ReleasePredatorLock(player)` is contractually IDEMPOTENT (R12-ai — promoted from a C.5.3 assertion to this normative block)**: releasing an already-released (or never-held) lock is a no-op, never an error — the C.5.3 reconciliation-success path and the C.6 T5 enum both re-invoke it on the strength of this contract. **S4 re-acquisition gate (R12-AI-4)**: the predator MUST **drop and refuse to (re-)acquire a target lock on any player in S4 (dead/awaiting-respawn)** — a dead player's `HumanoidRootPart` can linger (frozen collapse pose per V/A.1) for the full 30 s `RESPAWN_DELAY`, and without this gate the predator could re-lock the corpse the same tick the T5 `:ReleasePredatorLock(player)` call released it, re-creating the wasted-aggro state the release exists to clear. The gate holds for the player's entire S4 span (and S5); eligibility returns only at T6 (respawn), which is also when the damage/latch tables reset. **Gate mechanism ([R16-2], round-16 — the round-15 AI-1/NEW-1 closure)**: the gate's membership signal is the `OnPlayerS4Entered`/`OnPlayerT6Respawned` lifecycle pair (dedicated row below) — PredatorService no longer needs to infer S4 membership from position, which was impossible (the frozen corpse HRP is position-indistinguishable from a live player for the full 30 s). This is paired with the T6 clearance of `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp` + `predatorCausedImminent` (a dead player is no longer a damage source or a valid lock target). Without it, the predator can hold a lock on a player who is in S4/removed — incoherent with T6 clearing the damage timestamp, and a wasted-aggro bug. Aligns with ED's deferred `:ReleasePredatorLock` contract (ED Session C). | Predator-state coherence on death; PC's trigger is normative, the release algorithm + the S4 no-re-acquire gate are PredatorService's; idempotency is contractual. |
| **Predator AI** | **`OnPlayerS4Entered(player)` / `OnPlayerT6Respawned(player)` lifecycle signal pair ([R16-2], user-ruled 2026-06-11 — same caller-owns-contract style as `:ReleasePredatorLock`)**: PC fires both as server-internal cross-service signals (C.9 server-internal table; C.6 T5/T6 side-effects): `OnPlayerS4Entered(player)` at the T5→S4 transition (Path A only — a Path-B player is removed, covered by PredatorService's own `PlayerRemoving` teardown) and `OnPlayerT6Respawned(player)` at T6. **PC owns declaring the triggers; PredatorService owns the handler implementations**:<br>• **On `OnPlayerS4Entered`**: mark the player **not-a-valid-target** — drop any held lock (idempotent with the (g) `:ReleasePredatorLock` call) and refuse acquire/re-acquire for the player's entire S4/S5 span, even though the frozen corpse `HumanoidRootPart` lingers the full 30 s `RESPAWN_DELAY` (the R12-AI-4 gate's mechanism — H.59).<br>• **On `OnPlayerT6Respawned`**: restore target eligibility; execute the T6 clearance of `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp` / `predatorCausedImminent` / `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp` (the SINGLE trigger channel — round-16 AI-I1; the C.6 T6 row is the normative enumeration); AND perform a **predator state-flush** for this player — forget any remembered prior position / search focus (the respawned player is a new body at a new anchor; chasing death-point memory would be a phantom-position class — H.60).<br>• Both handlers are **idempotent** (a duplicate signal is a no-op) and both signals are server-internal — never RemoteEvents, never client-reachable. | Expands the F.2 perception contract from position-only to position + lifecycle ([R16-2]); resolves the round-15 S4 position-vs-corpse perception conflict (ai AI-1 + NEW-1 + build + systems convergence). |
| **Architecture (`RunController` / `/create-architecture`)** | **Single `RunEnded` broadcaster + victory-over-wipe precedence + caller-side provisional contract (round-8 B4 + round-11 R10-5, CD-ruled; [R16-1] user-ruled 2026-06-11 — the cross-GDD double-broadcaster contradiction closed)**: `RunEnded` MUST have exactly one authoritative broadcaster — the RunController arbiter. **[R16-1]: NEITHER PC NOR CRAFTING broadcasts `RunEnded`. ALL run-end conditions are *raised* to the arbiter via `RunEndConditionRaised`: PC raises its T7 whole-squad-wipe (pre-activation only — C.5.8 suppression) and its T8 victory (consuming Crafting's `OnBeaconWindowSurvived(timestamp)`); Crafting's BCT-DEFEAT in-window defeats are raised by CRAFTING DIRECTLY to the arbiter — NOT relayed through PC** (the matching one-line prose-pointer landed in Crafting's C.10 `runOutcomeResolved` schema this round; Crafting's APPROVED-by-acceptance is NOT reopened — doc-integrity touch only, per the panel-cannot-overturn precedent). The `conditionType` vocabulary for Crafting's raise (whether `"wipe"` is reused or a distinct defeat reason is added) is a RunController-GDD authoring decision; PC's own caller-side contract below is unchanged. A single owner (a `RunController` or equivalent, to be authored) resolves the broadcast once.<br>**Caller-side provisional contract PC authors NOW (so the arbiter conforms to PC, not the reverse — R10-5):** PC raises a run-end condition by calling **`RunController:RunEndConditionRaised(conditionType, squadState)`** where<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;— `conditionType ∈ {"wipe", "victory"}` — PC raises `"wipe"` on its T7 (all 2–4 members in S4 AND squad oxygen `< 1`); PC raises `"victory"` on its T8 (consuming Crafting's `OnBeaconWindowSurvived`, BCT4).<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;— `squadState` — a snapshot of per-member liveness `{ [userId] = "alive" | "S4" | "S5" }` at raise time, so the arbiter can resolve the S4-rescue rule (a member in S4 at a resolved victory is rescued → counted survivor, respawn timer cancelled).<br>**Arbiter resolution (fixed precedence):** a resolved `"victory"` outranks a coincident/subsequent `"wipe"`; a `"victory"` is broadcast immediately (no hold).<br>**N-6 defeat-hold window:** because at the 2-player floor a full-squad wipe and the BCT4 window-survived emit can land in **adjacent ticks**, the arbiter MUST NOT broadcast a `"wipe"` defeat the instant it is raised — it holds a raised `"wipe"` for a short **`RUN_END_DEFEAT_HOLD`** window (default **0.5 s**, range **0.25–1.0 s**, registered in G.6 as provisional/RunController-owned per R12-SD-5; to be tuned against the observed PC-T7-vs-Crafting-BCT4 server-tick skew at architecture) and broadcasts defeat only if no `"victory"` arrives during the hold. A `"victory"` arriving during the hold discards the held wipe (victory precedence). This closes the "a coincident wipe one tick before BCT4 paints a defeat over a survived window" race that the bare precedence rule alone did not bound. **Degenerate-at-0 failure (R12-SD-5)**: at `RUN_END_DEFEAT_HOLD = 0` the hold protection collapses — a defeat broadcast pre-empts an in-flight BCT4 victory arriving the next tick; the hold MUST be ≥ several 60 Hz Heartbeats (the 0.25 s range floor ≈ 15 Heartbeats). **Player experience during the hold (R12-game-B3)**: while the wipe is held, the defeat banner is **withheld** and all inputs stay locked (the S4/S5 input lock is already in force) — at ≤ 1.0 s this reads to the squad as a *beat of silence* after the last death, not a freeze or a hitch; the banner that arrives after arbitration (defeat, or a rescuing victory) is the authoritative one, and no provisional defeat UI may be shown and retracted.<br>**INBOUND side — PC subscribes to `RunEnded(outcome)` (round-20 keystone, build-B1/SD-19-B3):** the obligations above are the *raise* (outbound) half. PC ALSO **subscribes** to the arbiter's single `RunEnded(outcome ∈ {"victory","defeat"})` broadcast (declared in the C.9 inbound-subscription row) — its handler clears `_beaconActivated` and drives every alive/S4 player → S5 by outcome + input lock (C.6 T7/T8, H.61). This is the ONLY message-driven clear-path for the latch. The arbiter MUST deliver exactly one `RunEnded` per run.<br>**Self-heal backstop (R17-4):** because the arbiter is unauthored, PC carries a `BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` self-heal (C.6 latch table, G.6) that force-clears `_beaconActivated` if no `RunEnded` arrives within the timeout — so a dropped broadcast cannot wedge the run (Pillar-4). When the arbiter is authored it SHOULD make the broadcast reliable enough that the self-heal never fires in practice; the self-heal remains as defense-in-depth.<br>**RAISE-axis idempotency the arbiter MUST honor (round-22 ai Finding 4 — names the contract the self-heal implies):** the `BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` self-heal re-enables the T7 wipe terminal, which can then **re-raise** `RunController:RunEndConditionRaised("wipe", squadState)` for a condition the arbiter may already be resolving (e.g. a Crafting BCT-DEFEAT raised just before the self-heal fired). The arbiter MUST therefore be **idempotent on the RAISE axis, not only on the broadcast axis** — a second `RunEndConditionRaised` for an already-resolving/resolved run is a no-op, never a second `RunEnded` and never a re-opened run. PC names this here as a caller-side expectation so the RunController GDD authors it; PC's own duplicate-suppression is the `_beaconActivated` latch + the C.9 inbound-`RunEnded` idempotent handler, but the cross-tick raise-then-self-heal-then-re-raise window can only be closed on the arbiter's side. | Authority/no-double-broadcast invariant + the caller-side contract is authored now so the arbiter conforms to PC; the owner system is not yet in the systems-index — flagged here so the obligation is visible at architecture. **Round-20: the inbound subscription + the `BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` self-heal are PC-side; the reliable-delivery expectation is the arbiter's.** |

On first receipt Crafting one-way-latches off its interim `CharacterAdded` self-sourced re-add. Both edges idempotent at Crafting's buffered drain. | `RunService.Heartbeat` | Stamina drain/regen tick | Engine built-in; verified |
| `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` | All server-side timing (pulse intervals, grace window, regen delay, respawn timer) per ED.D.1 — **accessed exclusively through the C.11 `getServerTime()` seam; never called inline at PC call sites (R12-build-B1)** | Engine built-in; verified |

| `UserInputService.TouchStarted` | Two-finger ping gesture detection (per-frame accumulator pattern in client controller) | Engine built-in; verified |

| `HapticService` | Optional gamepad rumble for state transitions / death feedback | Engine built-in; **wrap calls in `IsMotorSupported()` guard** per gameplay-programmer flag |
| `workspace:HasChunkLoaded()` (or current-name equivalent) | Spectator camera StreamingEnabled chunk-load gate per C.5.6 + E.E | **Verify API name against current Roblox Creator Docs before architecture** — flagged as knowledge-gap risk |

## Tuning Knobs

All knobs are **server-authoritative** and live in a single config module (consumed by Knit `PlayerControllerService`). Categories: **Feel** (player-perceived responsiveness), **Curve** (drives a formula), **Gate** (rate-limit / threshold). Knobs flagged "**ED-locked**" are owned by the Ecological Disturbance registry and **MUST NOT** be redefined here — listed only to surface their relevance.

### G.1 — Locomotion Knobs

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WALK_SPEED` | 12 studs/s | 8–16 | Feel | C.1 | Movement feels punishment-slow; squad pacing breaks; gather travel time exceeds 5–20 min run window | Walk feels arcade-y; "fragile body" fantasy lost; sprint differentiation collapses |
| `SPRINT_SPEED` | 20 studs/s | 16–28 | Feel | C.1 | Sprint feels like fast-walk; loud-action distinction lost | Mobile touch precision degrades >24; players overshoot interaction targets |

### G.2 — Stamina Knobs

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `STAMINA_MAX` | 100 units | 60–200 | Curve | C.2, D.1 | Sprint windows feel trivial; "deliberate transaction" fantasy collapses | Sprint is effectively unlimited; Pillar 1 weakens |
| `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE` | 12.5 units/s | 8–20 | Curve | C.2, D.1, D.3 | Sprint duration feels generous (12+ s at default max); Pillar 1 weakens | Sprint windows shorten to <5s; aggressive players never escape predator |
| `STAMINA_REGEN_RATE` | 10 units/s | 5–18 | Curve | C.2, D.2 | Recovery takes >20s; sprint feels punishing | Recovery feels free; sprint-rest-sprint becomes optimal everywhere |
| `STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY` | 1.5 s | 0.5–4.0 | Feel | C.2, D.2 | Bar twitches on partial-sprint inputs; tap-sprint micro-management feels noisy | Felt as "broken regen"; punishment perception |

**Coupled tuning note**: `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE` and `STAMINA_REGEN_RATE` define the spend/recovery asymmetry (default ratio 0.8). Adjusting either independently breaks the design intent that "sprint everywhere, walk on empty" is a losing strategy. Tune as a coupled pair.

**G.2 invariant — `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE > 0` (R12-SD-3, force-resolved round-13 after 7 deferrals)**: the range floor (8 units/s) strictly excludes 0 and is **load-bearing, not advisory** — D.3 (`T_sprint = s_0 / STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE`) and the C.2.2 time-to-empty derivation (`STAMINA_MAX / STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE`) both divide by this knob and are undefined at 0. Config validation MUST reject any `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE ≤ 0` (machine-checkable: `assert(config.STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE > 0)`); see the D.3 divide-by-zero guard note. **Verified as a config-validation gate by H.12a clause (ii) (round-18 systems-B2 — H.12a is the single gate for all three machine-checkable invariants).**

**G.2 invariant — stamina-curve domain gate (round-28 T-4, systems NEW-1)**: `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE` was the ONLY stamina-curve knob sign-gated; the other three are now gated to their registered ranges by **H.12a clause (v)**, load-bearing not advisory. `STAMINA_MAX` is rejected at `<= 0`: a negative `STAMINA_MAX` makes the D.1/D.2 clamp `math.clamp(x, 0, STAMINA_MAX)` evaluate `min > max` and **throw on every Heartbeat tick (a hard per-frame crash)**, and `STAMINA_MAX = 0` is a permanent forced-walk; `STAMINA_REGEN_RATE < 0` silently drains stamina during rest; `STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY < 0` is a nonsensical pre-emptive regen. Config validation MUST assert each is within its G.2 range (`STAMINA_MAX >= 60`, `STAMINA_REGEN_RATE >= 5`, `STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY >= 0.5`) — see H.12a clause (v) and the C.12 completeness invariant (every sign-sensitive knob has a domain gate).

### G.3 — Lantern Knobs

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `LANTERN_VISIBILITY_RADIUS` | 20 studs | 12–40 | Feel | C.3 | Dark zones become navigationally hostile; players keep lantern up constantly | Lantern reduces tension; "where safety ends" rule weakens |

### G.4 — Coordination Knobs

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PING_DISPLAY_DURATION` | 8 s | 3–15 | Feel | C.4.1 | Pings disappear before squad reads them | Stale pings clutter HUD; ping volume in 4-player squads becomes overwhelming |
| `PING_COOLDOWN_PER_PLAYER` | 1.0 s | 0.5–3.0 | Gate | C.4.1, E.F | Ping spam from a single player floods squad HUD | Pings feel unresponsive in fast encounters |
| `PING_BURST_CAP` | 3 | 2–6 | Gate | C.4.1 | Single-player burst pinging in danger sequences feels unresponsive | Burst-cap stops gating spam |
| `PING_BURST_WINDOW` | 1.5 s | 1.0–5.0 | Gate | C.4.1 | Burst window too tight, legitimate fast-pings denied | Burst window too wide, spam tolerance returns |
| `PING_RAYCAST_RANGE` | 80 studs | 40–200 | Feel | C.4.1 | Pings can't reach distant threats; players feel unequipped to flag distant predators | Pings reach across map; intel becomes too cheap |
| `PING_ORIGIN_TOLERANCE` | 8 studs (placeholder) | 4–16 | Gate | C.4.1, C.9 | Legitimate pings rejected when client/server `HumanoidRootPart.Position` desyncs under lag; ping feels unreliable in fast encounters | Implausible/spoofed ping origins accepted; ping trust-boundary weakened (R6-B4). **First-pass placeholder — value tuned in Theme 2.** |
| `PING_TWO_FINGER_WINDOW` | 150 ms | 80–300 | Feel | C.7, E.F | Legitimate two-finger taps register as separate touches; quick-ping is unreliable on touch | Palm-rest false-pings register as intentional |
| `EMOTE_COOLDOWN_PER_PLAYER` | 3.0 s | 1.0–10.0 | Gate | C.4.2 | Emote spam clutters HUD | Emotes feel unresponsive; coordination signal degrades |
| `EMOTE_BURST_CAP` | 5 | 3–10 | Gate | C.4.2 | Burst tolerance too low; legitimate fast-emote sequences blocked | Burst tolerance too high; spam re-emerges |
| `EMOTE_BURST_WINDOW` | 15.0 s | 10.0–60.0 | Gate | C.4.2 | Window too short to detect spam patterns | Window too wide; legitimate sequential emoting feels rate-limited |
| `EMOTE_DISPLAY_DURATION` | 4 s | 2–8 | Feel | C.4.2 | Emotes vanish before squad reads | Emote markers persist past relevance; HUD clutter |
| `EMOTE_WHEEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT` | 4.0 s | 2–8 | Feel | C.4.2 | Wheel auto-closes while a player is still deciding under stress; emote selection feels snatched away | Accidental opens hold the T9 touch gather lockout too long — the round-16 failure this knob exists to bound |

### G.5 — Touch-Input Knobs

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TAP_HOLD_COMMIT_DURATION` | 500 ms | 300–800 | Feel | C.7 | Accidental holds register as commits; mis-tap defence weakens | Gather feels unresponsive on PC; accusations of "stuck input" |
| `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS` | 4 studs | 2–8 | Gate | C.7 | Gate disarms during slow approach; player must stop *exactly* on the node | Gate arms from too far; player can pre-arm gather while still walking past |
| `MOBILE_ANALOG_DRIFT_THRESHOLD` | 2 studs | 1–4 | Gate | C.7, D.4 | Real player movement classified as drift; emissions falsely attenuate | Genuine analog drift not filtered; stationary players unintentionally publish full-magnitude pulses |
| `EMOTE_TOUCH_TARGET_MIN` | 44 px (`UDim2` Offset — device-independent px via `UIScale`, ≈ Apple-HIG 44 dp; round-28 ux gate) | 40–60 | Feel | C.4.2 (round-28 ux NEW-1) | Emote-open touch target below the ~44 dp accessible minimum; mis-taps on iPhone-SE-class screens open the wheel and engage the T9 gather lockout | Oversized target crowds the lower-right HUD zone; competes with other touch affordances |
| `EMOTE_TOUCH_SEPARATION_MIN` | 12 px (`UDim2` Offset, as above) | 8–24 | Feel | C.4.2, C.7/T9 (round-28 ux NEW-1) | Emote button too close to the Jump button **or the right-thumb gather/tap-hold zone** (the higher-risk adjacency); a Jump tap or a gather tap-hold clips the emote target → unintended wheel open + T9 gather lockout | Excess separation pushes the emote button into an awkward reach on small screens |

### G.6 — Death/Respawn Knobs

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RESPAWN_DELAY` | 30 s | 10–60 | Feel | C.5 | Death feels inconsequential; squad doesn't feel the loss; Pillar 2 (shared risk) weakens | Death feels punishing; dead player loses interest before respawn; Pillar 2 (squad cohesion) weakens |
| `RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL` | 5.0 s | 2–10 | Gate | C.5.3, C.11 | Tick overhead grows; reconciliation thrash on transient RM failures | The nominal recovery bound (`2 × interval`) grows, so the surviving squad waits longer for the delayed `reconciliation-recovery` notification — a **UX** delay, NOT a correctness gap (a `Pending` record survives T6 and reconciles post-respawn since round-9). See the coupled-knob note below. |

**G.6 coupled-knob note — `RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL` × `RESPAWN_DELAY` (round-9, systems Finding 4 closure)**: the prior round stated `2 × RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL < RESPAWN_DELAY` as a hard correctness invariant — but it is **violable within the stated ranges** (at `RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL = 10` and `RESPAWN_DELAY = 10`, `20 < 10` is false) and was therefore not a valid invariant. The round-9 `DeathCostReconciliation` refactor **removes the correctness dependency**: a `Pending` record is `deathEventId`-keyed and survives T6, so a Path-A player respawning before reconciliation no longer loses the cost. The relation is now an **advisory UX target**: to guarantee the surviving squad receives the `reconciliation-recovery` notification *before* the dead player respawns (tighter feedback), pick `RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL ≤ floor(RESPAWN_DELAY / 2) − 1`. If the knobs are set outside this advisory band, the only consequence is a delayed notification, not a lost charge — so it is a *coupled tuning preference*, not a gate that can break Pillar 2.
| `SPRINT_CLIENT_TIMEOUT` | 3 s | 1–10 | Gate | E.D | Legitimate brief client lag (mobile reconnect flap) classified as zombie; sprint reverts during a valid session momentary hitch | Ghost-sprint persists up to N seconds after client becomes unresponsive; up to `N / SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL` extra pulses emitted |
| `HEARTBEAT_SEND_RATE` | 1 Hz | 0.5–2 | Gate | E.D, C.9 | Heartbeats arrive too infrequently; `SPRINT_CLIENT_TIMEOUT` fires spuriously during legitimate high-jitter connections | Heartbeat traffic exceeds bandwidth budget; at 2 Hz × 4 players × 40 bytes ≈ 320 B/s — still within H.29 budget but approaching it |
| `GRACE_REENTRY_COOLDOWN` | 6.0 s | 4.0–12.0 | Gate | C.8.1, C.8.2, D.4, E.C | Below 4.0 s the cadence exploit partially reopens (per-axis math floor `max(SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL + STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY, LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL) = 4.0`); legitimate second-sprint feels invisible but exploit re-emerges | Above 12 s a player who genuinely sprinted hard, walked 11 s to recover, and sprinted again finds their second sprint silenced — punishes legitimate long-sprint sequences |

**G.6 invariants for `GRACE_REENTRY_COOLDOWN` (B9 anchor-on-eligibility rule)**:

1. The cooldown anchor `t_lastGracePulse[axis] = t_0` is written when `grace_eligible == true` is *resolved at state-entry*, NOT when the grace-window pulse fires. Under server jitter (e.g., thermally-throttled mobile Heartbeat tick longer than 1.0 s), the first pulse may arrive after `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW`. The cooldown still holds because the anchor was recorded at eligibility resolution, not at pulse fire.
2. **G1 exploit class is closed under any server delivery latency** because the cooldown is a function of when eligibility was *granted*, not of whether the grace pulse was *delivered* on time. A player who toggles sprint off/on under thermally-throttled latency cannot harvest repeated grace exemptions by exploiting the missed-window state.
3. The trade-off is a cost the *player* pays under jitter (they may forfeit one grace exemption they were entitled to under nominal latency), not a cost the *predator* pays. Pillar 1 enforcement strictly biases toward cost-paid; never toward cost-skipped.
| `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW` | 30.0 s | 15–60 | Gate | C.5.3, E.E | Legitimate "lethal combo in flight" disconnects misclassified as non-predator (player took heavy graze, ran 20 s, disconnected at low HP — predator IS proximate cause but the window already expired); Pillar 2 false-negative class | Stale-graze false-positive class re-opens — slow oxygen-depletion death after a long-ago predator brush starts paying squad cost again; Pillar 2 false-positive class (the round-3 defect this knob closes) |
| `LATCH_MAX_AGE` | 120.0 s ([R13-F2] — user-ruled 2026-06-10: stays 120 s) | 60–180, **subject to the coupled floor `> HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW + 30 s`** (with `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW` at its 60 s range max, `LATCH_MAX_AGE` must be **> 90 s** — the bare 60 s range floor is reachable only when `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW` is tuned below 30 s) | Gate | C.5.3, F.4, E.E | **Below the coupled floor: re-opens Scenario C** — a player the predator drove sub-threshold who fled past the ceiling then disconnects pays nothing, restoring the round-8 B5 evasion path | The arm-3 weak-attribution **over-charge tail returns** — a player the predator touched once, then survived sub-threshold for minutes with no further contact, is still charged on an eventual disconnect (the predator's causal contribution has decayed) |
| `RUN_END_DEFEAT_HOLD` | 0.5 s | 0.25–1.0 | Gate | C.6 (T7), F.4 | **Degenerate at 0 (R12-SD-5)**: the defeat-hold protection collapses — a raised wipe broadcasts defeat before an adjacent-tick BCT4 victory can arrive, painting a defeat over a survived window; the hold must span **≥ several 60 Hz Heartbeats** (the 0.25 s floor ≈ 15 Heartbeats) to bound the PC-T7-vs-Crafting-BCT4 tick skew | Above 1.0 s the post-wipe beat-of-silence (F.4 player-experience prose) starts reading as a freeze/hang rather than a beat — the squad stares at locked inputs with no banner. **Provisional/RunController-owned** (PC introduced it; the RunController GDD is unauthored — this row is the registration of record until then, R12-SD-5/game-B3) |
| `BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` | 300 s (R17-4, user-ruled 2026-06-14) | 180–600 | Gate | C.6 (latch table), C.9 (inbound `RunEnded`), F.4 | **Below the longest legitimate Beacon window the self-heal fires during normal play** — the latch clears mid-window, T7's wipe terminal re-enables while the Beacon is still live, and an in-window squad-wipe could double-resolve (Crafting BCT-DEFEAT *and* a re-enabled PC T7 raise). The floor MUST stay above `activation → RunEnded` worst-case (`BEACON_HALF_LIFE` 128 s window-max + `RUN_END_DEFEAT_HOLD` + arbiter latency, ≈ <120 s of survival time per Crafting H.105 + ED `BEACON_HALF_LIFE ∈ [64,128] s`); 180 s is the hard floor, 300 s the ~2.5× default margin | Above ~600 s a genuinely lost `RunEnded` broadcast leaves the run wedged (T7 suppressed, inputs unlocked-but-purposeless) for up to the full timeout before the backstop trips — the squad sits in a dead run too long. **Safety backstop only** — never the normal terminal; provisional/RunController-coupled (the arbiter is unauthored, F.4). |
| `DEATH_SFX_MAX_VOICES` | 2 | **2–4** (floor raised 1→2 — round-14 audio ruling, CD-accepted, applied round-16 at all 3 sites: this row, the V/A.3 inline cite, entities.yaml) | Gate | V/A.3, V/A.4a | **Below 2 is FORBIDDEN**: at 1 voice a 2-player near-simultaneous wipe sheds the second death's positional voice — the squad's most common burst case loses its spatial identity, the exact regression the round-14 ruling closed (a tuner could previously legally set 1 and reintroduce it) | Overlapping percussive Death SFX impacts pile into audio mud during a squad wipe (the R11-D4 class the cap exists to prevent); above 4 is meaningless — the squad is at most 4, so at most 4 near-simultaneous deaths exist (R12-AD-B2 registration) |
| `DEATH_SFX_BATCH_WINDOW` | 0.25 s | 0.1–0.5 | Gate | V/A.3 | Deaths a few frames apart land in separate "batches" and each fires its own overflow cue — the coalescing the batch exists for stops happening | Deaths up to half a second apart coalesce into one overflow cue; a genuinely sequential pair of overflow deaths reads as one event (round-16 audio — the overflow "batch" boundary the V/A.3 one-cue-per-batch rule previously left undefined) |

**G.6 coupled-knob invariant — `LATCH_MAX_AGE` × `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW` ([R13-F2], user-ruled 2026-06-10; closes R12-SD-7/AI-8)**: machine-checkable, enforced by config validation:

```
LATCH_MAX_AGE > HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW + 30.0   -- margin ≥ 30 s, strict
```

The prior prose-only "must stay `>` with margin" admitted the degenerate 60/60 corner — `LATCH_MAX_AGE` at its 60 s range minimum with `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW` at its 60 s range maximum gives a margin of ZERO, and Scenario C reopens (a player can flee exactly past the shared boundary and disconnect cost-free). The invariant makes the margin a validation gate, not a tuning convention: at the [R13-F2] defaults (`120 > 30 + 30`) it holds with 60 s of headroom; with `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW` at its 60 s max, any `LATCH_MAX_AGE ≤ 90 s` is rejected. Config validation MUST assert this invariant alongside the G.2 drain-rate floor and the G.7 [R13-F1] magnitude hierarchy. **All three are bound by the single config-validation gate AC H.12a — clause (iii) here, clause (ii) for G.2, clause (i) for G.7 (round-18 systems-B2 closure: the three invariants previously cited H.12a as their gate but only the hierarchy was actually asserted there).**

**G.6 boundary-comparator semantics (B11)**: `HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD` is compared with strict `<` (HP=25.0 exactly does NOT fire arm 1). `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW` and `DAMAGE_RECENT_WINDOW` are also compared with strict `<` (an elapsed time exactly equal to the window does NOT satisfy the recency check). This is intentionally asymmetric with E.F's `PING_COOLDOWN_PER_PLAYER` precedent (`>=` accept-at-boundary): ping-cooldown UX favors player-friendly acceptance at the boundary; imminent-death cost attribution favors squad-friendly rejection at the boundary. See C.5.3 boundary semantics block for the full rationale and the practical "use 24.99 / 25.01" tuning guidance.

**Gate-comparator semantics for the three elapsed-time gates (round-9, systems IMPORTANT — B11 class)**: the elapsed-time gates fire on `elapsed > knob` (strict greater-than — the action is taken once the window has been *exceeded*), the inclusive direction opposite the strict-`<` recency comparators above:
- **`FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW = 1.0 s`** — a pulse is grace-window-eligible while `(t − t_0) <= FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW` (a pulse at exactly `t_0 + 1.0 s` is still within the window; the grace branch uses `<=` so a boundary-frame pulse is not wrongly denied its grace — D.4 Step 0a).
- **`RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL = 5.0 s`** — the tick-interval gate fires when `(now − lastTickTime) > RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL` (strict `>`; the first scan executes only once the interval has been strictly exceeded, per H.40).
- **`SPRINT_CLIENT_TIMEOUT = 3 s`** — the zombie-sprint timeout fires when `(now − _lastHeartbeatTime) > SPRINT_CLIENT_TIMEOUT` (strict `>`; a player exactly at the boundary is given the benefit of one more frame, per H.33a).
These comparators are mirrored in C.5.3 / C.8 / E.D and in the corresponding ACs so the tests exercise the documented edge.

### G.7 — ED-Locked Constants (referenced, not redefined here — owned by `design/gdd/ecological-disturbance.md` registry)

| Knob | Default | Owner | Used By |
|---|---|---|---|
| `EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR` | 1 stud | ED registry | C.7, C.8, D.4, D.6 |
| `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` | 0.30 | ED registry | C.7, C.8, D.4 |
| `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW` | 1.0 s | ED registry | C.6 (T1, T3), C.8.1, C.8.2, D.4 |
| `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL` | 2 s | ED registry | C.6 (T1), C.8.1 |
| `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL` | 4 s | ED registry | C.6 (T3), C.8.2 |
| `MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE` | 0.10 | ED registry | C.8.1, D.4 |
| `MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE` | 0.08 | ED registry | C.8.2, D.4 |
| `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR` | 0.09 ([R13-F1] — raised from 0.06, user-ruled 2026-06-10) | ED registry (**LANDED round-20 — ED G.5 + `entities.yaml`**) | C.8.1, D.4 (R10-6) — Sprint-axis stationary hold-floor; emission magnitude PC introduced round-11, now in the ED registry per the G.7 magnitude-ownership rule (the round-20 SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR→ED landing closed the 8-round PC-provisional carry); range (0.08, 0.10) exclusive per the invariant below. *(The ED-side D.7 stationary-sprint stress-test re-derivation + the design-intent conflict remain routed to ED Session B/C + CD — F.4; the VALUE here is final.)* |

Any change to a G.7 row requires modifying the ED GDD's source (registry update + ED.G.5 tuning section), not this GDD.

**G.7 emission-hierarchy invariant ([R13-F1], user-ruled 2026-06-10; closes R12-game-B1/SD-1)**: machine-checkable, enforced by config validation (and owed to the ED registry alongside the knob itself, F.4):

```
MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE (0.08) < SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR (0.09) < MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE (0.10)
```

Both comparisons strict. The lower bound preserves the Pillar-1 emission hierarchy (Walk silent < Lantern 0.08 < any Sprint pulse) — at the pre-[R13-F1] value 0.06 a stationary sprint was *quieter than a raised lantern*, inverting the hierarchy. The upper bound preserves the moving(`0.10`)-vs-holding gradient — at `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR = MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE` the `max()` collapses the gradient and stationary sprint costs the same as movement (the R12-SD-1 range-max degeneracy). The knob's tunable range is therefore **(0.08, 0.10) exclusive**; `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR` remains ED-registry-owned and the invariant travels with it (H.12a verifies it in config validation).

### G.8 — Tuning Methodology

1. Default values shipped here are **first-pass design intent**, not playtest-validated. Architecture phase consumes them as-is; first-playtest-data revises them.
2. **Never tune two coupled knobs in the same patch without playtesting between** (e.g., `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE` + `STAMINA_REGEN_RATE`).
3. **Touch-platform knobs MUST be playtested on iPhone SE-class hardware under thermal throttle** (per game-concept). PC-only validation is insufficient.
4. **No knob exposed to the client**. All values live in server-side config. Clients read state, not config.
5. **Playtest-plan forward obligation — the 2-player-floor degenerate-cascade hypothesis (round-16 R15 game NEW-3; round-18 game-GD-2: upgraded from a "watch for it" note to a FALSIFIABLE hypothesis with a named relief lever + kill-criterion; NOT an AC — experiential routing per the round-14 precedent)**. The cascade: one death → disturbance spike → the surviving partner faces the run (and any Beacon window) solo → squad-oxygen cascade.
   - **Hypothesis (H0, the testable claim):** at default tunings, a single death at the 2-player floor does NOT make the run statistically unrecoverable — the post-first-death win-rate at the 2-player floor stays within an acceptable margin of the no-death 2-player baseline (the cascade reads as *earned tension*, not a spiral).
   - **Kill-criterion (what falsifies H0 and forces action):** H0 is **rejected** if observed 2-player post-first-death runs win at **< 50% of the rate of no-death 2-player runs** (first-pass threshold — revise with data per G.8.1), OR if playtesters consistently report the post-first-death state as an unrecoverable spiral rather than recoverable tension. Either outcome is a falsification, not a vibe.
   - **Primary relief lever (named + ordered):** on rejection, the FIRST lever is a **squad-size-coupled `RESPAWN_DELAY`** (shorten the respawn wait at the 2-player floor so the lone survivor regains a teammate sooner), THEN the G.6 death-cost knob. The squad-size coupling IS the named sub-hypothesis under test: that a *flat* `RESPAWN_DELAY` is what tips the 2-player cascade into unrecoverable, and that coupling it to squad size is the minimal fix. Route findings to those knobs (OQ.6) — **never to new mechanics**.
6. **Playtest-plan forward obligation (round-16 — R15 game/UX, routed per the same experiential precedent; NOT an AC)**: observe the **3 s `EMOTE_COOLDOWN_PER_PLAYER` against 2-player Danger→Quiet pacing** — at the squad floor, the Danger-then-Quiet two-emote choreography spans the cooldown, so the second emote may land after the moment has passed. **Resolution (round-22 R21-2, user-ruled 2026-06-14): the 2-player cooldown friction is ACCEPTED as intended restraint cost.** If playtest confirms the beat is missed, the ONLY relief lever is the `EMOTE_COOLDOWN_PER_PLAYER` G.4 knob value — **never a new mechanic.** (The prior wording offered "or a Danger→Quiet exemption pair" as a candidate, which this same note forbids as a new mechanic — a self-contradiction; the exemption-pair candidate is **deleted**, leaving the knob as the sole lever.)

### G.9 — Audio-Feedback Floor Knobs (player-information / accessibility floors — round-18)

These knobs bound audio *feedback* cues from below so a mix pass or a cosmetic can never silently delete a player-information channel. They are NOT predator-perception inputs (V/A.3 boundary note) — they are own-player / squad-proximity feedback floors only.

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WALK_FOOTSTEP_VOLUME_FLOOR` | 0.20 (Sound.Volume scalar) | 0.10–0.40 | Gate | V/A.3, V/A.6 | Walk audio drops below the squad-proximity-awareness read; a cosmetic timbre swap can make a teammate's footsteps inaudible — silently removing the "where is my teammate" cue (player-information regression) | Walk footsteps dominate the mix; the deliberate quiet-walk feel is lost and walk reads as loud-and-detectable (Pillar-1 mental-model risk) |
| `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_VOLUME_FLOOR` | 0.25 (Sound.Volume scalar) | 0.10–0.50 | Gate | V/A.3, V/A.6 | The exertion cue (either variant) is inaudible on a lowest-tier mobile speaker — the moving-vs-holding gradient loses its only audio channel (R17-2 collapse) | The "subtle exertion/breath" cue reads as a danger alarm rather than effort; the cue starts to feel like a broadcast cue (V/A.3 boundary note) |
| `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_MIN_DELTA_DB` | **4.0 dB** (R17-2, user-ruled 2026-06-14 — raised from 2.0; the 2.0 default sat below JND under iPhone-SE ambient/speaker masking, 3–4 dB masked / 4–6 dB same-timbre, so the moving-vs-holding gradient was imperceptible on the baseline device — round-19 audio-AD-B2) | 3.0–6.0 | Gate | V/A.3, V/A.6 | Below 3.0 dB the full(0.10)-vs-hold(0.09) variants risk collapsing to one indistinguishable cue under same-timbre mobile masking (the R17-2 failure); the timbre/rhythm differentiator (below) is the second, masking-robust channel that lets the dB step stay at a non-alarming level | Above ~6 dB the full variant reads as an escalation/alarm rather than "you're moving" even with the timbre hook present — risks re-teaching audio-volume = mechanical-risk (Pillar-1 mental-model risk) |
| `WORLD_RESPONSE_CUE_VOLUME_FLOOR` | **4.0 dB above the Hunt-tier ambient bed** (R21-3, user-ruled 2026-06-14); **scoped to the SUSTAINED portion of the swell** (round-24 R23-1(b) — the 50–80 ms onset transient is exempt) | 3.0–6.0 dB | Gate | V/A.3, V/A.6, C.9 (`OnWorldResponseCue`) | Below 3.0 dB the R17-1 world-response swell is masked by the escalated disturbance-tier ambient it plays over — the in-run loop-closer becomes inaudible exactly when it matters (high disturbance), and loud→consequence stops being perceptible in-run (the round-19 #2 Pillar-1 hole re-opens). A **dB-above-ambient** floor (not a fixed 0–1 scalar) because the cue is a rare event (~2–4/run) that fires at the loudest ambient tiers | Above ~6 dB the swell reads as an alarm/escalation rather than a low "the world stirred" registration — drifts toward a danger/predator-tell read the V/A.3 anti-pillar forbids |

All four are **first-pass design intent, art-bible-calibrated**, NOT playtest-validated (G.8.1); the mix-review evidence items (H.57a for the three exertion/walk floors, H.78a for `WORLD_RESPONSE_CUE_VOLUME_FLOOR`) confirm they are perceptually true on iPhone SE-class hardware. Config-validation asserts each floor is positive and `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_MIN_DELTA_DB > 0` **AND rejects a config whose authored hold-variant volume is below `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_VOLUME_FLOOR`, whose authored full−hold delta is below `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_MIN_DELTA_DB`, or whose authored walk-footstep volume is below `WALK_FOOTSTEP_VOLUME_FLOOR`** — the authoritative, complete gate for those three is **H.57a Part 1**; `WORLD_RESPONSE_CUE_VOLUME_FLOOR`'s gate is **H.78a Part 1** — which (round-24 CONVERGENCE A) compares the authored **`worldResponseCueGainDb`** config field against the floor AND asserts the floor constant is in-range (`>= 3.0`, not merely `> 0`) (this line is a summary; build the validators from the named ACs, not from this sentence alone).

### G.9.1 — Authored Mix Fields (the per-mix values the G.9 floors gate against) — round-28 T-4

The G.9 floors above are **constants** (the lower bounds). The config-validation gates (H.57a Part 1, H.78a Part 1) compare each floor against an **authored, per-mix value** — but those authored fields had no declaration site (consumed-by-the-gate, produced-nowhere: round-26 flagged it for `worldResponseCueGainDb`; round-28 systems NEW-3 found it is 4 fields, not 1). They are declared here (and in `entities.yaml`) so the gate has a real field to read and the C.12 completeness hook can confirm every floor-gated field has a declaration site.

| Authored field | Domain / unit | Gated by (floor) | Gate AC | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sprintPulseExertionHoldVolume` | Sound.Volume scalar (0–1) | `>= SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_VOLUME_FLOOR` (0.25) | H.57a Part 1 | Authored playback volume of the **hold** exertion variant (stationary hold-floor pulse, 0.09). The floor cannot be silently undercut by a mix pass. |
| `sprintPulseExertionFullVolume` | Sound.Volume scalar (0–1) | `>= SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_VOLUME_FLOOR` (0.25) AND `(full − hold) dB >= SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_MIN_DELTA_DB` (4.0) | H.57a Part 1 | Authored playback volume of the **full** exertion variant (moving/grace pulse, 0.10). The full−hold delta is the gated quantity for the gradient-perceptibility floor. |
| `walkFootstepVolume` | Sound.Volume scalar (0–1) | `>= WALK_FOOTSTEP_VOLUME_FLOOR` (0.20) | H.57a Part 1 | Authored walk-footstep playback volume — the squad-proximity-awareness cue a cosmetic timbre swap must not drive inaudible. |
| `worldResponseCueGainDb` | dB above the named Hunt-tier ambient bed | `>= WORLD_RESPONSE_CUE_VOLUME_FLOOR` (4.0 dB) | H.78a Part 1 | Authored swell level of the R17-1 world-response cue (the in-run loop-closer). dB-above-ambient, NOT a 0–1 scalar, so it is domain-consistent with the floor; governs the sustained portion of the swell (V/A.3 R23-1(b)). **Onset detectability (the 50–80 ms transient) is NOT gated by this floor — it is separately confirmed by H.78a Part 2(d) (advisory mix-review), per the R23-1(b) onset-vs-sustain scope; a mix engineer owes both the floor AND the Part 2(d) onset check.** |

All four are **first-pass design intent, art-bible-calibrated, NOT playtest-validated** (G.8.1); the H.57a Part 2 / H.78a Part 2 mix-review evidence items confirm they are perceptually sufficient on iPhone SE-class hardware. None is client-exposed (G.8.4 — all values live in server-side config).

## Visual/Audio Requirements

Player Controller is in the "Animation / character movement" category — Visual/Audio direction is mandatory at the GDD stage. All requirements respect the **cosmetic-boundary rule**: no cosmetic may change inputs the predator AI consumes (audio emission volume / radius, light emission, motion signature, spatial signature) or inputs the player reads to make survival decisions. Detailed visual specs (palettes, exact pixel sizes) live in `design/art/art-bible.md` and the asset specs that follow per-system `/asset-spec` runs.

### V/A.1 — Animation Requirements

| Animation | Source | MVP? | Specs |
|---|---|---|---|
| Walk loop | Roblox Humanoid default | v2 candidate (override only if testing reveals it conflicts with the "fragile body" read) | n/a |
| Sprint loop | Custom AnimationTrack | **MVP** | Reads as effortful, not heroic — leaning forward, slightly labored. Single loop, ~0.4 s cycle. Mobile 30 fps: no sub-100 ms keyframe intervals. |
| Walk → Sprint blend | Custom cross-fade | **MVP** | Blend-in 0.15 s. Immediate feel communicates the deliberateness of the transaction. |
| Sprint → Walk blend | Custom + forced-walk variant | **MVP** | Standard blend = 0.15 s. **Forced-walk variant** (T2 from stamina = 0): 0.25 s stumble hold frame before settling into walk cycle — communicates depletion visually. |
| Jump | Roblox Humanoid default | v2 candidate | Use default unless playtest flags it as breaking the "fragile body" read. |
| Lantern raise | Custom one-shot, upper-body layer | **MVP** | ~0.3 s. Slightly slower than lower — deliberate weight signal. |
| Lantern lower | Custom one-shot, upper-body layer | **MVP** | ~0.25 s. |
| Emote slot animations (×6) | Custom full-body one-shots | **MVP** (all 6) | 1.5–2.5 s each. Asset count: 6 tracks. Blend-out 0.3 s to idle. Pillar-2 critical. |
| Death | Custom one-shot collapse | **MVP** | ~0.8 s fall-collapse pose. Body stays as a static frozen pose until respawn or run-end (Roblox ragdoll is unreliable cross-platform). No predator-consume animation at MVP. |
| Respawn | Fade-in via `LocalTransparencyModifier` | **MVP** | 0.5 s opacity ramp. No spawn-pop. Standard idle resumes. |

### V/A.2 — VFX Requirements

| Effect | MVP? | Specs |
|---|---|---|
| Sprint footstep dust | **No** — v2 candidate | Terrain-aware dust requires surface-material detection; cost exceeds value at MVP. |
| Lantern raise/lower brightness ramp | **MVP** | `PointLight.Brightness` lerp matching the animation: raise 0→full over 0.3 s, lower full→0 over 0.25 s. **Property tween, not a particle asset.** |
| Quick-ping marker | **MVP** | Screen-space (pending art-bible Decision B finalisation): 8 px filled circle + 1 px stroke. Own-player ping = white fill + squad-colored stroke; remote pings use category color (Danger violet, Resource teal, Navigate yellow, Untagged white). Opacity 1.0 → 0.0 linear over `PING_DISPLAY_DURATION = 8 s`. **No pulse animation** (motion-reduction compliance). |
| Emote on-screen indicator | **MVP** | World-space billboard above character head; squad-color stroke + white fill + emote icon. Visible 2.5 s, fade 0.5 s. Max 1 active per player. **S4 suppression (round-24 UX-B2 — fanout from C.5.4 item 2 / H.80): the world-space billboard is SUPPRESSED while the emitting player is in S4 (Dead-Respawning)** — a dead player's emote renders ONLY in the squad roster / spectator overlay, never as a world-space billboard over the corpse (a corpse-located billboard is the dead-position tell R17-3 cut S4 ping for). Re-enable gated on the Camera GDD dead-spectator distinguishability work (OQ.3, F.4). **Render-routing is driven by the server-minted `renderScope` field on `OnEmoteBroadcast` (round-28 T-1/T-2, C.9): HUD renders the world-space billboard ONLY when `renderScope == "world"` and suppresses it (squad-UI/spectator-overlay only) when `renderScope == "squad-ui"` — the dead-state determination is server-authoritative, not a client-side check of the emitter's state.** Build the S4 render-suppression from the `renderScope` discriminator + C.5.4 item 2 + H.80, not from this row's default "max 1 per player" alone. |
| Death VFX (own player) | **MVP** | Screen-edge vignette darkens to black over 1.5 s. **No particle burst, no full-screen flash** — photosensitive-safe. |
| Respawn VFX | **MVP** | Inverse vignette: lifts from black over 0.5 s. No flash. |
| Sprint/Light pulse-fired indicator on the player character | **No** | The disturbance HUD bar is the teaching signal. Player-character world-space VFX would create visual noise readable by other players and conflicts with the cosmetic-boundary rule if skinnable. |

### V/A.3 — Audio Cues

All in-world positional 3D audio uses Roblox `SoundService` with `RollOffMode = InverseTapered`. UI-channel sounds (ping, emote) are **non-positional** per game-concept's anti-pillar (emote audio must never be positional 3D audio that the predator AI could consume).

**Audio-perception boundary (Pillar 1 fidelity note)**: The predator AI's only perception inputs from the player controller are the discrete mechanical contracts emitted by `DisturbanceService:Emit()` on the server: `SprintEmission` (fired per `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL = 2 s` while sprint is authorized, magnitude 0.10) and `LightEmission` (fired per `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL = 4 s` while lantern raised, magnitude 0.08). No other signal from this controller crosses the predator-perception channel. **Footstep audio — both walk and sprint — is positional 3D feedback for player-to-player perception (own player + nearby squad members) only.** Neither walk nor sprint footstep audio is consumed by the predator AI; neither affects disturbance accumulation; neither carries any information about the player's detection risk. The predator does not "hear" walking any more than it "hears" sprinting — both are aural-feedback channels, never perception channels (OQ.1 closed Theme 1). The audio design for sprint footsteps must therefore communicate **effort and physical cost** (the body working hard), not **broadcast volume** (predator-detection radius). A louder or faster sound design that reads as "I am broadcasting danger" would train players to conflate audio volume with mechanical risk — a mental model that is wrong for this game and breaks Pillar 1 ("quiet is the only easy mode") by implying noisy sprint is loud-and-detectable rather than mechanically costly-and-discrete. Cadence is locked to approximate the 2 s pulse interval to the player's ear, reinforcing the sprint-as-transaction feel. Walk footsteps remain low-volume aural feedback **on the same player-feedback-only side of the audio-perception boundary defined above** — i.e., walk footstep audio, like sprint footstep audio, is never an input the predator AI consumes. Cosmetic skins may alter timbre only — see V/A.6.

| Cue | Channel | MVP? | Specs |
|---|---|---|---|
| Walk footstep | Positional 3D | MVP (uses Roblox default if no custom asset) | Low volume; aural feedback for own player + squad proximity awareness. **Walk footstep audio is NOT a predator-perception input** (OQ.1 closed, Theme 1) — predator consumes only the discrete `DisturbanceService:Emit()` mechanical contracts (`SprintEmission`, `LightEmission`); positional walk audio is player-feedback only. **Volume floor (audio F3)**: walk footstep playback volume MUST remain at or above `WALK_FOOTSTEP_VOLUME_FLOOR` (a non-zero audible minimum, art-bible-calibrated against the squad-proximity-awareness read) — a cosmetic timbre swap MUST NOT drive walk audio below this floor (an inaudible walk would silently remove the squad's "where is my teammate" cue, a player-information regression). Cosmetic skins MUST NOT alter volume, emission radius, or cadence; timbre is the only permitted cosmetic dimension. The mechanical-equivalence test is therefore NOT vacuous post-OQ.1: it verifies (a) disturbance generation unchanged, (b) movement speed unchanged, AND (c) playback volume ≥ `WALK_FOOTSTEP_VOLUME_FLOOR` and emission radius unchanged. |
| Sprint footstep | Positional 3D | **MVP** (custom asset) | Distinct timbre from walk — heavier texture, slightly breathless quality communicating physical effort. Volume at source: +3–4 dB above walk footstep maximum (effort cue, **not** broadcast cue). **Mental-model note (round-16 audio)**: +3–4 dB louder *to the player* does NOT mean louder *to the predator* — the predator hears only the discrete `Emit` contracts (boundary note above), whose magnitude gradient is deliberately near-flat (0.08/0.09/0.10); what is mechanically loud about sprint is its pulse RATE (Section B round-16 prose reconciliation). Mix reviews MUST keep the footstep delta reading as exertion, never as a danger-radius cue — the +3–4 dB is the single most likely surface to re-teach the wrong model. Cadence locked to approximate the `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL` window; cadence is the mechanical transaction-pacing signal to the player's ear, not a volume escalation. The formal disturbance signal is `SprintEmission` (discrete mechanical contract, server-fired); this audio is redundant aural feedback for the sprinting player and nearby squad only — **not a predator-perception input** (OQ.1 closed, Theme 1). Cosmetic skins MUST NOT alter volume, emission radius, or cadence; timbre is the only permitted cosmetic dimension. |
| Sprint pulse exertion layer | **UI channel only — NOT positional, emitting player only** | **MVP** | **Pulse-coincident floor-feedback cue (R12-game-B2 / R12-AD-I2 — the [R13-F1] gradient's player-perceivable channel)**: each Sprint emission pulse (every `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL = 2 s` while S2 is active, C.8.1) plays a subtle exertion/breath layer to the **emitting player only**, on the fixed UI/non-positional channel per the established emote-audio precedent (never positional 3D — nothing here may cross the audio-perception boundary or be readable by other players). Two intensity variants keyed to the pulse magnitude branch: **full variant** on a moving/grace pulse (0.10) and a **reduced-intensity variant** on a stationary hold-floor pulse (0.09) — so the player can *hear* that stationary sprint still publishes, and hear that it publishes slightly less than moving. **Transport (round-16 B1 — the cue is now buildable)**: each pulse's cue is driven by the **`OnSprintPulse {variant: "full" | "hold"}`** S→C event (C.9, owning client only) — the SERVER evaluates the variant from the D.4 branch and the client only renders it; the client never re-derives the branch (H.57). Caption-mode equivalent in V/A.4 (the gradient's accessibility floor). This is the feedback channel that makes the moving(0.10)-vs-holding(0.09) gradient perceivable (stamina drain and footstep cadence are identical in both cases). Communicates effort/cost per the V/A.3 boundary note, not broadcast volume. **Audibility floor + minimum gradient (round-18 R17-2, user-ruled — "subtle" is bounded below so the gradient can never collapse to zero in the mix; AD-1 closure)**: BOTH variants MUST play at or above `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_VOLUME_FLOOR` (a non-zero audible minimum on the UI channel, art-bible-calibrated; G.9, entities.yaml) — a mix can never render the exertion cue inaudible, which would silently delete the gradient's only audio channel; AND the **full** variant (0.10) MUST be at least `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_MIN_DELTA_DB` (now 4.0 dB, R17-2) louder than the **hold** variant (0.09), so the moving-vs-holding difference stays perceivable rather than collapsing into one indistinguishable cue. **Timbre/rhythm differentiator (R17-2, user-ruled 2026-06-14 — the masking-robust second channel; closes round-19 audio-AD-B2/AD-B1):** the two variants MUST ALSO differ on a non-loudness axis so the gradient survives iPhone-SE ambient/speaker masking (where a same-timbre dB step alone is unreliable below 4–6 dB). The **full** variant carries a fuller-bodied/two-part exertion texture (e.g. a breath with a faint footfall-coincident attack); the **hold** variant is a single thinner sustained breath — so a player reads moving-vs-holding by *texture and rhythm*, not by volume alone. This is the reason `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_MIN_DELTA_DB` need not be pushed into alarm territory: the timbre axis carries the gradient when masking eats the dB step. The differentiator is still own-player UI-channel only (never positional, never a predator-perception input). H.57a Part 2 verifies BOTH the dB delta AND the timbre/rhythm distinction are perceptible on the baseline device against the defined ambient baseline. The delta is an *exertion* difference between own-player UI cues, NOT a danger-radius escalation (it never crosses the audio-perception boundary; the predator hears only the discrete `Emit` contracts per the V/A.3 boundary note). Config-validation rejects a config whose hold-variant volume is below the floor or whose full−hold delta is below the minimum; the mix-review evidence item confirms both are perceptually true on the lowest-tier mobile speaker (H.57a). Cosmetic: not skinnable (it is a mechanical-feedback cue, not a vanity surface). |
| World-response cue (R17-1 in-run feedback loop) | **UI / ambient channel only — NOT positional, living squad** (rendered to living members only per C.9 `OnWorldResponseCue`) | **MVP** | **The in-run causal-loop closer (R17-1, user-ruled 2026-06-14 — closes round-19 #2: game-B1/B2 + ux-B3 + audio-AD-B3/I5).** The exertion layer above teaches the player *"I am working hard"* (own body); it does NOT teach *"my noise reached the world."* Without a second cue, the only in-run feedback that loud play has a consequence is **death** — and at the 2-player floor a lone survivor's sole learning signal becomes the run ending. This cue closes the loop: when the squad's loud play pushes the **Ecological Disturbance level across an escalation band** (an ED-owned threshold event, NOT a per-pulse event), a brief, **non-positional, non-directional** world-response plays to all living squad members — a low ambient "the world stirred / registered you" swell (audio) with a caption-mode equivalent (V/A.4). **It is explicitly NOT a predator tell and respects V/A.3's anti-pillar in full: it carries NO direction, NO distance, NO predator position — it says *"your noise raised the heat,"* never *"the predator is over there."*** That is exactly what preserves quiet-beats-loud uncertainty while still making loud→consequence perceptible in-run. **Cross-system ownership (forward obligation, F.4):** the band-crossing *event* is raised by Ecological Disturbance (it owns the disturbance level and its tier thresholds, ED.D/ED.G); PC/HUD render the non-positional cue. PC's role is to (a) name this cue as the R17-1 loop-closer and (b) carry the F.4 obligation that ED expose a non-positional `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed`-class signal and HUD/PC render it — it is NOT a new PC perception path. **Frequency discipline:** fires only on an **upward** band *crossing* (debounced with hysteresis so a value oscillating on a boundary does not machine-gun the cue — the same hysteresis class ED already owns for its tier transitions); never per emission pulse, never on a downward/de-escalation crossing. Cosmetic: not skinnable (survival-information surface). **Onboarding still owed:** even with this cue, the first-time teaching that *quiet is the easy mode* is reinforced out-of-run by the onboarding teaching beat (F.4 / OQ) — the cue closes the in-run loop; onboarding closes the first-contact gap.<br>**Buildable spec (round-22 R21-1 — closes round-21 #1b "unbuildable timbre/duration/onset/channel"):**<br>• **Transport:** the S→C `OnWorldResponseCue {newTier}` render event (C.9, living squad only), fired by the server on receipt of ED's `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed(direction="up")` inbound signal. The client renders on receipt; it never re-derives the trigger.<br>• **Channel:** the non-positional UI/ambient bus (the same bus class as the exertion layer — never `SoundService` positional 3D, never a predator-perception input).<br>• **Duration / envelope (round-24 R23-1(b) — onset/floor reconciled, user-confirmed 2026-06-15):** a single swell of **~2.0 s** total with a **fast 50–80 ms initial transient** (a quick but soft onset — fast enough to clear the Hunt-tier ambient masking floor at the moment of *first detection* on iPhone-SE-class hardware, but NOT a hard percussive alarm transient that would read as a predator tell), then a **gentle rise into a sustained swell, then a gentle decay to silence**. **The audibility floor `WORLD_RESPONSE_CUE_VOLUME_FLOOR` is scoped to the SUSTAINED portion** of the swell — the prior "soft ~0.3 s attack" read sub-floor at first-detection under Hunt-tier masking (audio AD-R23-B2); the 50–80 ms transient resolves the onset-vs-floor conflict by making the cue detectable at onset while the floor still governs the body. One-shot; it does not loop and does not stack (a second up-crossing within the swell re-triggers at most one fresh swell, never layered).<br>• **Timbre:** a **low-frequency ambient "the world inhaled / registered you" tonal bed** — deliberately distinct from (a) the own-player exertion *breath* (which is a per-pulse body cue) and (b) any predator/danger timbre (the predator's sonic identity is reserved per V/A.8 — the world-response cue must never borrow it). Tonal/atmospheric, NOT percussive, NOT vocal.<br>• **Audibility floor:** the swell's **sustained** level (round-24 R23-1(b) — floor scoped to sustain, not the 50–80 ms onset transient) is bounded below by `WORLD_RESPONSE_CUE_VOLUME_FLOOR` — a **+4.0 dB-above-the-Hunt-tier-ambient-bed** floor (G.9, `entities.yaml`; R21-3, user-ruled). The per-mix authored swell level lives in the **`worldResponseCueGainDb`** audio-config field (dB above the named Hunt-tier ambient bed; H.78a Part 1 compares it against the floor) — so a mix pass can never sink the cue beneath the (louder, escalated) ambient it plays over; it is a dB-above-ambient floor rather than a fixed scalar precisely because the cue plays at higher disturbance tiers where ambient is loudest. H.78a verifies the floor (config gate) + perceptibility against the defined Hunt-tier baseline (mix-review isolate test).<br>• **Tier scaling (optional, HUD/PC):** the `newTier` payload MAY scale swell intensity upward at higher tiers, but the floor and the non-locating character hold at every tier. |
| Jump grunt | — | **No** — skip MVP | No survival-decision information. |
| Lantern raise SFX | UI-adjacent (attached to character, not fully positional) | **MVP** | Short mechanical click/whirr, ~0.3 s. **Fires on the T3 (raise) state transition ONLY — NOT per `LightEmission` pulse** (R6-I9): a raised lantern emits one Light pulse per `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL`, but those pulses are silent to this SFX channel. |
| Lantern lower SFX | UI-adjacent | **MVP** | Softer click, ~0.2 s. **Fires on the T4 (lower) state transition ONLY — NOT per pulse** (R6-I9). **Path B suppression** (R6-I7): when T4 is forced by a disconnect (C.5.3 Path B), the player's character is being destroyed — this SFX MUST be suppressed (no character-attached sound on a destroyed/destroying character). |
| Death SFX | Positional 3D (own + nearby remote) | **MVP** | Non-vocal: low percussive impact + brief resonant tone. **No scream** (non-diegetic; conflicts with biological/organic predator sonic identity which must remain unique). ~1.0 s. **Emitter-attachment strategy — Path A vs Path B (CONV-3c, round-9)**: the remote Death SFX (heard by *nearby squad*) uses one of two emitter bindings, chosen by `pathOrigin`:<br>— **Path A (player present at death)**: the character still exists, so the remote cue is **parented to the dying character's `HumanoidRootPart`** (normal positional 3D, follows the collapse animation). The **own-player** Death SFX also plays (Path A only).<br>— **Path B (disconnect-induced) and any reconciliation path**: the character may already be destroyed when the cue would play, so the cue plays from a **world-anchored, character-detached emitter** at `deathContext.lastKnownPosition` (snapshotted before destruction; NOT parented to the gone character; exact audio-API binding deferred to Theme 2 per the post-cutoff API-verification flag).
