The own-player Death SFX does not apply on Path B.<br>**Nil-position guard**: if `deathContext.lastKnownPosition` resolved `nil` (no `HumanoidRootPart` and no cached server position — the C.5.3 nil-coalesce produced nil), the **positional remote Death SFX is SUPPRESSED** rather than played at the world origin `(0,0,0)` — a Death SFX erupting from map origin would be a worse artifact than silence. (This is the only case the Death SFX is silent on a `"died"`/`"disconnect-while-damaged"` cause.) **This silence is intentional, not a gap (R10-4 / R11-D5 — Pillar 3, "the world watches / the squad notices the absence")**: a teammate vanishing without a placeable death sound is itself information — the squad registers the absence. **Caption parity is preserved**: the non-positional "[Name] is down" caption (V/A.4) STILL fires for this death even though the positional SFX was suppressed, so caption-mode and audio-mode players both learn the teammate is down — only the *spatial* audio cue is withheld, never the *fact*. **Death SFX polyphony cap (R11-D4 / AD-1)**: at most `DEATH_SFX_MAX_VOICES` (**G.6-registered, default 2, range 2–4** — floor raised 1→2 per the round-14 audio ruling, applied round-16 at all 3 sites; also in `entities.yaml` per R12-AD-B2) concurrent positional Death SFX emitters play in a death burst; deaths beyond the cap drop their positional voice (the V/A.4 caption queue still shows them in death-event-timestamp order, deduped by `deathEventId`). **Near-coincident-voice comb-filter mitigation (round-16 audio)**: when two positional Death SFX voices play near-simultaneously (the 2-player wipe case the floor-2 protects), the second voice applies a slight per-voice pitch offset (±2–3%) and start-time stagger (~30–60 ms) so the two identical samples do not comb-filter/phase-cancel into one smeared impact — the two deaths must remain countable by ear. **Overflow parity cue (R12-AD-B2 — closes the accessibility-parity inversion)**: overflow deaths are NOT silent to hearing players — they coalesce into a single **non-positional UI-channel squad-wide death cue** (the "Squad death overflow cue" row below): **one cue per overflow batch, not one per overflow death**, fired when a burst first exceeds the cap. Hearing players thus get at-least-parity with the caption queue (which lists every death individually); the overflow cue says "more of the squad went down than the mix can place," and the caption queue carries the names. This prevents the inversion where caption-mode players received full info while hearing players got silence beyond voice 2. **`reconciliation-recovery` suppression (R6-B12 — load-bearing for the horror audio-as-feedback contract)**: the Death SFX MUST fire on `deathCause ∈ {"died", "disconnect-while-damaged"}` only, and MUST NOT fire on `deathCause="reconciliation-recovery"`. The reconciliation broadcast can arrive up to 10 s after the player actually vanished (the deduction-retry delay); playing a Death SFX at that moment would plant a false death cue 10 s late, training the squad to misread predator activity (a fresh kill that did not happen). The delayed `reconciliation-recovery` event is a **HUD + UI-chime** notification (see F.4 HUD row) — **no positional audio, no caption (V/A.4)**; its only audio treatment is the non-positional UI-channel notification chime (R12-AD-B3 — the "Reconciliation-recovery chime" row below), which fires coincident with the HUD roster-confirm indication and can never be mistaken for a placeable fresh-kill cue. |
| Squad death overflow cue | **UI channel only — NOT positional, squad-wide** | **MVP** | **(R12-AD-B2)** Single coalesced cue fired once per overflow batch when a death burst exceeds `DEATH_SFX_MAX_VOICES` (see Death SFX row). **Batch boundary (round-16 audio — previously undefined)**: a "batch" is the set of overflow deaths whose `deathTimestamp`s fall within `DEATH_SFX_BATCH_WINDOW = 0.25 s` (G.6; range 0.1–0.5 s) of the batch's first overflow death; an overflow death past the window starts a new batch (and a new single cue). Short, somber, non-vocal, clearly distinct from the individual Death SFX timbre — reads as "multiple squad members down," not as one more placeable death. Non-positional by design: the overflowed deaths' positions are already unrenderable in the mix; a UI-channel cue gives hearing players the *fact* at parity with the caption queue without inventing false spatial information. One cue per batch, never stacked. |
| Reconciliation-recovery chime | **UI channel only — NOT positional** | **MVP** | **(R12-AD-B3)** Fires on `OnPlayerDied(deathCause="reconciliation-recovery")` — the delayed (≤ 10 s nominal) reconciliation broadcast — **coincident with the HUD roster-confirm indication** (F.4 HUD row). Soft, neutral notification chime: a status-settle sound, NOT an alarm and NOT the Death SFX (the world-anchored positional Death SFX remains suppressed on this cause per R6-B12, which round-7 already locked). This fills the previously-unspecified audio treatment of the reconciliation-recovery window: hearing players now get a non-alarming audio confirmation that the roster ambiguity resolved, with zero risk of misreading it as a fresh kill (non-positional, distinct timbre, paired with the HUD-only visual class). |
| Respawn SFX | UI-adjacent | **MVP** | Soft ambient inhale-analog tone, ~0.5 s. Non-vocal. **Triggered on the T6 respawn event ONLY — never on any `OnPlayerDied` cause (audio F2)**: a delayed `reconciliation-recovery` broadcast can arrive within ~10 s of a teammate's actual respawn, and tying any respawn audio to a death broadcast would compress the death→respawn timeline into a confusing cluster. Respawn SFX is gated solely on the player's own T6 transition; it is independent of the death-broadcast channel. |
| Quick-ping audio | **UI channel only — NOT positional** | **MVP** | Short neutral tone (~200 ms). **Same sound for all ping categories** — category is distinguished visually only. **The uniform tone is INTENTIONAL — zero urgency encoding (round-16 audio, documented as a design choice)**: encoding category/urgency in the tone would (a) make audio a survival-information channel owed at caption parity, and (b) train players to react to sound over the visual marker; the tone says only "a teammate marked something," and the marker carries all meaning. Any future proposal to differentiate ping audio must re-open this row, not just add assets. |
| Emote audio | **UI channel only — NOT positional** | **MVP** | Brief acknowledgment tone per emote type; non-vocal. |

### V/A.4 — Captions Mode Visual Equivalents

When the player enables captions mode (per game-concept accessibility floor), the following indicators appear in a `CaptionsOverlay` ScreenGui (top-left band, 1-line-at-a-time display):

| Audio cue | Captions equivalent |
|---|---|
| Walk footstep cadence | Animated footstep icon cycling at walk tempo, beside nearest squad member's nameplate |
| Sprint footstep cadence | Distinct sprint-stride icon (heavier footstep shape, wider than walk icon) at walk-equivalent cycle speed + squad-color highlight stripe. **The stride icon shape communicates sprint body-state (effort), not broadcast rate** — cycle speed is NOT increased. (Parallels V/A.3 design note: caption players must build the same effort-vs-broadcast mental model as hearing players.) |
| Sprint pulse exertion layer (round-16 B1 — caption parity for the [R13-F1] gradient) | Per `OnSprintPulse` (C.9), a brief pulse tick on the sprint-stride icon, own-player only: **`variant="full"` renders a FILLED pulse tick** (moving/grace pulse, 0.10); **`variant="hold"` renders a HOLLOW/reduced pulse tick** (stationary hold-floor pulse, 0.09). Caption-mode players MUST be able to perceive the moving-vs-holding gradient — this row is the gradient's sole non-audio channel (Pillar 1 accessibility floor; H.57). Static-show under motion-reduction (V/A.5) — the tick swaps state, never animates. |
| World-response cue (R17-1) | Non-positional caption line "The world stirs" (or art-bible-final wording) in a **neutral non-danger color — sharpened to a testable spec (round-28 ux gate, UX-R26-1): a desaturated amber-white / cool light-grey that achieves ≥ 3:1 contrast against the dark caption strip (WCAG 1.4.11 non-text/graphical contrast; the text targets ≥ 4.5:1 against its background) AND whose hue does NOT map to the danger (red/orange) or safe (green) category under a deuteranopia/protanopia simulation (exact value = art-bible final)**, ~2 s, fired on the same ED band-crossing event — **no direction/distance text** (parity with the audio cue's non-locating design). **Shape/icon redundancy (round-24 UX-B1 — game-concept accessibility floor: color is NEVER the sole channel):** the caption line is accompanied by a **non-directional "world-stir" glyph** (a concentric-ring / ripple icon — deliberately NOT an arrow, compass, heading, or any directional/positional shape, so it cannot read as a locating tell), so a player who cannot distinguish the neutral-non-danger color still reads the cue by shape. The optional `newTier` scaling alters the glyph in **intensity only** (e.g. ring count/weight), never in direction. This is the caption-mode channel for the R17-1 in-run loop; caption players must learn loud→consequence at parity with hearing players. Static-show under motion-reduction (V/A.5). Verified by H.79. |
| Lantern raise SFX | "Lantern raised" text badge with lantern icon, 1.5 s |
| Lantern lower SFX | "Lantern lowered" text badge, 1.5 s. **Path-B suppression parity (round-16 audio)**: when T4 is forced by a Path-B disconnect, V/A.3 suppresses the lower SFX (destroyed character) — the caption badge is suppressed too, so caption-mode players get the same silence as hearing players; the departure itself is conveyed by the "[Name] is down" caption, never by a stray "Lantern lowered" badge for a vanished teammate. |
| Death SFX (own player) | Vignette already serves; no additional caption needed |
| Death SFX (remote) | "[Name] is down" text in squad color, 3 s. **Fires on `deathCause ∈ {"died", "disconnect-while-damaged"}` only — suppressed on `deathCause="reconciliation-recovery"` (R6-I10)**: the delayed reconciliation broadcast (up to 10 s late) read out of context as a fresh "[Name] is down" trains caption players to misread predator activity, exactly as the Death SFX would for hearing players (V/A.3 R6-B12). The `reconciliation-recovery` delayed notification is HUD + non-positional UI chime (V/A.3, R12-AD-B3) — caption parity with the *positional/alarm* audio channel is preserved by both the caption and the Death SFX suppressing; the chime is a non-alarming status-settle cue, not a death announcement, so no caption equivalent is owed beyond the HUD roster-confirm itself. **Nil-position caption parity (R11-D5 / AD-2)**: the "[Name] is down" caption fires on `{"died", "disconnect-while-damaged"}` **regardless of whether the positional remote Death SFX was suppressed for a `nil` `lastKnownPosition`** (V/A.3 nil-position guard). The caption is a non-positional `CaptionsOverlay` line, so a missing world position has no bearing on it — the squad still gets the "[Name] is down" notice even when the *audio* could not be placed in the world. The two suppressions are distinct: `reconciliation-recovery` suppresses caption AND SFX (delayed-broadcast misread); `nil-position` suppresses ONLY the positional SFX, never the caption. **Multi-death ordering/dedup**: subject to the V/A.4a caption-queue rule below. |
| Respawn SFX | "Respawn" text badge, 1 s |
| Quick-ping audio | Ping marker is already visual-primary; no caption |
| Emote audio | Emote icon is already visual-primary; no caption |

### V/A.4a — Multi-Death Caption Queue (R11-D4 / AD-1)

When two or more teammates go down close together (a predator sweep, a coordinated wipe, or several `reconciliation-recovery` broadcasts landing near each other), the single-line `CaptionsOverlay` (V/A.4 header: 1-line-at-a-time) needs a deterministic ordering and de-duplication rule so captions neither stack illegibly nor drop a death silently.

- **Order = death-event timestamp (ascending).** Captions are enqueued and shown in order of the `deathTimestamp` carried by each `OnPlayerDied` payload (**produced once per death event at death-time via the C.11 `getServerTime()` seam — C.5.3 round-16 B2, H.58**; earliest death shown first), NOT in network-arrival order — so a reconciliation-recovery that arrives late but carries an earlier timestamp does not jump ahead of a more-recent fresh death. (Moot for `reconciliation-recovery` itself, which is caption-suppressed, but the ordering rule governs the `{"died","disconnect-while-damaged"}` captions that DO show.)
- **Explicit hold-time.** Each "[Name] is down" caption holds for **3 s** (the V/A.4 row value), shown one at a time; the next queued caption begins when the current one's hold expires. Under a burst, total on-screen time is `queueDepth × 3 s` — bounded because the squad is at most 4.
- **Dedup by `deathEventId`.** A caption is enqueued **at most once per `deathEventId`.** A replayed `Humanoid.Died` (C.5.2), a `PlayerRemoving` no-op against an already-`Committed` record, or a `reconciliation-recovery` for a death whose original `{"died","disconnect-while-damaged"}` caption already showed MUST NOT enqueue a second caption for the same `deathEventId`. (This is the caption-side mirror of the C.5.3 single-charge-per-death-event guarantee — exactly one caption per death event, never one-per-broadcast.)
- **Overflow → caption + coalesced UI cue (polyphony interplay, amended R12-AD-B2).** When the squad-wide Death SFX polyphony cap (`DEATH_SFX_MAX_VOICES`, V/A.3) is exceeded in a burst, the deaths beyond the cap drop their **positional** voice but are NOT audio-silent: the overflow batch fires the single non-positional squad-wide death cue (V/A.3 "Squad death overflow cue" — one cue per batch, not per death), and the queued "[Name] is down" caption still shows for every overflow death. Caption legibility is the floor: a player must always be able to read who went down even when the audio mix sheds voices — and hearing players now keep at-least-parity with that floor via the overflow cue.
- **Motion-reduction / photosensitive**: the queue uses the same static-show / instant-advance treatment as the rest of V/A.4 under the motion-reduction toggle (V/A.5) — no animated slide between queued captions.

### V/A.5 — Photosensitive / Motion-Reduction Compliance

**WCAG 2.3.1 (max 3 flashes/sec; no full-screen flashes)**: every effect introduced by Player Controller is compliant by design — death vignette is single-direction fade (not a flash); respawn vignette inverse-fade; ping opacity is a static decay; emote indicator is a fade-out; lantern PointLight ramp is a gradual lerp. **No full-screen flashes exist in this spec.**

**Motion-reduction toggle must disable or substitute**:

- Emote billboard fade → instant-show / instant-hide at toggle boundary frames
- Ping marker opacity decay → static display + instant-remove at 8 s
- Captions-mode footstep icon cycling → static footstep icon
- Sprint footstep VFX (when introduced in v2) → static dust decal
- World-response caption line "The world stirs" (V/A.4, R17-1) → static-show / instant-advance (no animated slide-in); the cue is **audio-led** and has no visual flash, so the motion-reduction substitution is caption-only (round-22 — closes the dangling V/A.4→V/A.5 reference). The audio swell itself is WCAG-compliant by construction (round-24 R23-1(b): a fast 50–80 ms onset transient followed by a gradual rise — the onset is an audio level change only, NOT a visual flash; no full-screen flash, no positional motion). The non-directional world-stir glyph (V/A.4) static-shows under this toggle (no animated ring expansion).

### V/A.6 — Cosmetic-Boundary Compliance Implications

| Asset | Cosmetic-replaceable? | Constraint |
|---|---|---|
| Sprint footstep VFX (v2 only) | YES — shape/color only | Volume / radius / emission rate locked. Mechanical-equivalence test: predator-AI inputs unchanged. |
| Lantern `PointLight` (radius, brightness, edge behaviour) | **NO — locked** | These are survival-decision inputs (Principle 1 — the lit-pool boundary). |
| Lantern raise/lower SFX | YES — timbre/texture only | Duration and volume locked. |
| Sprint/walk footstep SFX | Timbre YES; volume / emission radius / cadence NO | **Volume locked**: sprint footstep must remain at +3–4 dB above walk maximum (effort cue calibrated against walk baseline; altering volume changes the effort-vs-broadcast read). **Emission radius locked**: any radius change would alter the squad proximity-awareness signal (player-information input). **Cadence locked**: cadence approximates the `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL` transaction-pacing signal to the player's ear; altering it would degrade mechanical-contract legibility. Walk footstep audio is NOT a predator-perception input (OQ.1 closed, Theme 1) — cosmetic sign-off does not require predator-AI validation, but mechanical-equivalence test must still confirm disturbance generation unchanged and movement speed unchanged. |
| Sprint pulse exertion layer (incl. `OnSprintPulse` caption tick) | **NO — locked** | Mechanical-feedback cue for the D.4 magnitude branch (round-16): the full/hold variants, their timing, audibility, and the caption tick shapes are survival-information surfaces, not vanity surfaces — skinning them would let a cosmetic blur the moving-vs-holding gradient (V/A.3 row: "not skinnable"). **Round-18 R17-2**: `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_VOLUME_FLOOR` and `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_MIN_DELTA_DB` are locked alongside the variants — no cosmetic (and no mix pass) may drop either variant below the audibility floor or shrink the full−hold delta below the minimum. |
| World-response cue (R17-1) | **NO — locked** | The in-run loop-closer (V/A.3): its trigger (ED band-crossing), its non-positional/non-directional character, audibility, and caption equivalent are survival-information surfaces — a cosmetic (or a mix pass) MUST NOT mute it, make it positional/directional, or re-tie it to per-pulse events. ED owns the band-crossing event; PC/HUD own the rendered cue (F.4). |
| Death collapse animation | YES — pose/style only | Duration locked (0.8 s). No cosmetic may restore health signal. |
| Death SFX | YES — timbre only | Must remain non-vocal, non-musical. |
| Emote animations | YES | No emote may introduce light emission or audio emission above locked baseline. |
| Quick-ping marker shape | YES — decorative skin | Color must remain squad-color-coded; shape ≤ 12 px. Visibility is a survival-decision input. |

### V/A.7 — Art Bible Principle Application

- **Principle 1 — Contrast Is the Map**: Player Controller owns the lantern `PointLight` lifecycle. Hard-edged 12-stud pool toggled strictly on T3/T4 transitions. **Never linger in a partial state. Brightness lerp on raise/lower is permitted because the transition is brief and directional — it does not produce a soft ambient fill.**
- **Principle 2 — Scale Signals Danger Before Color Does**: Player Controller's contribution is to ensure player-character animations do not visually puff up the player's apparent size. Sprint animation leans forward and narrows; emote animations are contained; death collapse compresses the silhouette downward. **No camera FOV widening on sprint** — flagged for the camera/UX layer (Player Controller does not own the camera, but PC's animations must support the small-player read).
- **Principle 3 — Biological Saturation Is the Disturbance Meter**: Player Controller does not author environment saturation. Its contribution is ensuring **no player-controlled visual effect introduces color outside the disturbance-tier palette or touches the predator's reserved hue**. Ping markers, emote indicators, and squad outline colors all defer to the locked palette in `art-bible.md` §7.5.

### V/A.8 — Out of Scope (for clarity — owned elsewhere)

1. **Predator audio cues and perception range** — owned by Predator AI GDD.
2. **Bioluminescent flora animation and disturbance-tier saturation shifts** — owned by Ecological Disturbance GDD's flora subsystem.
3. **HUD rendering** (stamina bar, disturbance bar, oxygen bar visual presentation) — owned by HUD GDD. Player Controller only specifies the *state changes* that drive HUD updates.
4. **Safe-room lighting and environmental ambience** — owned by future Level/Environment GDD.
5. **Run-end / whole-squad death screen layout and animation** — owned by HUD GDD. Player Controller owns only the "your character is gone" feedback (death vignette + collapse animation) before the screen transition.
6. **Camera behaviour during sprint / death / respawn** — owned by future Camera GDD. Player Controller flags the no-FOV-widening-on-sprint constraint here as an obligation owed forward.

> **📌 Asset Spec** — Visual/Audio requirements are defined. After the art bible is fully approved, run `/asset-spec system:player-controller` to produce per-asset visual descriptions, dimensions, AI generation prompts, and audio specifications from this section.

## UI Requirements

Player Controller introduces several **input affordances** the player interacts with directly. Pixel-precise rendering specs (radii, fonts, exact dimensions) live in the HUD GDD and the eventual `/ux-design` spec for each screen. This section defines **what UI elements PC needs and the contract those elements consume**.

### U.1 — Input Affordances Owned by Player Controller

| Affordance | Platforms | What it consumes | What it produces |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Sprint button** | Touch only (PC + gamepad have key/button bindings, no on-screen widget) | Server-pushed sprint state (S1 vs S2) for visual on/off rendering | Tap fires `RequestSprintToggle` |
| **Stamina bar** | All platforms | `OnStaminaChanged` (≤ 5 Hz) | Read-only — display |
| **Lantern button** | Touch only | Server-pushed lantern state (S3a vs S3b) | Tap fires `RequestLanternToggle` |
| **Quick-ping affordance** | Gesture (touch two-finger); key (PC `G`); bumper (gamepad `L1/LB`) — **no dedicated touch button** | None (input-only widget) | Fires `RequestPing` |
| **Emote wheel** | All platforms | None (input-only widget); category list of 6 slots | Fires `RequestEmote(slot)` |
| **Gather progress ring** | All platforms (rendered when tap-hold or proximity-gate is active) | Local hold-time elapsed (client-predicted); server `GatherNodeArmed` / `GatherNodeDisarmed` | Fires `RequestGather` on completion |
| **Death screen vignette** | All platforms — own dying player only | `OnPlayerDied` | Read-only |
| **Respawn fade-in** | All platforms — own respawning player only | `OnPlayerRespawned` | Read-only |
| **Captions overlay** | All platforms when captions mode enabled | All audio cues from V/A.4 | Read-only |

### U.2 — Touch UI Layout (per ux-designer recommendation, V/A-aligned)

```
 ____________________________________
|  [Lantern]               [Captions]|  Notch / Dynamic Island free
|                                    |
|                                    |
|   [L-Stick]                        |
|                       [Emote Open] |
|                       [Jump]       |
|   [Sprint]            [Interact /  |
|                        Gather]     |
|____________________________________|
```

**Layout commitments:**

- Lantern button is in the upper-left zone, **physically separated from sprint + stick** to defend against predator-encounter mis-tap (Section E.D pitfall).
- Quick-ping is **gesture-only on touch** (no dedicated button) to preserve right-thumb screen real estate.
- Sprint button is a single 44×44 pt tap target (Apple HIG floor). On tap → toggle sprint.
- Jump is bottom-right, above Interact/Gather, within natural right-thumb arc.
- Emote-wheel-open button is above Interact/Gather; opening locks tap-hold gather per T9.

### U.3 — Cross-Platform Parity

- Every PC keyboard binding has a touch and a gamepad equivalent (per C.4 + V/A.3 + game-concept's "all interactive prompts must work with touch tap, mouse click, and gamepad button").
- **No hover-only interactions** anywhere in this controller's UI.
- All affordances scale via `UIScale` and `UIAspectRatioConstraint` per `current-best-practices.md`.
- Tested on a 16:9 PC, 4:3 emulated, and a tall mobile aspect (e.g., 19.5:9) per Roblox UI guidance.

### U.4 — Input Display State

- **Sprint button** visual state: ON when in S2, OFF when in S1, disabled (grey) when stamina = 0 (server has rejected sprint toggles).
- **Lantern button** visual state: ON when in S3b, OFF when in S3a.
- **Stamina bar** visual state: fill = `stamina / STAMINA_MAX`. When in `STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY` window (S1 with regen suppressed), bar shows a static-no-regen indicator (e.g., greyed-out tip).
- All visual state updates respect motion-reduction + photosensitive rules from V/A.5.

### U.5 — UX Spec Flag

> **📌 UX Flag — Player Controller**: This controller has UI requirements that span the in-run HUD, the emote-wheel screen, and the death/respawn overlay. In Phase 4 (Pre-Production), run `/ux-design` to create a UX spec for each screen or HUD element this system contributes to **before** writing epics. Stories that reference UI should cite the resulting `design/ux/[screen].md`, not this GDD directly. Note this in the systems index for Player Controller when it's updated.

## Acceptance Criteria

All criteria are independently verifiable by a QA tester without reading this GDD. Test types: **AUTO-UNIT** (TestEZ headless), **AUTO-INTEGRATION** (Lemur or Studio multi-client), **MANUAL-PLAYTEST**, **MANUAL-DEVICE** (iPhone SE-class required).

### H.1 — Locomotion

- **H.1** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a `PlayerControllerService` with default config, WHEN `GetMoveSpeed(state)` is called, THEN returns 12 studs/s for `Walk` and 20 studs/s for `Sprint`. (Verifies C.1.1 / C.1.2 walk/sprint speed values.)
- **H.2** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (Logic core, BLOCKING) + MANUAL-PLAYTEST (PC, feel-only ADVISORY) — **(round-20 qa relabel — was MANUAL-only; the displacement is deterministic Logic and must carry an automated gate per coding-standards, not rest on a tester's ruler.)** GIVEN a Lemur headless server with one player at `WALK_SPEED`, WHEN the player holds W for 3 s on flat terrain and releases, THEN the XZ-displacement computed by the D.6 distance function equals `WALK_SPEED × 3 s ± 0.5 studs` AND Y-axis displacement is uncounted (the XZ-only assertion is the Logic gate). The MANUAL-PLAYTEST layer (Studio ruler / `print(HumanoidRootPart.Position)`) is ADVISORY on-device confirmation only. (Verifies D.6 XZ-only distance as an automated Logic gate; the manual run confirms feel.)
- **H.3** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (Logic core, BLOCKING) + MANUAL-PLAYTEST (PC, feel ADVISORY) — **(round-20 qa relabel — was MANUAL-only; timer-independence is deterministic state-machine Logic.)** GIVEN a sprinting player with `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN a jump physics event fires mid-sprint, THEN the Sprint emission interval timer is unaffected — the next pulse fires at the expected `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL = 2 s` boundary from the prior pulse, asserted against the injected clock, regardless of the jump. (Verifies C.1.4 jump-does-not-reset-pulse-timers as an automated Logic gate; manual run is ADVISORY feel confirmation.)

### H.2 — Stamina

- **H.4** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN stamina = 100 and sprint state active, WHEN `TickStamina(dt = 0.01667)` is called once (one Heartbeat at 60 Hz), THEN stamina = `clamp(100 − 12.5 × 0.01667, 0, 100) ≈ 99.792` (within ±0.001). (Verifies D.1 drain formula.)
- **H.5** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN stamina = 50, sprint inactive, and the regen-delay timer expired (`(t − t_sprint_end) > 1.5`), WHEN `TickStamina(dt = 0.01667)` is called, THEN stamina = `clamp(50 + 10 × 0.01667, 0, 100) ≈ 50.167` (within ±0.001). (Verifies D.2 regen formula.)
- **H.6** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN starting stamina `s_0 = 50`, WHEN `T_sprint(s_0)` is computed, THEN result = 4.0 s ± 0.01 s. (Verifies D.3 effective sprint duration.)
- **H.7** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN stamina = 0, WHEN the client sends `RequestSprintToggle(sprint=true)`, THEN the server rejects the request, the player remains in S1, and `OnSprintStateChanged` is NOT broadcast. (Verifies C.2.5 force-walk + sprint-input-ignored at zero stamina.)
- **H.8** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN sprint just exited at server time `t_end` (`getServerTime()` injected per C.11), WHEN simulation advances to `t_end + 1.4 s` and `IsRegenActive()` is queried, THEN returns `false`; advance to **exactly** `t_end + 1.5 s` and re-query, THEN **STILL returns `false`** (D.2 uses strict `>` — at the exact boundary the player is still inside the delay window); advance the injected C.11 clock to `t_end + STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY + ε` for an arbitrary strictly-positive `ε` (e.g. `ε = 0.001 s` — an ε-based assertion, NOT a tick-rate-derived constant like the prior `1.51 s`, R12-qa), and re-query, THEN returns `true`. (Verifies C.2.4 / D.2 regen delay with the strict-`>` boundary semantics from D.2 R6-I1 — regen begins on the first query *strictly past* `t_end + STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY`, NOT at the exact boundary, for ANY positive ε; the injected clock makes the test independent of frame timing. The prior wording asserted `true` at exactly `t_end + 1.5 s`, contradicting D.2's strict `>`; the round-11 fix then hardcoded `1.51 s`, which tied the AC to an implied 100 Hz step.)

### H.3 — Lantern

- **H.9** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player in S2 (sprinting) + S3a (lantern lowered), **`getServerTime()` injected via the C.11 clock seam (round-20 qa clock-pin — both axes run pulse-interval timers off the seam; the AC must control the clock to assert the concurrent-timer state deterministically rather than against wall-clock drift)**, WHEN the player toggles lantern on, THEN locomotion axis remains S2 and lantern axis transitions to S3b, and BOTH axes' pulse-interval timers advance independently against the injected clock. Server state dump shows both states active concurrently. (Verifies C.3.4 orthogonal lantern + sprint axes; clock-pinned per C.11.)
- **H.10** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (Logic core, BLOCKING) + MANUAL-PLAYTEST (PC + Mobile + Gamepad, cross-platform-feel ADVISORY) — **(round-20 qa relabel — was MANUAL-only; the converge-to-final-input behavior is deterministic state-machine Logic and is automatable; only the per-platform input-feel layer is genuinely manual.)** GIVEN lantern in S3b with the same-state debounce + burst cap active (C.9 `RequestLanternToggle`, NET-B1), WHEN toggled S3b → S3a → S3b within 200 ms, THEN the server lantern-axis state converges to the final input with no intermediate phantom emission, asserted via server state dump (the Logic gate). The MANUAL-PLAYTEST layer confirms there is no client/server desync *visible to the player* on PC, Mobile, and Gamepad (ADVISORY — the cross-platform-feel part that cannot be headlessly automated). (Verifies C.3.1 toggle reliability: the convergence Logic is an automated gate; cross-platform feel is the manual layer.)

### H.4 — Disturbance Emission Contract

- **H.11** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a player with `t_lastGracePulse_sprint = nil` (fresh life or post-cooldown) enters S2 at `t_0`, WHEN `GetPulseMagnitude(t = t_0 + 0.5, d_xz = 0.4, platform = "pc")` is called (within `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW = 1.0 s`), THEN returns `MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE = 0.10` (full magnitude — grace-exempt because cooldown is not active and pulse is within grace window), **AND the test asserts the grace-anchor side-effect was written — `t_lastGracePulse_sprint == t_0` after the call (round-28 T-3, qa Miss #1)**. Previously the recording was narrated ("Server records …") but not asserted, so a build that returned 0.10 yet failed to write the anchor would pass H.11 while silently breaking H.11h (which depends on the anchor being set at eligibility resolution). (Verifies D.4 grace-eligible branch + the cooldown-anchor side-effect H.11h relies on.)
- **H.11b** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `t_lastGracePulse_sprint = t_now - 3.0 s` (cooldown active; `GRACE_REENTRY_COOLDOWN = 6.0 s` not yet elapsed), the player enters S2 again, and the first pulse fires within `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW`, WHEN `GetPulseMagnitude(t = t_0 + 0.5, d_xz = 0.4 stud, platform = "pc")` is called for Sprint, THEN `grace_eligible = false`, the formula falls through to the movement-delta branch, the stationary attenuation gives `0.10 × 0.30 = 0.03`, and the **R10-6 Sprint hold-floor raises it to `max(0.09, 0.03) = 0.09`** ([R13-F1]; because 0.4 < EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR). (Verifies G1 cooldown-blocks-grace-exemption AND the Sprint hold-floor on a stationary cooldown-blocked pulse; closes round-1 exploit.)
- **H.11c** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `t_lastGracePulse_sprint = t_now - 3.0 s` (cooldown active), the player enters S2 again moving (`d_xz = 5 stud`), WHEN `GetPulseMagnitude` is called for Sprint, THEN `grace_eligible = false` AND `effective_d = 5 >= EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR`, formula falls through to the moving branch, result = `0.10` (full magnitude). **A moving sprinter pays the correct cost regardless of cooldown** — only the stationary-bypass exploit is closed.
- **H.11d** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player who has fired a Sprint grace-exempt pulse 2 s ago (cooldown active), WHEN `Humanoid.Died` fires (T5), and the player respawns, and immediately enters S2, THEN the first Sprint pulse on the new life is grace-exempt at full magnitude (`t_lastGracePulse_sprint` was reset to `nil` on T5). (Verifies T5 cooldown-reset side-effect; Pillar 4 fairness.)
- **H.11e** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `t_lastGracePulse_sprint = nil` AND `t_lastGracePulse_light = nil` (fresh life), the player enters S2 (sprint) at `t_0`, then S3b (lantern) at `t_0 + 0.5 s` while S2 is still active, WHEN both first-pulses are evaluated, THEN BOTH are grace-exempt: Sprint first pulse = 0.10, Light first pulse = 0.08. **Per-axis independence**: entering Sprint does not consume Light's grace exemption. (Verifies axis independence at the rule level.)
- **H.11f** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `t_lastGracePulse_light = t_now - 3.0 s` (Light-axis cooldown active; `GRACE_REENTRY_COOLDOWN = 6.0 s` not yet elapsed), the player enters S3b again, and the first pulse fires within `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW`, WHEN `GetPulseMagnitude(t = t_0 + 0.5, d_xz = 0.4 stud, platform = "pc")` is called for Light, THEN `grace_eligible = false`, the formula falls through to the movement-delta branch, and result = `0.08 × 0.30 = 0.024` (stationary attenuation, because 0.4 < EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR). (Verifies Light-axis parity with H.11b — closes the round-1 lantern toggle-cycle 10× exploit at the AC level.)
- **H.11g** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `t_lastGracePulse_light = t_now - 3.0 s` (Light-axis cooldown active), the player enters S3b again moving (`d_xz = 5 stud`), WHEN `GetPulseMagnitude` is called for Light, THEN `grace_eligible = false` AND `effective_d = 5 >= EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR`, formula falls through to the moving branch, result = `0.08` (full magnitude). **A moving lantern-toggler pays the correct cost regardless of cooldown** — only the stationary-bypass exploit is closed. (Verifies Light-axis parity with H.11c.)
- **H.11h** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `t_lastGracePulse_sprint = nil` (eligible) and the player enters S2 at `t_0`, but the simulated server delivers the first pulse at `t = t_0 + 1.5 s` (past `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW = 1.0 s`, simulating a thermally-throttled mobile Heartbeat), WHEN the formula evaluates Step 0a, THEN `t_lastGracePulse[Sprint]` is set to `t_0` regardless of whether the grace-window check passes (it does NOT pass — the pulse falls through to the movement-delta branch). A subsequent S2 entry at `t_0 + 5.0 s` (within `GRACE_REENTRY_COOLDOWN = 6.0 s` from the originally-anchored `t_0`) observes `grace_eligible = false`. (Verifies B9 anchor-on-eligibility — the missed-grace-window jitter exploit is closed even when the server fails to deliver the first pulse within the grace window.)
- **H.12** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `t = t_0 + 4.0` (grace expired), `d_xz = 0.5 stud`, `platform = "pc"`, WHEN `GetPulseMagnitude` is called for Sprint, THEN the stationary attenuation gives `0.10 × 0.30 = 0.03` and the **R10-6 Sprint hold-floor returns `max(0.09, 0.03) = 0.09`** ([R13-F1]). (Verifies D.4 stationary branch + the Sprint hold-floor Step 2.) **Paired Light case → extracted to the independent AC H.81 (round-28 T-3, qa Miss #10):** the Light-axis no-floor property — the *security* property that the R10-6 hold-floor is **Sprint-axis-ONLY**, which is what keeps the round-1 lantern-cycle exploit closed — was embedded here as prose, so a CI run could skip it silently. It is now its own testable AC (H.81); this row keeps only the Sprint assertion.
- **H.12a** ([R13-F1] isolated floor-boundary AC — R12-qa) — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a stationary sprint-hold pulse (grace not eligible, `effective_d < EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR`), WHEN `GetPulseMagnitude` is called for Sprint, THEN the emitted magnitude is **exactly `config.SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR`** (round-20 stale-literal fix — the assertion reads the CONFIG value, not the hardcoded literal `0.09`, so a legitimate tune within the `(0.08, 0.10)` band does not falsely fail the test; the surrounding prose's "0.09" is the default, the assertion is `== config.SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR`) — and it is **not** the attenuated `0.03`, not the lantern's `MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE`, not the moving `MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE`. AND GIVEN the config-validation pass, WHEN the G.7 hierarchy invariant is checked, THEN `MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE (0.08) < SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR (0.09) < MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE (0.10)` holds (both comparisons strict), and a config with `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR <= MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE` or `>= MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE` is **rejected** by validation. **(round-28 systems-B3 — the gate compares against the LIVE ED-registry values `MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE`/`MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE`, NOT the hardcoded literals `0.08`/`0.10`: an ED magnitude retune that the G.7 ownership rule permits would otherwise silently invert the hierarchy without tripping this PC gate — the same literal-bake class clause (iv) already dissolved for the grace-cooldown floor; the `(0.08, 0.10)` band in the prose is the value at the current registry magnitudes, not a literal the gate hardcodes.)** **AND (round-18 systems-B2 — H.12a is the single config-validation gate cited by ALL machine-checkable invariants; previously it asserted only the hierarchy, leaving the others cited-but-unverified)** the same config-validation pass also asserts: **(ii) the G.2 drain-floor** — `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE > 0`, and a config with `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE ≤ 0` is **rejected** (D.3 `T_sprint` / C.2.2 time-to-empty divide-by-zero); **(iii) the G.6 [R13-F2] coupled margin** — `LATCH_MAX_AGE > HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW + 30.0` (strict), and a config violating it (e.g. `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW = 60`, `LATCH_MAX_AGE = 90` → margin 0) is **rejected** (closes the degenerate 60/60 Scenario-C corner); **(iv) the E.C/G.6 grace-cooldown floor (round-20 — was a prose-only "QA MUST enforce" in E.C/G.6 with no config-gate AC; round-22 — evaluate the floor EXPRESSION over live config, not the stale literal 4.0)** — `GRACE_REENTRY_COOLDOWN >= max(SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL + STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY, LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL)` **computed over the CURRENT config** (NOT the hardcoded `4.0`, which is only the value of that expression at the *default* `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL=2.0` + `STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY=1.5` / `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL=4.0`), and a config violating it is **rejected** — e.g. if a tuner raises `STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY` to `4.0` the floor becomes `max(2.0+4.0, 4.0) = 6.0`, so a `GRACE_REENTRY_COOLDOWN` of 5.0 that the stale `>= 4.0` literal would have passed is now correctly rejected (below the live floor the round-1 G1 cadence exploit partially reopens — a player re-enters the axis with a fresh grace-exempt pulse before the natural pulse cadence fires once). The test asserts BOTH: a config at the default knobs accepts `GRACE_REENTRY_COOLDOWN = 6.0` (default), AND a config with `STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY = 4.0, GRACE_REENTRY_COOLDOWN = 5.0` is rejected (proving the gate reads the live expression, not the literal). **AND (v) the stamina-curve domain gate (round-28 T-4 — systems NEW-1; closes the sign/domain under-coverage class that left every stamina-curve knob EXCEPT `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE` ungated for sign):** config-validation asserts each sign-sensitive stamina-curve knob is **within its G.2 registered range** — the rejection threshold is the G.2 range floor, not a bare sign check (round-28 systems-B1/B2 — the prior wording asserted the floor but rejected only at `< 0`, an internal contradiction that also disagreed with `entities.yaml`; the gate, `entities.yaml`, and G.2 now all reject below the registered floor): **`STAMINA_MAX` must be `>= 60` (its G.2 range floor) and is rejected below it — and a `STAMINA_MAX <= 0` is the worst sub-case**: it makes the D.1/D.2 per-Heartbeat `stamina_new = clamp(stamina ± rate*dt, 0, STAMINA_MAX)` call `math.clamp(x, 0, negative)`, whose `min > max` **throws every single Heartbeat tick — a hard runtime CRASH**, strictly worse than the `STAMINA_MAX = 0` permanent-forced-walk degenerate; **`STAMINA_REGEN_RATE` must be `>= 5`** (G.2 floor) and is rejected below it (a `< 0` value silently DRAINS stamina during rest; a `0`–`4` value is below-range degraded/never-recovering); **`STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY` must be `>= 0.5`** (G.2 floor) and is rejected below it (a `< 0` value is a nonsensical pre-emptive regen; a `0`–`0.49` value is the below-range bar-twitch degenerate). The test asserts a config at the G.2 defaults accepts, AND a config with `STAMINA_MAX = -1` (or `0`, or `30` below the 60 floor), `STAMINA_REGEN_RATE = -1` (or `3` below the 5 floor), or `STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY = -1` (or `0.2` below the 0.5 floor) is **rejected** — proving the gate is range/domain-correct, not merely a sign check present for `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE`. (Verifies all five config-validation invariants as one gate: the [R13-F1] emission hierarchy, the G.2 drain-floor, the G.6 [R13-F2] coupled margin, the live-config grace-cooldown floor, and the stamina-curve domain gate can none of them silently re-invert/zero/reopen/crash via tuning. The five are independent assertions in the same config-validation pass — a config failing ANY one is rejected.)
- **H.13** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `t = t_0 + 4.0`, `d_xz = 0.2 stud`, `platform = "touch"`, WHEN `GetPulseMagnitude` is called for Light, THEN `effective_d` is set to 0 (drift floor zeroed; `0.2 < 2.0`) and result = `0.08 × 0.30 = 0.024` (stationary attenuation applies after drift-zeroing). (Verifies D.4 mobile-drift branch on touch context.)
- **H.14** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `platform = "pc"`, `d_xz = 0.2 stud`, `t = t_0 + 4.0`, WHEN `GetPulseMagnitude` is called for Light, THEN drift-floor gate does NOT apply (PC platform), `effective_d = 0.2`, and stationary branch fires: result = `0.08 × 0.30 = 0.024`. **Distinguishes that PC and touch produce the same magnitude here, but via different code paths** — drift gate must not engage on PC.

### H.5 — Squad Coordination — Ping

- **H.16** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a 2-player server, WHEN player A fires `RequestPing` targeting a tagged entity at distance 50 studs (within `PING_RAYCAST_RANGE = 80`), THEN player B's client receives `OnPingBroadcast` with the entity's tag (e.g., `enemy`) within ≤ 1 server Heartbeat (≤ 17 ms server-side); marker displays for 8.0 s ± 0.1 s and auto-removes. (Verifies C.4.1 ping broadcast + display.)
- **H.17** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a player has fired 3 pings within a 1.5 s window, WHEN a 4th ping is requested within the same 1.5 s window, THEN the server rejects (`PING_BURST_CAP = 3` exceeded), no `OnPingBroadcast` fires, and no rate-limit budget for legitimate pings is consumed. (Verifies C.4.1 burst cap.)
- **H.18** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (touch simulation) — GIVEN three `TouchStarted` events arrive within `PING_TWO_FINGER_WINDOW = 150 ms`, WHEN the gesture detector evaluates, THEN no ping fires (3rd-finger cancel) and no cooldown is charged. (Verifies C.7 / E.D third-finger cancel rule.)

### H.6 — Squad Coordination — Emote

- **H.19** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player in any state opens the emote wheel and selects any of slots 1–6, WHEN the emote fires, THEN `DisturbanceService:Emit` is NOT called (zero invocations) for the duration of the emote. **Non-vacuous wiring anchor (round-28 T-3, qa Miss #8 — same class as H.78's assert-cue-fired anchor):** the zero-`Emit` assertion is made **in the same test body, AFTER** asserting the emote **actually fired** — exactly one `OnEmoteBroadcast` was broadcast for the selected slot (the wiring-proof positive). So an unwired/empty emote handler (one that never fires the emote at all) **fails the cue-fired anchor and can never reach a vacuous pass** on the zero-`Emit` negative; the test proves the live emote path was exercised and produced no disturbance, not merely that nothing happened. (Verifies C.4.2 ZERO-disturbance contract; tester mocks both `DisturbanceService` and the emote-broadcast surface, asserts `OnEmoteBroadcast` count = 1 for the slot AND `DisturbanceService:Emit` count = 0.)
- **H.20** — MANUAL-PLAYTEST (Gamepad — D-pad-only configuration) — GIVEN a controller with right stick masked off (single-input-mode), WHEN the player presses D-pad Up (held 0.3 s) to open the wheel, then D-pad Left or Right to cycle slots, then `A / Cross` to commit, THEN one of the 6 emotes fires correctly and displays for `EMOTE_DISPLAY_DURATION = 4 s`. (Verifies C.4.2 D-pad fallback flow.)

(Verifies C.5.5 + C.6 T5 sprint hard-reset.)
- **H.22** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN `Humanoid.Died` fires for player A, WHEN the same `Humanoid.Died` event fires a second time within 1 s (replication race), THEN the death handler is a no-op (idempotent guard: no second oxygen deduction; no second timer reset). (Verifies C.5.2 idempotent guard.)

- **H.22c** — AUTO-INTEGRATION *(Lemur-caveat per C.11 — `Players.PlayerRemoving` instance-lifecycle fidelity — R12-qa)* — GIVEN an alive player A with `Humanoid.Health = 100` (above `HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD`), `getServerTime()` injected to return a fixed value `T`, and `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[A] = nil` (no recent predator damage), WHEN the player disconnects, THEN the `PlayerRemoving` handler evaluates the imminent-death predicate as `false`, T5 is NOT invoked, squad oxygen is unchanged, no `OnPlayerDied` event is broadcast. - **H.22d** — AUTO-INTEGRATION *(Lemur-caveat per C.11 — `Players.PlayerRemoving` instance-lifecycle fidelity — R12-qa)* — GIVEN an alive player A with `Humanoid.Health = 100` (above `HP_IMMINENT_THRESHOLD`, so arm 1 cannot fire), `getServerTime()` injected to return a fixed value `T`, `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[A] = T - 1.5` (predator damage 1.5 s ago — within `DAMAGE_RECENT_WINDOW = 3.0 s`, so arm 2 carries), and squad oxygen = 5, WHEN the player disconnects (`Players.PlayerRemoving` fires) without `Humanoid.Died` having fired, THEN within the same server Heartbeat: a `DeathCostReconciliation` record is created and reaches `state="Committed"`, squad oxygen decrements to 4, `OnPlayerDied(deathCause="disconnect-while-damaged")` is broadcast to the squad. - **H.22e** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a 4-player squad with squad oxygen = 5, player A dies a first time at `T_1` (Path A; the life-1 record reaches `state="Committed"`; squad oxygen 5 → 4), then T6 fires at `T_1 + 30 s` (A respawns; `currentDeathRecord[A]` cleared to `nil`; the life-1 record — retained `Committed` since its success path per N-2 — is **removed at this T6** when the pointer detaches), WHEN player A dies a second time during the same server session, THEN (1) **`currentDeathRecord[A] == nil` is asserted immediately after the GIVEN's T6** (the pointer clear is an observable post-T6 state, not just a setup assumption — covers the T6 `currentDeathRecord` clear transition), and (2) a **fresh `deathEventId` record** is minted off that clean pointer (`state="Pending" → InFlight → Committed`), `RequestSquadOxygenSpend(amount=1, reason="death", deathEventId=<life-2 id>)` is invoked **with the full 3-field arity, and the test asserts the life-2 call's `deathEventId` is DISTINCT from life 1's** (two monotonic ids for two death events — round-16 qa), squad oxygen decrements 4 → 3, and `OnPlayerDied(playerId=A)` is broadcast. (Verifies multi-life independence AND the `currentDeathRecord` clear transition — life 2's charge is governed by its own record off a cleared pointer, never by a leftover life-1 flag. Pillar 2 across multi-life squad runs.)
- **H.22f** — AUTO-INTEGRATION *(Lemur-caveat per C.11 — `Players.PlayerRemoving` instance-lifecycle fidelity — R12-qa)* — GIVEN player A satisfies the C.5.3 Path B imminent-death predicate at disconnect time (record minted in `state="Pending"`), AND a fault is injected into RM's **server-internal `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` service method** (a PC→RM Knit service-to-service call, NOT a RemoteFunction — C.5.3 step 3, round-16; the test mocks the RM service handle) such that `pcall` returns `(false, errorMessage)`, WHEN the handler executes its dispatch sequence, THEN: (1) the record transitions `Pending → InFlight` BEFORE the pcall'd service call, (2) the pcall captures the failure without crashing the handler, (3) the rollback transitions `InFlight → Pending` (the row is now reconcile-eligible again), (4) the failure is surfaced to a server log channel reading `row.playerName`/`row.userId` (the snapshot, never the live instance), and (5) the squad oxygen ledger is unchanged. (Verifies clear-on-failure — the InFlight state has a mandatory failure exit, so a transient RM failure leaves the row Pending and recoverable by the tick, never stuck or silently zeroed.)
- **H.23** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a 2-player squad with squad oxygen = 5, WHEN player A dies, THEN squad oxygen decrements to 4 within the same server Heartbeat as the death event (NOT at respawn arrival 30 s later). (Verifies C.5.3 deduct-at-death timing.)
- **H.24** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player in S4 (Dead-Respawning), WHEN the player sends `RequestEmote`, THEN it is accepted server-side; WHEN the player sends `RequestPing` (round-20 R17-3 cut), THEN it is **rejected** (no `OnPingBroadcast`); movement / sprint / lantern / gather requests in the same window are also rejected. (Verifies C.5.4 dead-player allowed input set after the R17-3 ping cut + C.9 `RequestPing` S4/S5-reject validation. **Round-22: corrected — the prior wording accepted S4 `RequestPing`, contradicting R17-3; a conforming server now rejects it.**)

#### H.7r — Death-Cost Reconciliation Tick (R6-B11 — maps qa-lead F3a–F3h)

All ACs in this block require the C.11 clock-injection seam (the tick-interval gate reads `getServerTime()`); see the C.11 Lemur fidelity caveat for the instance-lifecycle portion of the Path-B ACs.

- **H.35** (F3a) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a Path A player A who dies (`Humanoid.Died`), AND a fault injected so the *initial* dispatch `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` `pcall` returns `(false, err)` (record rolled back `InFlight → Pending` per step 4; no `OnPlayerDied` broadcast; squad oxygen unchanged), `getServerTime()` injected under test control, WHEN the clock is advanced by `RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL = 5.0 s` to fire one reconciliation tick AND the fault is cleared so the retry succeeds, THEN the tick finds A's record reconcile-eligible (`state=="Pending"`), re-dispatches (`Pending → InFlight → Committed`), squad oxygen decrements by exactly 1, the record reaches `state="Committed"`, and `OnPlayerDied(playerId=A, deathCause="reconciliation-recovery")` is broadcast — **the payload's `deathCause` FIELD is asserted equal to `"reconciliation-recovery"` (round-16 qa — field assertion, not merely event-fired)**. (Verifies the rollback→tick recovery handoff; the clear-on-failure rollback is a handoff, not an endpoint.)
- **H.36** (F3b) — AUTO-INTEGRATION *(Lemur-caveat: depends on removed-player instance lifecycle — AUTO where Lemur fidelity permits, else MANUAL-DEVICE/in-Studio per C.11)* — GIVEN a Path B player B who disconnects while satisfying the imminent-death predicate, a record created with `deathEventId = E` snapshotting `{userId, playerName, lastKnownPosition}`, `pathOrigin="B"`, `state="Pending"`, AND a fault injected so the initial deduction rolls back, `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN the clock advances one tick interval and the retry succeeds, THEN the tick reconciles `reconcileRows[E]` (NOT keyed by any `Player` instance — B's instance is destroyed), squad oxygen decrements by exactly 1, the record reaches `state="Committed"`, `OnPlayerDied(deathCause="reconciliation-recovery")` is broadcast (**`deathCause` FIELD asserted — round-16 qa**), and `reconcileRows[E]` is removed. No access to `B.Name` or `B.Character` occurs (the log path reads `row.playerName`, R6-B10). (Verifies the deathEventId-keyed Path-B recovery + destroyed-instance safety.)
- **H.37a** (F3c — tick-scan skip; split from H.37 per R12-qa, two independent outcomes) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN player A (still present, in S4) whose initial dispatch actually *succeeded* (record reached `state="Committed"` and is **retained** in `reconcileRows` per N-2, pending its T6/`PlayerRemoving` removal; squad oxygen already decremented once), `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN two further reconciliation ticks fire **before** A's T6, THEN A's death event is NOT reconcile-eligible on either tick (`reconcile_eligible` selects only `state=="Pending"`; the retained `Committed` row is skipped, not selected), `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` is NOT re-invoked, and squad oxygen is unchanged across both ticks. (Verifies that a retained `Committed` row is correctly skipped by the eligibility scan — exactly one unit per death event regardless of tick count.)
- **H.37b** (F3c — replayed-`Died` short-circuit; split from H.37 per R12-qa) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN the same setup as H.37a (retained `Committed` row, pointer live), WHEN a replayed `Humanoid.Died` for A fires in the retain window, THEN the replay resolves the retained `Committed` row at step 1 and no-ops (N-2 replay short-circuit): no fresh `deathEventId` is minted, `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` is NOT invoked, squad oxygen is unchanged, and no second `OnPlayerDied` is broadcast. (Verifies the N-2 replay short-circuit independently of the tick-scan behavior.)
- **H.38** (F3d) — AUTO-INTEGRATION *(Lemur-caveat per C.11 where a Path-B removed player is exercised)* — GIVEN a death event whose initial deduction fails and whose retry succeeds on the *second* tick **with RM available** (worst case: a tick fired immediately before the failure), `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN the clock is advanced, THEN the record reaches `state="Committed"` no later than the nominal `2 × RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL = 10.0 s` after the original death event. **Paired negative-bound case:** if the injected RM fault persists across 3 ticks, the record remains `Pending` and re-dispatches each interval (it is NOT abandoned, NOT double-charged, NOT lost) — verifying the 10 s figure is the nominal single-retry bound, not a guarantee under sustained outage. (Verifies the reconciliation latency model; correctness no longer depends on completing before `RESPAWN_DELAY` since the `Pending` record survives T6 — reconcile-before-respawn is the advisory UX target in G.6, not a correctness invariant.)
- **H.39** (F3e) — AUTO-INTEGRATION *(Lemur-caveat per C.11 — Path-B removed-player lifecycle)* — GIVEN a Path-B `reconcileRows[E]` entry that reconciles successfully on a tick (the player has already departed, so **no live `currentDeathRecord` pointer references `E`** — the orphaned-row case), WHEN the success path runs, THEN the record transitions to `state="Committed"`, `OnPlayerDied(deathCause="reconciliation-recovery")` is broadcast exactly once **with the payload's `deathCause` FIELD asserted (round-16 qa — this AC previously asserted no broadcast at all)**, and `reconcileRows[E]` is **removed from the working-set in the same committing tick** (orphaned-row exception — nothing retains it) AND a subsequent tick performs no work for `E` (the entry no longer exists). **Paired Path-A-while-present case:** a Path-A row that reconciles on a tick *while the player is still in S4* (pointer still live) transitions to `Committed` and is **retained** (skipped by the eligibility scan), removed only at the player's T6/`PlayerRemoving`. (Verifies the success-path exit / working-set termination for both the orphaned-immediate-removal and the retained-until-T6 cases.)
- **H.40** (F3f) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN server start at injected time `T_0` with the reconciliation tick connected in `Knit:Start()`, `reconcileRows={}`, AND **`lastTickTime` bootstrap-seeded to `T_0`** (R11 Tier-0), WHEN the **very first** Heartbeat fires (clock advanced by one frame, `< RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL` since `T_0`), THEN the interval gate evaluates `(now − lastTickTime) = (now − T_0)` — a real number, **never `now − nil`** — raises **no error**, and executes no scan; the first scan executes only once the elapsed-time gate (`> RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL`, routed through `getServerTime()`) is satisfied — **AND the test MUST additionally assert that, when the interval gate passes, `lastTickTime` is updated to the scan-fire time (`lastTickTime == getServerTime()` as injected at the gate-pass frame), so the next interval is measured from this scan rather than drifting (R12-qa)**. (Verifies the first-tick timing gate + bootstrap ordering + the gate-pass `lastTickTime` write **AND the Tier-0 show-stopper: an unseeded `lastTickTime` would crash the Heartbeat callback on frame 1 and kill the tick for the session** — build-F1 / SD-B1 / N-4.)
- **H.41** (F3g — binary signal for multi-life record independence) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a 4-player squad, player A dies life 1 (initial dispatch succeeds: life-1 record `Committed` and retained per N-2, squad oxygen 5→4), T6 fires at `T_1 + 30 s` (A respawns; `currentDeathRecord[A]=nil`; the retained `Committed` life-1 record is removed at this T6), WHEN A dies a second time (life 2) and that second dispatch fails-then-rolls-back to `Pending`, AND a reconciliation tick fires, THEN A's **life-2 record** (a fresh `deathEventId`) is reconcile-eligible (`state=="Pending"`) and the tick recovers the life-2 deduction (squad oxygen 4→3, `OnPlayerDied(deathCause="reconciliation-recovery")` — **`deathCause` FIELD asserted, round-16 qa**). **This is the binary pass/fail signal that no per-player sticky death-cost flag survives across lives** — under the retired two-boolean model a leftover `deathDeductionCommitted[A]=true` from life 1 would have made the life-2 record never eligible and the cost permanently lost (the R6-B5 defect class). The state record makes that failure mode structurally impossible because each `deathEventId` carries its own independent `state`. (Replaces the former R6-B5 flag-clearance signal; the refactor dissolves the defect rather than guarding it.)
- **H.42** (F3h — documented invariant + Abandoned transition) — MANUAL-DEVICE / in-Studio *(NOT Lemur-injectable — `BindToClose` server-shutdown timing is not reproducible under the headless Lemur harness; this AC must be confirmed at least once in live Studio/TestService per C.11)* — GIVEN a `Pending` (or `InFlight`) `reconcileRows[E]` entry (initial deduction rolled back, not yet retried), WHEN `BindToClose` fires before the next reconciliation tick, THEN the record transitions `→ "Abandoned"` (logged with the snapshotted `playerName`/`userId`) and no squad-oxygen decrement is applied for `E` this session. This is the **accepted in-session-bridge bound made an explicit terminal transition** (reconciliation state is in-memory, not a durable queue; RM owns the ProfileStore ledger and the run is ending at shutdown). (Verifies the persistence-boundary invariant AND that the loss is a logged `Abandoned` transition, not a silent row-drop — NOT a defect; asserts the bound the spec claims.)
- **H.43** (R6-I12 exact-boundary) — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN the imminent-death predicate with `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN evaluated at the exact boundary values `Humanoid.Health = 25.0`, `(now - lastPredatorDamageTimestamp) = 30.0 s` (arm 1), `= 3.0 s` (arm 2), and `predatorCausedImminent = false` (arm 3 latch unset), THEN all strict-`<` comparators return `false` and the predicate does NOT fire (none of the three arms). Paired with off-boundary cases `24.99` / `29.99 s` / `2.99 s` (arms 1/2 fire) to exercise the live branches. (Verifies C.5.3 / G.6 strict-`<` boundary semantics across all three arms — the "use 24.99 / 25.01" tuning guidance is encoded as an AC.)
- **H.44** (arm-3 / Scenario-C closure — B5; outer ceiling — R10-1) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN an alive player A with `Humanoid.Health = 18` (sub-threshold), `predatorCausedImminent[A] = true` (predator drove A below threshold earlier this life), `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[A] = T - 45` (latch set 45 s ago — within `LATCH_MAX_AGE = 120 s`), `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[A] = T - 45` (predator damage 45 s ago — OUTSIDE `HP_ARM_RECENT_WINDOW = 30 s`, so arm 1 cannot fire; arm 2 cannot fire), `getServerTime()` injected, squad oxygen = 5, WHEN A disconnects (`Players.PlayerRemoving`), THEN **arm 3 fires**: a death record reaches `state="Committed"`, squad oxygen decrements to 4, `OnPlayerDied(deathCause="disconnect-while-damaged")` is broadcast. **Paired negative #1 (causality):** identical setup but `predatorCausedImminent[A] = false` (a minor graze that never crossed the threshold) → predicate is `false`, no record, squad oxygen unchanged (round-3 stale-graze false-positive stays closed). **Paired negative #2 (outer ceiling, R10-1):** identical positive setup but `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[A] = T - 150` (latch set 150 s ago — OUTSIDE `LATCH_MAX_AGE = 120 s`) → arm 3 does NOT fire, no record, squad oxygen unchanged (the weak-attribution over-charge tail is bounded). **Exact-boundary pair (R12-qa):** `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[A] = T - 120.0` (elapsed exactly equal to `LATCH_MAX_AGE`) → arm 3 does **NOT** fire (the ceiling comparator is strict `<` — boundary rejection per the C.5.3/G.6 cost-attribution convention); `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[A] = T - 119.99` → arm 3 **fires** (strictly inside the ceiling). (Verifies B5 Scenario-C closure via the latch, the R10-1 outer-ceiling bound, and that threshold-crossing causality — not recency or magnitude — is the distinguishing fact within the ceiling.) **Production caveat (N-3 — controlled-order test vs live):** this AC asserts the *positive* arm-3 charge under a controlled latch-read order. In production, arm 3 reads the latch through the C.5.3 cache-first snapshot and is therefore subject to the **same accepted Race-2 under-charge as the timestamp arms** (F.4: if PredatorService's `PlayerRemoving` clears the latch *before* PC's handler entry, PC caches `false` and the squad is not charged — the Pillar-2-safe under-charge direction, never an over-charge). The cache-first read closes Race 1; Race 2 remains an accepted under-charge for arm 3 exactly as for arms 1/2. So H.44 verifies the rule, not a guarantee that every live predator-caused disconnect charges — that bound is the documented Race-2 acceptance.
- **H.45** (mid-retry skip — no double-dispatch across ticks) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a `Pending` record whose retry is dispatched on tick N (record transitions `Pending → InFlight` synchronously, then the yielding `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` is `task.spawn`'d and is still outstanding when tick N+1 fires), `getServerTime()` injected to fire ticks N and N+1 while the spawned retry is held mid-yield, THEN tick N+1's scan observes `state="InFlight"` (not `Pending`) and does NOT dispatch a second retry for the same record. When the held retry resolves, exactly one `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` has been invoked for the death event. (Verifies the synchronous `Pending → InFlight` transition prevents the across-tick double-dispatch the in-flight marker formerly guarded — qa coverage gap.)
- **H.46** (rejoin-during-reconciliation race) — AUTO-INTEGRATION *(Lemur-caveat per C.11 — removed-then-rejoined player lifecycle)* — GIVEN a Path-B record `reconcileRows[E]` still `Pending` (initial deduction rolled back) for a player who has left, WHEN the same human rejoins under the same `userId` (a fresh `Player` instance, `currentDeathRecord[newPlayer]=nil`) BEFORE the next reconciliation tick, AND that tick then fires, THEN `reconcileRows[E]` reconciles on its own `deathEventId` (squad oxygen −1, `state="Committed"`, removed) and the rejoined player's fresh instance is NOT charged, NOT collided with, and carries no record from the prior life. Exactly one unit is charged for death event `E`; the new life's eventual death is a separate `deathEventId`. (Verifies the deathEventId keying closes the rejoin-during-reconciliation race — qa coverage gap; load-bearing for the R6-B9 keying decision.)
- **H.54** (staleness-sweep late-success discard — R12-net-B2 / R12-SD-4) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN an `InFlight` record whose dispatched coroutine is held mid-yield in `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` (mock RM holds the call) long enough that the staleness sweep fires (`getServerTime() − row.inFlightSince > 2 × RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL`, clock injected) and re-arms the row to `Pending` — **the test asserts INLINE, at the moment of re-arm, that `row.inFlightSince` was cleared to `nil` (round-16 qa — the H.54 sibling of H.56's lifecycle assertions)** — AND the next tick dispatches a second coroutine that commits the row (`Pending → InFlight → Committed`), WHEN the held FIRST coroutine subsequently resolves **successfully**, THEN the late coroutine's step-5 `state == "InFlight"` re-check fails: it does **NOT** transition the row to `Committed` (the row is already `Committed` by its current owner and stays so), does **NOT** broadcast `OnPlayerDied` a second time (exactly one broadcast total for the death event), does **NOT** roll the row back, and logs-and-exits. **Exactly one squad-oxygen unit is charged**: the mock RM counts spend-*commits* and asserts the count is 1 — the late coroutine's duplicate spend *attempt* is the documented accepted residual whose result PC discards (C.5.3 step-4 staleness-sweep block). **The test additionally asserts both spend attempts carried the SAME `deathEventId` (the R13-net-I1 idempotency key), so a real RM deduplicating per `(userId, deathEventId)` per its F.2 contract-row obligation would no-op the duplicate at its own ledger.** (Verifies the step-5 ownership re-check closes the sweep-vs-slow-coroutine double-charge.)
- **H.56** (`inFlightSince` schema — R12-qa) — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a freshly minted `DeathCostReconciliation` record, THEN `row.inFlightSince == nil` while `state == "Pending"`. WHEN the dispatcher performs the synchronous `Pending → InFlight` transition (step 2), THEN `row.inFlightSince` is stamped to the injected `getServerTime()` value of that exact step (same synchronous span as the state write — never a later frame). WHEN the attempt fails and rolls back `InFlight → Pending` (step 4, or the error guard, or the staleness-sweep re-arm), THEN `row.inFlightSince` is cleared back to `nil` in the same transition. AND at no point does any consumer (the staleness sweep included) read `inFlightSince` as an undeclared/absent field — the field exists from record creation (initialized `nil`), per the C.5.3 schema. (Verifies the declared-field lifecycle: nil-while-Pending, stamped-at-dispatch, cleared-on-rollback, never undeclared.)

#### H.7w — Run-End Conditions: T7 wipe / T8 victory (round-11 — closes the round-10/11 "T7/T8 zero ACs" gap; arbiter ACs are against the F.4 provisional `RunController` contract)

- **H.47** (T8 consumes window-survived, NOT activation — GD-02 / R11-D1) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a squad whose Beacon has fired `OnEscapeBeaconActivated` (BCT3, window-start) but NOT yet `OnBeaconWindowSurvived`, `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN PC's T8 consumer is evaluated, THEN PC raises **no** `"victory"` condition and no `RunEnded(victory)` resolves (activation is the window-start cue only). WHEN `OnBeaconWindowSurvived(timestamp)` (BCT4) subsequently fires, THEN PC calls `RunController:RunEndConditionRaised("victory", squadState)` exactly once. **Mock strategy for the cross-GDD signal (R12-qa; arity corrected round-16)**: PC's tests mock the **signal emitter** — a stub emitter (Knit `Signal`-compatible) standing in for Crafting's `OnEscapeBeaconActivated(activatorPlayerId, timestamp, windowDurationSeconds)` / `OnBeaconWindowSurvived(timestamp)` surface, fired directly by the test in the GIVEN/WHEN steps **with those exact arities** (the prior arity-zero `OnBeaconWindowSurvived()` mock was stale against Crafting's declared `{timestamp: number}` — F.2); the test MUST NOT depend on, import, or simulate the Crafting & Items implementation (which is unauthored as code) — only on the F.2 provisional contract's signal names and arities. (Verifies PC subscribes to the survival-window emit, not the activation edge — the regression GD-02 found; matches `crafting-and-items.md` C.9.)
- **H.48** (T8 S4-rescue) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a 2-player squad where player B is in S4 (Dead-Respawning, respawn timer running) at the moment `OnBeaconWindowSurvived` fires, WHEN T8 resolves victory, THEN B transitions S4 → S5(victory), B's respawn timer is cancelled, and B is counted as a survivor in `squadState`. (Verifies the C.6 T8 S4-rescue rule.)
- **H.49** (T7 raises, does NOT broadcast) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN all 2–4 squad members in S4 AND squad oxygen `< 1`, `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN T7's condition is met, THEN PC calls `RunController:RunEndConditionRaised("wipe", squadState)` exactly once and **PC itself never fires a `RunEnded` RemoteEvent** (asserted by mocking the arbiter and the RemoteEvent surface: the arbiter receives the raise; no `RunEnded` is broadcast by PC). (Verifies the single-broadcaster invariant on PC's side — round-8 B4.)
- **H.50** (PC's side of the run-end raise + defeat-hold — re-scoped per R12-qa: PC-only, the arbiter is mocked) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a **mocked** `RunController` arbiter (the real arbiter is unauthored — its precedence/hold behavior is a **RunController GDD obligation**, noted in F.4, NOT tested here), all squad members in S4 and squad oxygen `< 1` at injected time `T`, WHEN PC's T7 condition fires and `OnBeaconWindowSurvived` is then emitted (via the H.47 stub emitter) at `T + Δ` with `Δ < RUN_END_DEFEAT_HOLD`, THEN: (1) PC raised `RunEndConditionRaised("wipe", squadState)` **exactly once with the correct payload** — `conditionType = "wipe"` and `squadState` a per-member liveness snapshot `{ [userId] = "alive" | "S4" | "S5" }` taken at raise time per the F.4 contract, **asserted against CONCRETE values (round-16 qa): for this test's setup with all members in S4, `squadState` must deep-equal `{ [userIdA] = "S4", [userIdB] = "S4", … }` for every member — exact-value equality, not shape-checking** *(note: the GIVEN deliberately never fires `OnEscapeBeaconActivated`, so PC's `_beaconActivated` latch is unset and the T7 raise is un-suppressed — the round-16 H.61 suppression is out of scope here)*; (2) PC raised `RunEndConditionRaised("victory", squadState)` exactly once on the BCT4 emit, **and (round-20 qa — victory-raise payload was shape-only; now asserted against CONCRETE values like clause 1) the victory raise's `squadState` deep-equals the per-member liveness snapshot taken at the BCT4 emit — for this setup a member in S4 at window-survived reads `"S4"` in the snapshot (the arbiter then applies the S4-rescue, H.48), every alive member reads `"alive"` — exact-value equality, not shape-checking**; (3) throughout the `RUN_END_DEFEAT_HOLD` window, **PC itself broadcasts no `RunEnded`, no defeat banner, and no defeat UI state** — the defeat presentation is withheld for the hold and only the arbiter's eventual broadcast drives the results screen (F.4 player-experience rule); and (4) PC mutates no run-end state on its own authority while the hold is pending. (Verifies PC's raise payload + hold-compliant withholding; the arbiter's own precedence resolution — victory-over-wipe, broadcast-once-after-hold — is owed by the RunController GDD and is verified there, against the F.4 provisional contract.)
- **H.51** (`:ReleasePredatorLock` on T5, both paths — SD-B2 / AI-1) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN player A holding a predator target-lock (mock `PredatorService` recording lock state), WHEN A dies via Path A (`Humanoid.Died`), THEN `PredatorService:ReleasePredatorLock(A)` is invoked exactly once as a T5 side-effect (g). **Paired Path-B case:** a player who disconnects while satisfying the imminent-death predicate also triggers `:ReleasePredatorLock` once. **Paired reconciliation case (`getServerTime()` injected via the C.11 clock seam — round-28 qa-I1; the tick fires only when the injected clock advances past `RECONCILIATION_TICK_INTERVAL` from `lastTickTime`, so without the seam annotation this branch is paper-only):** a Path-A death whose initial charge rolled back and recovers on a tick re-invokes `:ReleasePredatorLock` on the reconciliation-success path (idempotent — net one effective release). (Verifies side-effect (g) fires on both death paths and on reconciliation success, closing the wasted-aggro / stale-lock class.)
- **H.52** (caption queue dedup by `deathEventId` — R11-D4) — AUTO-INTEGRATION (caption-overlay logic) — GIVEN captions mode enabled and player B dies once (`deathEventId = E`, cause `"died"`, "[Name] is down" caption shown), WHEN a replayed `Humanoid.Died` for B AND a later `reconciliation-recovery` broadcast both carry the same `deathEventId = E`, THEN the caption overlay enqueues "[Name] is down" **exactly once** for `E` (the replay and the recovery do not re-enqueue), and a death-burst of two distinct events (`E1` at `t1`, `E2` at `t2 > t1`) shows both captions in ascending-timestamp order, one at a time. (Verifies V/A.4a order + dedup — one caption per death event, never one per broadcast.)
- **H.53** (nil-position caption parity — R11-D5) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a Path-B death of B with `deathContext.lastKnownPosition` resolved `nil` (no HRP and no cached position) and cause `"disconnect-while-damaged"`, WHEN the death is rendered, THEN the **positional remote Death SFX is suppressed** (V/A.3 nil-position guard) BUT the non-positional "[Name] is down" caption (V/A.4) **still fires** for B. (Verifies the caption fires on `{"died","disconnect-while-damaged"}` independent of the positional-SFX nil-position suppression — the squad always learns the *fact* of the death even when the *spatial audio* cannot be placed.)

### H.8 — State Machine Concurrency

- **H.25** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN the four locomotion×lantern combinations (S1+S3a, S1+S3b, S2+S3a, S2+S3b), WHEN each axis is mutated independently, THEN the other axis remains unchanged in all 8 single-axis transitions tested. (Verifies C.6 orthogonal axis invariant — no cross-axis side effects.)

### H.9 — Cross-Platform Parity

- **H.26** — MANUAL-PLAYTEST (PC + Gamepad) — GIVEN one PC tester (KB+M hold) and one gamepad tester (L3 hold) on the same server, WHEN both initiate sprint from `s_0 = 50` and hold continuously, THEN both exhaust stamina at exactly `t = 4.0 s ± 1 frame`; both transition to S1 within the same Heartbeat. (Verifies cross-platform stamina parity per D.3.)
- **H.27** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (Logic core, BLOCKING) + MANUAL-DEVICE (iPhone SE-class, touch-visual ADVISORY) — **(round-20 qa relabel — was MANUAL-only; the toggle-state-clears-on-T2 behavior is deterministic state-machine Logic; only the button's visual return is device-specific.)** GIVEN a touch sprint toggle active (sprint started), `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN stamina depletes to 0 and force-walk engages (T2), THEN the server clears sprint-authorization state such that no further `RequestSprintToggle=true` is honored until a *fresh* toggle assertion arrives — i.e. the toggle does not ghost-hold across the forced T2 (the Logic gate, asserted server-side). The MANUAL-DEVICE layer confirms the touch button *visually* returns to "off" and a fresh tap re-arms sprint when stamina recovers (ADVISORY — the on-device visual). (Verifies C.1.3 no-ghost-hold-post-force-walk: the state-clear Logic is an automated gate; the touch-visual is the device layer.)

### H.10 — Performance and Bandwidth

- **H.28** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (Lemur headless) — GIVEN a 4-player server running 300 Heartbeat ticks (5 s simulated), all 4 players cycling Walk/Sprint/Dead with lantern toggling each second, **AND all 4 carrying `Pending` `reconcileRows` entries under an injected RM fault so the reconciliation tick exercises its full scan-and-dispatch path each interval** (the Heartbeat body scans the rows, performs the synchronous `Pending → InFlight` transition, and `task.spawn`s each retry off the handler), WHEN total CPU time attributed to Player Controller state machines + stamina + lantern + emission timers **+ the reconciliation tick's per-tick scan + dispatch (`debug.profilebegin("PC_ReconcileTick")` around the Heartbeat body, excluding the spawned retry coroutines)** is measured via `debug.profilebegin` / `debug.profileend`, THEN average per-frame cost < 0.1 ms AND the reconciliation Heartbeat-body scan contributes < 0.01 ms/tick (the yielding `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` retries run in their own `task.spawn` coroutines and are NOT counted in the Heartbeat-body budget). (Verifies the gameplay-programmer feasibility budget AND that the reconciliation scan + `task.spawn` dispatch model keeps the Heartbeat body within budget — R6-I5.)
- **H.29** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a 4-player server in steady-state sprint, WHEN RemoteEvent traffic is sampled over 60 frames excluding character replication, THEN total outbound bandwidth for Player Controller events (`OnSprintStateChanged`, `OnLanternStateChanged`, `OnStaminaChanged`, **`OnSprintPulse` (round-16 B1 — one event per Sprint pulse per sprinting player, ~60–80 B/s total at 4 steady-state sprinters)**, ping/emote broadcasts) cumulative across all clients < 1 KB/s. (Verifies feasibility bandwidth budget including the round-16 exertion transport. `GatherNodeArmed`/`GatherNodeDisarmed` are RN-owned and accounted in the RN GDD, not here — C.9 cross-ref note.)

### H.11 — Accessibility

- **H.30** — MANUAL-PLAYTEST (Gamepad — D-pad-only) — GIVEN a player using only D-pad + face buttons (no thumbsticks), WHEN the player opens the emote wheel and reaches any of the 6 slots, THEN every slot is reachable in ≤ 5 button presses (Up-hold to open, Left/Right cycle, A to commit; worst-case 3 cycles for opposite-side slot in 6-slot wheel). (Verifies C.4.2 D-pad fallback as full path.)

### H.12 — Inherited ED Obligations

- **H.31** — MANUAL-PLAYTEST (PC) — GIVEN a fresh player spawned in the level-design-flagged Calm-tier first zone (Q11 obligation), WHEN the player walks (no sprint) for the first 10 s after spawn, THEN no Sprint emission is published, no predator state transition occurs, and the squad disturbance meter remains in Calm tier. (Verifies inherited Q11 teaching beat — level-design dependency; PC's role: do not auto-emit on spawn.)
