- **H.32** — MANUAL-DEVICE (iPhone SE-class) — GIVEN a stationary player with thumbstick deflected sub-perceptibly (drift), **with the explicit displacement precondition (R12-build-I4): the character's actual XZ-displacement over each pulse interval is `< MOBILE_ANALOG_DRIFT_THRESHOLD = 2 studs` (the drift deflection produces sub-threshold movement only — the tester MUST confirm this from the position log for the measured window before asserting magnitudes; a deflection that moves the character ≥ 2 studs per interval invalidates the run)**, WHEN sprint state is active for 10 s, THEN every Sprint pulse fired at `t > FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW` is at the **Sprint hold-floor `0.09`** (the drift-zeroed displacement triggers the stationary gate → `0.03`, raised to `0.09` by the R10-6 hold-floor at the [R13-F1] value; NOT full `0.10`) — verified by `DisturbanceService` event log. (Verifies C.7 + D.4 mobile drift floor + R10-6/[R13-F1] hold-floor + Tier-2 #17.)

### H.13 — Heartbeat / `SPRINT_CLIENT_TIMEOUT` (verifies E.D ghost-sprint safeguard via `PlayerHeartbeat`)

- **H.33a** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player in S2 (sprinting) actively sending `PlayerHeartbeat` at 1 Hz, `getServerTime()` injected to advance under test control, WHEN the client stops sending `PlayerHeartbeat` (simulated zombie: server receives no further heartbeat packets) and the injected clock advances `SPRINT_CLIENT_TIMEOUT = 3 s` past `_lastHeartbeatTime[player]`, THEN within 1 server Heartbeat of timeout: server transitions the player to S1, stops the sprint pulse timer, stops emitting Sprint pulses, and starts `STAMINA_REGEN_DELAY` timer. (Verifies E.D ghost-sprint timeout via heartbeat mechanism. **Test executability requires the C.11 clock-injection seam** — without it, the test cannot deterministically advance the timeout window.)
- **H.33b** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player in S2 (sprinting) actively sending `PlayerHeartbeat` at 1 Hz, `getServerTime()` injected to advance under test control, WHEN the client sends additional `PlayerHeartbeat` events faster than `HEARTBEAT_SEND_RATE = 1 Hz` (simulated flood: 10/s), THEN the server drops excess events (rate-limited), `_lastHeartbeatTime[player]` is updated by accepted events ONLY (excess flood events do NOT update the timestamp — verifying the rate-limit is on the write path, not just on the read path), stamina continues draining at `STAMINA_DRAIN_RATE = 12.5 units/s`, and the sprint session terminates at the normal stamina-0 boundary — NOT extended by heartbeat traffic. (Verifies exploit resistance: heartbeat cannot extend sprint authorization beyond stamina bound. **Test executability requires the C.11 clock-injection seam.**)
- **H.33c** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player in S1 (walking, not sprinting), WHEN the client sends `PlayerHeartbeat`, THEN the server silently drops the event (no error, no rate-limit ban), `_lastHeartbeatTime` is NOT updated for this player, and no state transition occurs. (Verifies S2-only validation rule from C.9.)

### H.14 — Emote-Wheel Gather Lockout (added — verifies T9 mutual exclusion)

- **H.34** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (touch simulation) — GIVEN a player on touch with the emote wheel open, WHEN a `RequestGather` would normally fire from a tap-hold on the right-thumb zone, THEN the gather input is suppressed client-side and no `RequestGather` reaches the server. After the wheel closes, the same input fires `RequestGather` normally. (Verifies C.4.3 / C.6 T9 mutual-exclusion rule.)

### H.15 — Round-16 Closures (R16-1/R16-2 rulings + round-15 BLOCKING/IMPORTANT sweep; AC numbering append-only)

- **H.57** (`OnSprintPulse` variant-to-branch binding — round-16 B1) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player in S2 with `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN a Sprint pulse fires on the **moving/grace branch** (D.4 outputs 0.10), THEN exactly one `OnSprintPulse {variant="full"}` is sent to the owning client only (no other client receives it); WHEN a Sprint pulse fires on the **stationary hold-floor branch** (D.4 outputs `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR = 0.09`), THEN exactly one `OnSprintPulse {variant="hold"}` is sent. The variant is asserted equal to the server-evaluated D.4 branch for every pulse in a mixed moving/stationary sprint (one event per pulse, no extras, no drops), and NO `OnSprintPulse` fires outside S2 or on the Light axis. (Verifies the C.9 exertion transport binds variant-to-branch — the V/A.3 cue and V/A.4 caption tick render server truth, never a client guess.)
- **H.57a** (sprint-pulse exertion audibility floor + minimum gradient — round-18 R17-2) — split into a config gate + a mix-review evidence item. **Part 1 (AUTO-UNIT, config-validation gate, BLOCKING):** GIVEN the audio config, WHEN config-validation runs, THEN it asserts `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_VOLUME_FLOOR > 0`, `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_MIN_DELTA_DB > 0`, and `WALK_FOOTSTEP_VOLUME_FLOOR > 0`; AND a config whose authored **hold**-variant playback volume is below `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_VOLUME_FLOOR`, OR whose authored **full − hold** volume delta is below `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_MIN_DELTA_DB`, OR whose authored walk-footstep volume is below `WALK_FOOTSTEP_VOLUME_FLOOR`, is **rejected**. **Part 2 (MANUAL-DEVICE, ADVISORY — mix-review evidence per the coding-standards Visual/Feel gate; ambient baseline DEFINED per R17-2):** **Defined ambient baseline (round-20 R17-2 — the prior "above ambient" gated against a vacuum):** the test runs against a **named reference soundscape** — the Hunt-tier ambient bed at the art-bible-calibrated default mix level (the loudest ambient tier a sprinting player realistically occupies), played on iPhone SE-class hardware at the device's median user volume, with no other squad SFX active (the cue must read against worst-case masking, not silence). Against THAT baseline, a hearing playtester (a) hears the exertion cue on every Sprint pulse — both `full` and `hold` variants audible above the Hunt-tier ambient bed — and (b) can distinguish a moving (full) pulse from a stationary-hold pulse by ear **via BOTH the ≥ `SPRINT_PULSE_EXERTION_MIN_DELTA_DB` (4.0 dB) loudness step AND the timbre/rhythm differentiator (V/A.3) — the tester must confirm the moving-vs-holding distinction survives even when the dB step alone is partially masked** (the R17-2 masking-robustness claim). (Verifies the gradient's sole audio channel cannot be silently zeroed by a mix pass, and the moving-vs-holding gradient stays perceivable against a DEFINED worst-case ambient — the R17-2 ruling; Part 1 is the machine-checkable floor, Part 2 confirms the floor is perceptually sufficient against the Hunt-tier baseline, not merely non-zero against silence.)
- **H.58** (`deathTimestamp` production + order binding — round-16 B2) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN player A dies via Path A at injected time `T_A` and player B dies via Path B at injected time `T_B > T_A`, THEN (1) each death's `deathContext.deathTimestamp` (and `reconcileRows[*].deathTimestamp`) equals the injected clock value at its death event (`T_A` / `T_B` — clock-source: the C.11 seam, Path-A handler entry / Path-B `cachedNow`); (2) each `OnPlayerDied` payload carries that exact stamp; (3) a delayed `reconciliation-recovery` broadcast for A carries the ORIGINAL `T_A`, not the commit time; and (4) when B's broadcast ARRIVES BEFORE A's delayed broadcast, the V/A.4a caption queue still renders A's caption FIRST (ascending `deathTimestamp`, not arrival order) — binding the H.52 ordering assertion to a produced value; **(5) clock-seam distinguisher (round-18 qa — proves causation, not correlation): the injected `getServerTime()` value is deliberately set DIFFERENT from the harness wall-clock (`os.clock`/`tick`/real `GetServerTimeNow`) at each death event, and the assertion is that each `deathTimestamp` equals the INJECTED seam value, NEVER the wall-clock value — so an implementation that read wall-clock instead of the C.11 seam FAILS (the prior H.58 could pass against a wall-clock read whenever the two happened to agree); (6) Path-B single clock read: the test injects a seam that returns a DIFFERENT value on a hypothetical second read within the handler and asserts `deathTimestamp == cachedNow ==` the value the imminent-death predicate evaluated against — a non-conforming implementation that re-reads the clock for the stamp produces a stamp that disagrees with the predicate `now`, which this assertion catches (one read serves both predicate and stamp). **(round-22 qa — seam call-count proxy: the injected seam is a counting stub, and the test additionally asserts `getServerTime()` is invoked EXACTLY ONCE across the Path-B handler's predicate-evaluation + stamp-mint span — call-count `== 1`; a second invocation (the re-read the prose forbids) fails the count even in the degenerate case where the two reads happen to return equal values, making the single-read discipline machine-checkable independently of the value-disagreement assertion).** (Verifies the consumed-never-produced closure: population, clock source — proven to be the seam, not wall-clock — payload carriage, single-read discipline, and the V/A.4a order binding.)
- **H.59** (`OnPlayerS4Entered` + S4 no-re-acquire — [R16-2]) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN player A holding a predator target-lock (mock `PredatorService` recording lock state and received lifecycle signals), WHEN A dies via Path A (T5 → S4), THEN `PredatorService:OnPlayerS4Entered(A)` is invoked exactly once (alongside the (g) `:ReleasePredatorLock(A)` call), AND while A is in S4 the mock's acquire path — offered A's still-present frozen corpse `HumanoidRootPart` position every tick for the full 30 s `RESPAWN_DELAY` — never (re-)acquires A as a target. **Paired Path-B negative:** a Path-B death (player removed) fires NO `OnPlayerS4Entered` (the removed case is PredatorService's own `PlayerRemoving` teardown). **Vacuity scope (round-20 qa relabel — closes the round-19 ai-F4/qa-B-1 "tests the mock, not PC" finding):** the "mock never re-acquires" half exercises a mock that *implements the gate it is asserting*, so it is **vacuous coverage of the gate behavior** — it proves nothing about a real PredatorService. The **real BLOCKING gate this AC carries is ONLY PC's fire-side obligation** (PC fires `OnPlayerS4Entered` exactly once at T5→S4, Path A; NOT on Path B) — that half is genuine and machine-checkable against PC. The re-acquire-refusal half is **ADVISORY-until-Predator-AI-GDD** (it becomes a real integration gate only when the actual PredatorService handler exists and is reverse-cited, F.4). (Verifies PC's fire-side mechanism; the gate-behavior half is explicitly vacuous-against-mock, not counted as real predator-side coverage.)
- **H.60** (PC FIRES `OnPlayerT6Respawned` — PC-side single-trigger-channel, [R16-2] / AI-I1; **round-18 qa split — was conflated with the handler behavior**) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN player A in S4 with a mock `PredatorService` recording the signals it receives, WHEN T6 fires, THEN `PredatorService:OnPlayerT6Respawned(A)` is invoked **exactly once**, and the test asserts NO OTHER PC code path fires it — not T5, not the reconciliation tick (H.70), not a duplicate replayed `Humanoid.Died` (the C.6 T6 row is the single trigger channel). This AC verifies ONLY PC's firing obligation and is independently testable without asserting any PredatorService internals (it checks the mock's received-signal count, nothing more). (Verifies the single-trigger-channel contract on PC's side.)
- **H.60a** (PredatorService `OnPlayerT6Respawned` HANDLER — clearance + state-flush + eligibility, [R16-2] / AI-I1; **CROSS-GDD — a PredatorService obligation PC declares; ADVISORY here against a mock until the Predator AI GDD is authored, reverse-cited in F.4**) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN the signal `OnPlayerT6Respawned(A)` delivered with A's `lastPredatorDamageTimestamp[A]` non-nil, `predatorCausedImminent[A] = true`, `predatorCausedImminentSetTimestamp[A]` set, and a remembered prior position for A, WHEN the handler runs, THEN it clears all three entries to `nil` (paired reset), performs the state-flush (drops the remembered prior position / search focus so A is not re-acquired from death-point memory), and restores target-eligibility; a duplicate signal is a no-op (idempotent). **Vacuity + classification (round-20 qa relabel — closes the round-19 #6 finding that H.60a was mislabeled):** this AC tests a **mock PredatorService that implements the very handler it asserts**, so against the mock it is **vacuous coverage** and is correctly **ADVISORY**, NOT a BLOCKING gate — it is a placeholder that becomes a real AUTO-INTEGRATION gate only when the Predator AI GDD authors the handler (reverse-cited in F.4). **The BLOCKING AUTO-INTEGRATION gate for this lifecycle lives entirely on PC's signal-FIRE side (H.60 — PC fires `OnPlayerT6Respawned` exactly once, single trigger channel)**, which IS machine-checkable against PC today. The split is deliberate: PC owns and gates the fire; PredatorService owns (and will gate) the handler. (Verifies the handler's clearance+flush behavior against a mock — ADVISORY/vacuous until the real GDD; the BLOCKING obligation is H.60's fire-side.)
- **H.61** (BC4 all-dead suppression — round-16 Crafting adoption) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a mocked RunController arbiter and the H.47 stub emitter, WHEN `OnEscapeBeaconActivated(activatorPlayerId, timestamp, windowDurationSeconds)` fires (PC's `_beaconActivated` latch set) and THEN all squad members enter S4 with squad oxygen `< 1`, THEN PC raises **NO** `RunEndConditionRaised("wipe", …)` — for the WHOLE post-activation lifecycle, not merely a 0.5 s hold (advance the injected clock well past `RUN_END_DEFEAT_HOLD`; still no raise). **Paired pre-activation positive:** the identical all-dead condition WITHOUT a prior `OnEscapeBeaconActivated` raises `"wipe"` exactly once (H.49 behavior). **Paired latch-release case (round-18 qa — the release TRIGGER is now asserted, closing the Pillar-4 one-way-ratchet risk; round-20 R17-4 carve-out for the self-heal):** the `_beaconActivated` latch is cleared by the arbiter's `RunEnded` broadcast (primary) **OR the `BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` self-heal backstop (H.61c) — and by nothing else**. The test asserts BOTH directions: **(a) it DOES clear on `RunEnded`** — after the arbiter broadcasts `RunEnded`, a subsequent run's pre-activation all-dead condition raises `"wipe"` again, proving the latch did not stick `true` across the run boundary (the ratchet that would suppress T7 *forever* — a Rounds-Not-Saves / Pillar-4 regression); and **(b) it does NOT clear mid-run on any non-`RunEnded` event WITHIN the `BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` window** — advancing through BC4 with `OnSquadMemberAliveChanged` edges, a T6 respawn, and arbitrary Heartbeats (no `RunEnded`, clock kept inside the timeout) leaves the latch `true`, so suppression holds for the whole post-activation lifecycle. (The timeout self-heal itself — clearing after `BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` with no `RunEnded` — is H.61c's subject, deliberately out of scope here so (b)'s "non-`RunEnded` events leave it true" claim is asserted only inside the timeout window.) The latch's clear has **(primary)** exactly one message trigger (the inbound `RunEnded`) **plus (self-heal) the `BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` backstop (H.61c)**; set has exactly one (`OnEscapeBeaconActivated`). (Verifies C.5.8/C.6 T7: post-activation, Crafting's BCT-DEFEAT owns the wipe terminal — and the suppression latch is neither a one-way ratchet nor mid-run-clearable except by its declared clear-paths.)
- **H.61b** (`_beaconActivated` bootstrap false-init + N+1-run fresh start — round-20 [R16-2] bootstrap-fanout closure, build-B2/SD-19-I4) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN `PlayerControllerService` at server/run start with `getServerTime()` injected, THEN immediately after bootstrap (before any `OnEscapeBeaconActivated`) `_beaconActivated == false` AND `_beaconActivatedAt == nil` are asserted as observable post-bootstrap state (the C.5.3 bootstrap enumeration — `_beaconActivated` is listed alongside `reconcileRows`/`currentDeathRecord`/`lastTickTime`, not left implicit), so the very first T7 evaluation reads a real `false`, never a `nil`-indexed latch. **Paired N+1-run fresh-start:** after a full run completes (`OnEscapeBeaconActivated` set the latch, then the inbound `RunEnded` cleared it), a fresh run begins with `_beaconActivated == false` again — the latch never ratchets across run boundaries, and the next run's pre-activation all-dead correctly raises `"wipe"` (the Pillar-4 no-permanent-suppression guarantee, complementary to H.61's clear-on-`RunEnded` assertion). (Verifies the round-18 half-finished fanout is closed: the latch is enumerated at the bootstrap site AND starts every run `false`.)
- **H.61c** (`BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` self-heal — round-20 R17-4, user-ruled 2026-06-14) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN the `_beaconActivated` latch SET by `OnEscapeBeaconActivated` (recording `_beaconActivatedAt`), `getServerTime()` injected, AND **no inbound `RunEnded` is ever delivered** (simulating a dropped/never-sent arbiter broadcast), WHEN the injected clock advances past `_beaconActivatedAt + BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` and a reconciliation-tick Heartbeat fires the self-heal guard, THEN the guard force-clears `_beaconActivated → false` (and `_beaconActivatedAt → nil`), emits a `beacon-latch-timeout` warning, and the T7 wipe terminal is re-enabled (a subsequent all-dead + oxygen `< 1` raises `"wipe"` again). **Paired negative (no premature self-heal):** with the clock advanced only to `_beaconActivatedAt + BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT − ε` (still inside the timeout) the latch stays `true` and T7 stays suppressed — the self-heal never fires during a legitimate Beacon window (the floor is above the longest legitimate `activation → RunEnded` span). **Paired straggler-`RunEnded`:** a `RunEnded` arriving AFTER the self-heal already cleared the latch is the idempotent no-op the C.9 inbound handler specifies (latch already clear, players already resolved). **Paired double-`RunEnded` (round-22 qa — the no-self-heal idempotency case the C.9 handler clause (3) claims but no AC bound):** with the latch SET normally and NO self-heal involved, two `RunEnded(outcome)` broadcasts delivered for the same run (a duplicated/replayed arbiter broadcast) resolve to exactly one effect — the FIRST clears `_beaconActivated` and drives all players to S5 by `outcome`; the SECOND is a full no-op (latch already `false`, all players already in S5, no second S5 transition, no `OnPlayerDied`/state churn, no re-raise). The test asserts the post-second-broadcast server state is byte-identical to the post-first-broadcast state. (Verifies the Pillar-4 self-heal — a wedged run cannot persist past `BEACON_LATCH_TIMEOUT` — without firing during normal play, AND the C.9 inbound-handler clause-(3) double-`RunEnded` idempotency in the ordinary no-self-heal path.)
- **H.62** (`RequestGather` trust boundary — round-16 B5) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN the C.9 registry limits, WHEN `RequestGather {nodeId}` arrives (a) from a player in S4 → rejected (alive-guard); (b) with an unknown/stale `nodeId` → rejected silently; (c) from a player whose server-tracked `HumanoidRootPart.Position` is farther than `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 4 studs` from the node center → rejected (server-side proximity re-check — a spoofed armed-prompt state is not trusted); (d) at a rate exceeding 2/s + burst 4/2.0 s → excess dropped silently; and (e) alive + valid `nodeId` + in range + within limits → forwarded to the Resource Node receive side. (Verifies PC registers the trust boundary; RN owns deep validation.)
- **H.63** (`RequestInteract` trust boundary — round-16 B5) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — same shape as H.62 against `RequestInteract {targetId}`: S4/dead reject, unknown `targetId` reject, out-of-range reject (server-tracked position vs `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS` of the target), rate limit 2/s + burst 4/2.0 s, and the valid case forwarded to the Crafting receive side. (Verifies the second round-16 trust-boundary registration.)
- **H.64** (step-4 thrown-error guard — round-16 B8; the sole stuck-`InFlight` prevention gets test binding) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a death record dispatched through the C.5.3 sequence with `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN an error is **thrown** (not a failure result — e.g. the success/failure classifier itself throws, injected via the mock) inside the guarded span while `state == "InFlight"` (after the step-2 transition, before any commit), THEN the record is **NOT left `InFlight`**: the whole-span error guard transitions it `InFlight → Pending` (rollback/handoff path), `inFlightSince` is cleared to `nil`, an operator log line is emitted reading the record's snapshots (`row.playerName`/`row.userId` — never the live instance), the squad-oxygen ledger is unchanged, and the row is selected by the next reconciliation tick. **Injection site pinned (round-18 qa — previously unpinned):** the thrown error is injected at a NAMED site — immediately after the step-3 `pcall` returns and BEFORE the step-4 `InFlight → Committed` write (i.e., in the success/failure classifier evaluation while `state == "InFlight"`), via the mock classifier hook; a SECOND variant injects inside the `task.spawn`'d reconciliation-retry coroutine body (the non-`pcall`'d-line case the whole-span guard exists for). Both variants assert the `InFlight → Pending` rollback. **Lemur caveat (C.11):** the time-advance portion is deterministic under Lemur via the clock seam, but the Path-B/destroyed-instance variant (error thrown on a reconciliation retry AFTER the `Player` was removed) is **MANUAL-DEVICE/in-Studio** — Lemur does not reproduce `Players.PlayerRemoving` instance-destruction timing with full fidelity, so the snapshot-log-safety assertion (the log reads `row.playerName`, never `player.Name`) must be confirmed at least once in live Studio/TestService. (Verifies the SD-B4 whole-span error guard — the single-point-of-failure path that previously had zero AC coverage — with the injection site pinned and the Lemur-fidelity boundary labeled.)
- **H.64b** (post-`Committed` side-effect error does NOT roll back — round-20 qa split from H.64; two independent outcomes were conflated in one AC) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a death record that has reached `state == "Committed"` (the step-3 spend succeeded and the step-5 `state == "InFlight"` re-check passed), `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN an error is **thrown inside the post-`Committed` block** — the `OnPlayerDied` broadcast or any of side-effects (a)–(f) throws — THEN the error is **caught and logged (reading the record snapshots) but the record stays `Committed`**: it is NOT rolled back to `Pending`, the squad-oxygen ledger is NOT re-debited and NOT refunded, no second `RequestSquadOxygenSpend` is ever invoked for the death event, and the reconciliation tick does NOT re-select the row (it is `Committed`, not `Pending`). (Verifies the SD-B4 reorder guarantee — the broadcast + (a)–(g) run AFTER the `Committed` transition, so a side-effect throw can never reopen the charge; split from H.64 so the pre-commit rollback and the post-commit no-rollback are independently asserted, each with its own GIVEN/WHEN/THEN.)
- **H.65** (T6 oxygen-gated respawn wait — round-16) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a 2-player squad where player A is in S4 with the respawn timer elapsed, player B alive, and squad oxygen pool `= 0`, WHEN server Heartbeats continue, THEN A REMAINS in S4 (no transition, no timer restart, no additional oxygen charge), the S4 input set (emote/spectate; `RequestPing` CUT in S4 per R17-3) stays accepted, and the T6 condition is re-evaluated each Heartbeat; WHEN the pool is restored to `≥ 1` (mock RM restore), THEN A respawns via a normal T6 on the FIRST Heartbeat at which the pool reads `≥ 1`. (Verifies the E.E oxygen-gated wait: timer-elapsed + pool < 1 + no wipe is a defined held state, not an undefined corner.)
- **H.66** (`deathEventId` monotonic-counter uniqueness — round-16 qa) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a server session in which N death events occur across a mix of Path A deaths, Path B disconnects, a rejoin-then-death under the same `userId`, and a multi-life sequence for one player, WHEN all minted `deathEventId`s are collected, THEN they are strictly increasing integers, none is ever reused within the session, every event got exactly one id, and no id derives from any client-supplied value (server-authoritative identity per C.5.3 `nextDeathEventId()`). (Verifies the monotonic-counter contract directly rather than via its consumers.)
- **H.67** (`RequestEmote` slot validation — round-16 qa) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — WHEN `RequestEmote` arrives with `slot` = `2.5` (non-integer), `7` / `0` / `-1` (out-of-range), `"3"` (non-number), or `nil`, THEN each is rejected server-side (no `OnEmoteBroadcast`, no cooldown charged); `slot ∈ {1,…,6}` as an integer is accepted within rate limits. (Encodes the C.9 I4 validation as a test binding.) **Round-28 cross-ref:** `RequestEmote` carries ONLY `slot` — the dead-state render discriminator `renderScope` is **server-minted** and its gate is **H.80** (not here); H.67 covers slot validation + rate-limit only, and a client-supplied `renderScope` has no inbound field to validate (H.80 asserts it is ignored).
- **H.68** (`RequestPing` origin tolerance — round-16 qa) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN player A's server-tracked `HumanoidRootPart.Position` = `P`, WHEN `RequestPing` arrives with `rayOriginPos` farther than `PING_ORIGIN_TOLERANCE = 8 studs` from `P`, THEN it is rejected (no broadcast, origin-plausibility trust boundary); a `rayOriginPos` within the tolerance is accepted and the server-side raycast result is broadcast (client `candidateTargetId` advisory per C.4.1/E.F). (Encodes the R6-B4 origin validation as a test binding.)
- **H.69** (mid-session-join initialization — round-16 qa) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN an active run in progress, WHEN a new player joins mid-run and immediately (same-frame onward) sprints, raises the lantern, pings, and then dies, THEN no `nil`-arithmetic error occurs on any code path: `currentDeathRecord[joiner] == nil` at join, grace anchors are `nil` (first sprint/light pulses grace-exempt), `_lastHeartbeatTime` has no entry until T1 seeds it, `lastServerTrackedPosition[joiner]` is populated by the first Heartbeat upkeep and nil-guarded before that, and the joiner's death mints a normal record. (Verifies the C.5.3 mid-session-join init rule — per-player tables are join-time-independent.)
- **H.70** (reconciliation-tick side-effect scope — round-16 qa) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a death whose initial charge rolled back and which recovers on a reconciliation tick, in BOTH the live-pointer case (player still in S4) and the orphaned case (player departed/respawned), WHEN the tick commits, THEN the dispatch invokes ONLY the player-removal-safe subset **(a) sprint pulse-timer stop, (b) lantern force-T4/Light timer stop, (g) `:ReleasePredatorLock`** and **suppresses (c)–(f) AND the round-16 (h)/(i) signals**: the test asserts the spectator camera is NOT re-activated, stamina is NOT re-frozen, the grace slate is NOT re-reset, the input lock is NOT re-applied, `OnPlayerS4Entered` is NOT re-fired, and `OnSquadMemberAliveChanged` is NOT re-fired — the full (a)–(i) set runs only on the initial T5 dispatch. (Binds the R13-build-G2/R13-net-I2 carve-out to a test.)
- **H.71** (reconciliation-recovery chime — round-16 audio) — AUTO-INTEGRATION (audio-routing logic) — GIVEN captions/audio routing under test, WHEN `OnPlayerDied(deathCause="reconciliation-recovery")` is received, THEN the non-positional UI-channel notification chime (V/A.3 R12-AD-B3) fires coincident with the HUD roster-confirm indication, AND the positional Death SFX and the "[Name] is down" caption are both suppressed (R6-B12/R6-I10); WHEN `deathCause ∈ {"died", "disconnect-while-damaged"}`, THEN the chime does NOT fire (those causes route to the Death SFX + caption channels). (Verifies the chime's cause-gating in both directions.)
- **H.72** (`OnSquadMemberAliveChanged` edges + positioned-HRP timing — round-16 Crafting adoption) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a mock subscriber recording `(playerId, isAlive)` tuples, WHEN player A dies via Path A, THEN exactly one `(A, false)` fires at T5; WHEN a player dies via Path B (disconnect-while-imminent), THEN exactly one `(thatPlayer, false)` fires; WHEN A respawns at T6, THEN exactly one `(A, true)` fires and ONLY AFTER A's respawned `HumanoidRootPart` exists AND is positioned at the respawn anchor (the test asserts the signal-time HRP position equals the anchor, never the world origin — Crafting's positioned-HRP re-add guarantee). A replayed `Humanoid.Died` fires no duplicate edge (the record short-circuit precedes the side-effect). (Verifies PC's side of Crafting's BLOCKING `aliveMembers` contract.)
- **H.73** (V/A.4a caption-queue durable-FIFO insert-without-evict — round-18 AD-5; **was a no-AC gap**) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN the single-line `CaptionsOverlay` with the V/A.4a 3 s per-caption hold, WHEN a burst of `K` deaths (`2 ≤ K ≤ 4`) with distinct `deathEventId`s and ascending `deathTimestamp`s lands within one 3 s hold window (so they cannot all show at once), THEN every one of the `K` shown-cause captions (`deathCause ∈ {"died","disconnect-while-damaged"}`) is **durably enqueued and eventually shown — none evicted or dropped** — in ascending `deathTimestamp` order, each held the full 3 s, for a bounded total of `K × 3 s` (`K ≤` squad size 4). The test asserts: (a) the queue depth never silently caps — no death's caption is lost while the queue is non-empty (insert-without-evict); (b) a late-arriving `reconciliation-recovery` (itself caption-suppressed) does NOT evict a pending or in-progress shown-cause caption; (c) the dedup-by-`deathEventId` rule still holds under the burst (a replayed `Humanoid.Died`, or a `PlayerRemoving` no-op against an already-`Committed` record, enqueues no second caption for an already-queued/shown event). (Verifies the V/A.4a insert-without-evict durability — the caption-legibility floor: a player can always read who went down even under the squad's worst simultaneous-death burst; closes the round-17 AD-5 "durable-FIFO insert-without-evict has no AC" gap.)

#### H.16 — Round-20 Closures (R17-1..R17-4 rulings + the round-19 gating wiring/AC sweep; AC numbering append-only)

- **H.74** (`RequestLanternToggle` burst cap + same-state debounce + drop-first ordering — round-20 NET-B1) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player whose lantern axis is in S3a (lowered), `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN `RequestLanternToggle {raised=false}` arrives (same-state as current), THEN it is **dropped before any side-effect** — no lantern transition, no Light pulse, and critically **`t_lastGracePulse_light` is NOT touched** (drop-first ordering: the same-state/rate checks evaluate before any grace-anchor read). **Burst-cap case:** WHEN more than 8 `RequestLanternToggle` events arrive within 2.0 s, THEN events beyond the cap are dropped silently (no transition, no error) while the 5/s per-second limit also holds. **Exploit-parity case:** a flood of alternating-then-same-state toggles cannot produce more grace-exempt Light pulses than the C.8.2 cooldown permits (the same-state drops never re-anchor the cooldown). (Verifies the NET-B1 burst cap + same-state debounce + drop-first ordering — exploit-parity with `RequestSprintToggle`, not an authority change.)
- **H.75** (dispatch-origin discriminator — round-20 SD-19-B2) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a death dispatched through the C.5.3 sequence with a mock RM held mid-yield and `getServerTime()` injected, the dispatch captures `dispatchOrigin = "initial"`, WHEN the player **departs mid-yield** (so at the step-5 commit there is NO live `currentDeathRecord` pointer — orphaned exactly like a reconciliation orphan) AND the held spend then resolves successfully, THEN the commit selects the **initial** side-effect set from `dispatchOrigin` (the `OnPlayerDied` broadcast + (a)/(b)/(c)–(f) run; NOT the reconciliation-only (a)/(b)/(g) subset — the test asserts (c)–(f) DID run **via their server-observable proxies, NOT client state (round-22 qa — names the proxies so the assertion is machine-checkable on the server)**: the `OnPlayerDied` broadcast fired (initial-only — the cleanest discriminator, absent on the reconciliation subset), the server-side **input-lock flag is set `true`** ((e)), the **stamina value is frozen** at its death-instant value and does not advance on subsequent Heartbeats ((c)), the **grace anchors `t_lastGracePulse_sprint`/`_light` are reset to `nil`** ((d)), and the **spectator-camera-activate signal was emitted** ((f) — observable as the server's camera-state event, not the client render); proving the set was chosen by `dispatchOrigin`, not by live-pointer presence), while the **orphan removal** is selected by live-pointer-absence (the `Committed` row is removed immediately, not retained for a T6 that will never come). **Paired reconciliation case:** a genuine reconciliation-tick dispatch (`dispatchOrigin = "reconciliation"`) whose player is still in S4 (live pointer) runs ONLY (a)/(b)/(g), suppresses (c)–(f), and RETAINS the row until T6 — confirming the two axes (`dispatchOrigin` → side-effect SET; live-pointer → retain/remove) are independent. (Verifies the SD-19-B2 seam fix: a mid-yield-orphaned initial dispatch is not mis-routed to the reconciliation subset.)

#### H.17 — Round-22 Closures (R21-1/R21-2/R21-3 rulings + the round-21 gating AC sweep; AC numbering append-only)

- **H.76** (`EMOTE_WHEEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT` auto-close — round-22 qa; **round-28 T-3 RECLASSIFIED to a single MANUAL-DEVICE / client-integration ADVISORY AC, qa Miss #11**) — **Reclassification rationale (the round-24 "Part 1 AUTO-UNIT headless BLOCKING" split was vacuous):** the emote-wheel-open state, the idle timer, the auto-close, and the T9 gather lockout are **all client-side** (C.4.2; H.34 is explicit — while the wheel is open the gather input is "suppressed **client-side** and **no `RequestGather` reaches the server**"). There is **no server-side wheel-open state**: the server only ever sees `RequestEmote` on a *commit*, so an open-but-never-committed wheel is invisible to the server. The round-24 Part 1 "server-observable proxy" — *"a `RequestGather` the server suppressed while the wheel was open is now accepted"* — therefore tests something **the server never does**: the server accepts that `RequestGather` regardless of wheel state, so the proxy passes **VACUOUSLY in headless CI (a false pass)**. There is no headless server contract to gate here. The genuinely security-critical emote properties (zero disturbance, slot validation, rate-limit, the dead-state `renderScope`) are gated server-side elsewhere (H.19, H.67, C.9) and are unaffected. **The AC (MANUAL-DEVICE / client-integration, ADVISORY):** on a touch device, GIVEN a player opens the emote wheel (client T9 gather lock engaged) and makes no slot selection, WHEN `EMOTE_WHEEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT = 4.0 s` (G.4) elapses, THEN the wheel auto-closes client-side, **no emote is committed** (the client sends no `RequestEmote`, so no `OnEmoteBroadcast` and no `EMOTE_COOLDOWN_PER_PLAYER` charge — a subsequent legitimate emote is accepted), and the **client-side T9 gather lock releases** (the right-thumb gather gesture is interactable again); a paired observation confirms the wheel is still open at `open + timeout − ε`. Evidence logged to `production/qa/evidence/emote-wheel-idle-close.md`. (Verifies the C.4.2 accidental-open lockout — an accidental open cannot hold the T9 gather lockout indefinitely; closes the round-21 zero-coverage gap. **Round-28: the false AUTO-UNIT BLOCKING label is removed — the mechanism is client-side and not headlessly verifiable in Lemur; the prior split implied a machine-checkable server gate that does not exist.**)
- **H.77** (`RequestSprintToggle` burst-cap + same-state debounce — round-22 qa; ZERO coverage despite the H.74 lantern precedent) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player in S1 with `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN `RequestSprintToggle {sprint=false}` arrives (same-state as current S1), THEN it is **dropped before any side-effect** (debounced same-state, C.9 — no transition, no `OnSprintStateChanged`, and no grace-anchor read). **Burst-cap case:** WHEN more than 15 `RequestSprintToggle` events arrive within 2.0 s (above the C.9 `burst cap 15 per 2.0 s`), THEN events beyond the cap are dropped silently while the 10/s per-second limit also holds. **Exploit-parity case:** a flood of alternating-then-same-state toggles cannot drive more sprint state transitions (and therefore more grace-exempt Sprint pulses) than the C.8.1 cooldown permits — the same-state drops never re-anchor `t_lastGracePulse_sprint`. **Paired alternating-from-S2 burst case (round-28 T-3, qa Miss #4 — the H.77 precedent covered same-state from S1 but not the toggle-burst originating from the *sprinting* state):** GIVEN a player in **S2** (sprinting), WHEN a burst of alternating `RequestSprintToggle {sprint=false}`/`{sprint=true}` events arrives at above-cap rate (so each carries a genuine state change and is NOT same-state-dropped), THEN the burst cap (15 per 2.0 s) + the 10/s per-second limit still bound the number of *accepted* T1 sprint-entries, so the number of grace-exempt Sprint first-pulses the burst can manufacture is capped at the C.8.1 cooldown-permitted rate — a player cannot harvest extra grace-exempt pulses by rapidly toggling sprint off-then-on from S2 (the `GRACE_REENTRY_COOLDOWN` anchor still gates each re-entry, H.11h). (Verifies the C.9 `RequestSprintToggle` burst cap + same-state debounce as a test binding — exploit-parity with H.74's lantern gate, including the from-S2 alternating-burst path; closes the round-21 zero-coverage gap and the round-28 qa Miss #4.)
- **H.78** (world-response cue trigger→render wiring — round-22 R21-1; the R17-1 loop-closer had no AC) — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a `PlayerControllerService` subscribed to a mocked Ecological-Disturbance `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` producer and a recording squad of 2 living players + 1 player in S4, `getServerTime()` injected, WHEN the mock raises `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed(direction="up", newTier=N, timestamp=T)`, THEN PC fires **exactly one** `OnWorldResponseCue {newTier=N}` to **each LIVING squad client only** (the S4 player receives nothing), the payload carries `newTier=N` and **NO position/direction/distance field** (the test asserts the payload schema is non-locating). **Non-vacuous anchor (round-24 qa BLK-1 — closes the empty-handler-passes class, same as r19 H.59/H.60a):** the negatives below are asserted **in the same test body, AFTER** the positive up-crossing above has already fired **exactly one** `OnWorldResponseCue` (the assert-cue-fired anchor) — so a PC that never subscribed to `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` (an empty/unwired handler) **fails the positive cue-fired assertion and can never reach a vacuous pass on the negatives**; the test proves the live subscription is exercised, not merely that nothing happened. **Paired down-crossing negative:** with the subscription proven live by the anchor, a `direction="down"` crossing fires **NO** `OnWorldResponseCue`. **Paired per-pulse negative:** a stream of Sprint/Light emission pulses with no band-crossing fires **NO** `OnWorldResponseCue` (the cue is band-crossing-driven, never per-pulse) — and because the anchor already proved the handler is wired, a zero-emit here is a real negative, not an unwired no-op. **Paired hysteresis note:** the producer's debounce/hysteresis is ED-owned (mocked here); this AC verifies ONLY PC's up-filter + living-squad re-broadcast, not the producer cadence (that lands with ED Session B/C, F.4). (Verifies the C.9 `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed`→`OnWorldResponseCue` render wiring on PC's side; closes the round-21 #1b "no trigger→cue→player AC" gap.)
- **H.78a** (world-response cue audibility floor — round-22 R21-3; parity with H.57a) — split into a config gate + a mix-review evidence item. **Part 1 (AUTO-UNIT, config-validation gate, BLOCKING — round-24 CONVERGENCE A: names the authored field + corrects the floor domain):** GIVEN the audio config, WHEN config-validation runs, THEN it asserts the floor constant is **in its registered range — `WORLD_RESPONSE_CUE_VOLUME_FLOOR >= 3.0`** (a `> 0` check is the wrong domain: the floor is a dB-above-ambient value with range 3.0–6.0 dB per G.9, so any value `< 3.0` is itself a misconfiguration, not merely a non-positive one); AND a config whose **authored swell-level field `worldResponseCueGainDb`** — the per-mix authored world-response swell level, expressed in **dB above the named Hunt-tier ambient bed**, the schema field the gate compares against (parity with how H.57a Part 1 compares the authored hold-variant volume) — is below `WORLD_RESPONSE_CUE_VOLUME_FLOOR` is **rejected** (both values are dB-above-ambient against the same Hunt-tier baseline H.57a Part 2 names, so the comparison is domain-consistent; the floor governs the swell's sustained level per the V/A.3 round-24 R23-1(b) envelope). **Part 2 (MANUAL-DEVICE, ADVISORY — mix-review evidence per the coding-standards Visual/Feel gate; BINARY pass criterion):** against the **named Hunt-tier ambient bed** (the loudest ambient tier a band-crossing realistically fires at) at the device's median user volume on iPhone SE-class hardware with no other squad SFX active, a hearing playtester confirms — as a **binary pass/fail**, evidence logged to `production/qa/evidence/world-response-cue-mix-review.md` — that **(a)** the world-response swell is audible above the Hunt-tier ambient on an upward band-crossing, AND **(b)** it reads as a low, non-directional "the world stirred" registration, NOT as a directional/positional alarm or a predator tell (the V/A.3 anti-pillar — the tester confirms no perceived direction/distance), AND **(c)** it is **timbrally distinct** from the own-player sprint-pulse exertion breath (the tester does not confuse the two cues), AND **(d) (round-24 R23-1(b) onset operationalization, audio narrow-gate ADVISORY B1)** the **fast 50–80 ms onset transient is itself detectable** at first-crossing against the Hunt-tier ambient (the cue is not "discovered" only once the sustain arrives) **AND reads as a tonal/atmospheric onset, NOT a percussive click/alarm transient** (the onset must clear the masking floor without crossing into a predator-tell/alarm read). (Verifies the cue's audio channel cannot be silently zeroed by a mix pass AND is perceptually a non-alarm world-registration against a DEFINED worst-case ambient — Part 1 is the machine-checkable floor, Part 2 confirms it is perceptually sufficient and anti-pillar-compliant against the Hunt-tier baseline, not merely non-zero against silence; the binary criterion + evidence-file path close the round-21 "gate against a vacuum / no binary pass / no evidence path" audio finding.)

#### H.18 — Round-24 Closures (R23-1 ruling + the round-23 gating cue-completeness/accessibility sweep; AC numbering append-only)

- **H.79** (world-response caption shape/icon redundancy — round-24 UX-B1; the caption was color-only, violating the game-concept accessibility floor) — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN the V/A.4 world-response caption render spec, WHEN the cue renders on an upward band-crossing, THEN the `CaptionsOverlay` line presents **both** the neutral-non-danger color **and** a non-directional concentric-ring "world-stir" glyph (a redundant non-color channel), AND the glyph carries **NO directional/positional shape** (no arrow/compass/heading — the test asserts the glyph asset is drawn from the non-directional set), AND under the motion-reduction toggle (V/A.5) the glyph static-shows (no animated ring expansion). **Paired accessibility-floor assertion:** with color information removed (a greyscale / color-blind simulation of the caption render), the cue is still distinguishable as the world-response cue by glyph shape alone. **Color-contrast assertion (round-28 ux gate, UX-R26-1):** the rendered caption color achieves ≥ 3:1 contrast against the dark caption-strip background (WCAG 1.4.11), and a luminance-collapse (greyscale) of the rendered color does NOT coincide with the danger-category caption color (so a low-vision player cannot confuse the world-response line with a danger line on luminance alone). (Verifies color is never the sole channel for the cue AND the color itself is contrast- and colorblind-safe — game-concept accessibility floor; closes round-23 UX-B1 + round-28 UX-R26-1.)
- **H.80** (emote-while-dead world-space billboard suppression — round-24 UX-B2; **round-28 T-1/T-3 RE-SCOPED to gate the server-minted `renderScope` discriminator, NET-2 closure**) — split into a server-observable part + a client-device part. **Re-scope rationale (the round-24 Part 1 was a true-but-insufficient proxy):** the prior Part 1 asserted "the S4 emote-broadcast payload carries NO positional field." That is true but does **NOT** close the dead-position tell — the corpse `HumanoidRootPart` is still present at a known position, so a client (especially an exploit client ignoring a client-side routing rule) can resolve the corpse position itself and render a billboard there regardless of whether the payload carries a position (NET-2: the suppression must be **server-driven**, expressed authoritatively in the broadcast, not left to a client routing convention). **Part 1 (AUTO-UNIT, headless, BLOCKING):** GIVEN a player in S4 (dead; corpse `HumanoidRootPart` still present) who fires `RequestEmote`, WHEN the server processes it, THEN the emote is accepted and `OnEmoteBroadcast` is sent with **`renderScope == "squad-ui"`** — a **server-minted** value the test asserts is set from the emitter's server-tracked S4 state (the test also asserts a client-supplied `renderScope` in the inbound `RequestEmote` is ignored — the field does not exist on the request and could not override the minted value), AND the payload carries **NO world-position / no corpse-anchor field**. **Paired positive contrast (makes the negative non-vacuous):** a **living** player's `RequestEmote` broadcasts `renderScope == "world"` — so the discriminator is **S4-specific and server-driven**, not a global removal; a build that minted the same `renderScope` for everyone (or that never set it) fails one of the two contrasted assertions. **Part 2 (MANUAL-DEVICE, ADVISORY):** on a client, an emote fired by a dead teammate (`renderScope == "squad-ui"`) shows in the squad roster / spectator overlay but renders **no world-space billboard over the corpse**, while a living teammate's emote (`renderScope == "world"`) renders the normal billboard; evidence logged to `production/qa/evidence/dead-emote-billboard.md`. (Verifies the round-24 UX-B2 ruling + the round-26/27 NET-2 tightening — the suppression is server-authoritative via the minted `renderScope`, HUD owns render; a corpse-located billboard would be the dead-position tell R17-3 cut S4 ping for; closes round-23 UX-B2 and the round-27 H.80-Part-1-unimplementable finding.)

#### H.19 — Round-28 Closures (template T-1..T-7 vacuous/mislabeled-gate fixes + extracted ACs; AC numbering append-only)

- **H.81** (Light-axis no-floor — the Sprint-axis-ONLY security property; round-28 T-3, qa Miss #10 — extracted from H.12's embedded prose) — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `t = t_0 + 4.0` (grace expired), `d_xz = 0.5 stud`, `platform = "pc"`, WHEN `GetPulseMagnitude` is called for **Light**, THEN the stationary attenuation gives `MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE × STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR = 0.08 × 0.30 = 0.024` with **NO hold-floor applied** — the result is **exactly `0.024`, NOT raised to `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR`** (the R10-6 hold-floor is **Sprint-axis-ONLY**). **Paired Sprint contrast (makes the negative non-vacuous):** the same GIVEN for Sprint returns `max(SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR, 0.03) = SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR` (per H.12/H.12a) — so a build that wrongly applied the floor to the Light axis would fail this AC's `== 0.024` assertion while the Sprint case still passes, proving the floor is axis-specific. (Verifies the security property that keeps the round-1 lantern-cycle exploit closed: if the hold-floor leaked onto the Light axis, a stationary raised lantern would emit at the Sprint floor and the Light-axis stationary attenuation that closes the lantern-toggle exploit would be defeated. Was embedded as prose in H.12 — round-28 T-3 makes it an independently runnable AC so a CI run cannot skip it silently.)
- **H.82** (D.1/D.2 negative-`dt` guard — round-28 qa-I2; the normative R12-SD-6 guard had ZERO AC coverage despite an explicit logic-inversion hazard) — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `stamina = 80` and sprint state active (D.1 drain path), WHEN `TickStamina(dt = -0.05)` is called (a negative frame delta from a clock anomaly / anomalous `RunService.Heartbeat` arg), THEN `stamina` remains **exactly `80`** — the `dt = math.max(0, dt)` guard clamps `dt` to `0` BEFORE the D.1 evaluation, so the drain formula cannot *refund* stamina on a negative delta. **Paired regen case:** GIVEN `stamina = 50`, sprint inactive, regen-delay expired (D.2 regen path), WHEN `TickStamina(dt = -0.05)` is called, THEN `stamina` remains **exactly `50`** — the same guard clamps `dt` to `0`, so the regen formula cannot *drain* stamina on a negative delta. (Verifies the R12-SD-6 normative negative-`dt` guard for BOTH the drain and regen formulas — the logic-inversion hazard the D.1/D.2 guard prose flags, previously asserted nowhere: H.4/H.5 exercise only positive `dt`.)

Captures items that emerged during design but were not fully resolved. Each has an owner and target resolution date.

|---|---|---|---|
| OQ.1 | **CLOSED — Theme 1 (2026-05-01)**: Walk footstep audio is NOT a predator-perception channel. The predator consumes only discrete `DisturbanceService:Emit()` mechanical contracts (`SprintEmission`, `LightEmission`, plus any contracts the Predator AI GDD's Perception submodule defines). No positional 3D audio from the Player Controller is an input the predator processes. Theme 3 (downstream contract) must enumerate the full predator-perception input set in F.4. | audio-director (closed) | Closed 2026-05-01 |
| OQ.2 | Should jump have a custom animation / SFX, or is the Roblox `Humanoid` default acceptable for MVP? | art-director + audio-director | Before first internal playtest (data-driven) |
| OQ.3 | A future **Camera GDD** does not exist in the systems index. Spectator camera (C.5.6 + E.E), camera FOV behaviour during sprint (V/A.7), and respawn camera fade are owned there. Should we add a Camera system to the systems-index (raising MVP system count from 7 to 8), or fold it into Player Controller? | game-designer + producer | Before `/create-architecture` |
| OQ.4 | The `workspace:HasChunkLoaded()` API name is unverified post-cutoff (Roblox is a live platform). Does this exact method still exist? Architecture phase will block on this. | gameplay-programmer | At `/create-architecture` |
| OQ.5 | Stamina drain rate (12.5 units/s = 8 s sprint window) and regen rate (10 units/s = 10 s recovery) are **first-pass design intent**. Are 8 s sprint and 10 s recovery the right *felt* values? Validate by playtest, not desk-calc. | game-designer (first playtest) | After `/prototype` validation |
| OQ.6 | Is `RESPAWN_DELAY = 30 s` (locked at concept) the right pacing for Roblox 5–20 min sessions? Too short undermines Pillar 2 (shared risk); too long loses the dead player's interest. Validate by playtest. | game-designer (first playtest) | After `/prototype` validation |
| OQ.7 | The 4 ADRs flagged for `/create-architecture` (F.4) — are these the complete set, or will architecture surface more decisions? | technical-director (at `/create-architecture`) | At `/create-architecture` |
| OQ.8 | Cosmetic emote slot 4–6 (`All clear`, `Wait`, `Help/SOS`) — what are the **exact monetization rules** for animation-replacement skins? E.g., can a "danced waving" replacement for `All clear` ship in the launch shop, or is the cosmetic-replaceable nature deferred to v1.x? | live-ops-designer + economy-designer | Before launch-shop spec |
| OQ.10 | **Disturbance-bar threshold-probing degenerate (round-24 GD-5 — OQ stub, ED-owned mitigation).** Because the always-on disturbance bar (HUD/ED) is the proactive-quiet signal (Section B, R23-1(a)), a squad could treat it as a meter to *probe* — deliberately riding just below an escalation band to farm the safest-possible loud play, draining the dread the cue is meant to preserve. Mitigation is ED-owned (band hysteresis width; whether the exact bar value vs a coarse tier is shown; deliberate noise/uncertainty on the displayed value) and **couples to the ED Session B/C band-threshold authoring**. What is the anti-probing treatment, and its AC? | game-designer + ecological-disturbance (ED Session B/C) | When ED band thresholds are authored |
| OQ.11 | **2-player Danger→Quiet de-escalation sequence as a named first-playtest target (round-24 GD-4).** At the 2-player floor the full loop — loud play → upward band-crossing → world-response cue → squad chooses to go quiet → (no de-escalation cue, by design) — has never been observed end-to-end. Whether players correctly read the one-directional cue and self-correct to quiet (vs waiting for a "safe again" signal that never comes, R23-1(a)) is the load-bearing experiential question. Flag the 2-player Danger→Quiet sequence as a first-playtest observation target. | game-designer (first playtest) | After `/prototype` validation |
| OQ.9 | **Onboarding teaching beat for "quiet is the easy mode" (round-22 R21-1 paired obligation).** The R17-1 world-response cue (V/A.3, C.9 `OnWorldResponseCue`) closes the *in-run* loud→consequence loop, but the *first-contact* teaching that quiet is the default easy mode is reinforced out-of-run and is NOT owned by PC. Who owns the onboarding beat (a Calm-tier first-zone teaching moment per the F.4 Level-Design row), and what is its AC? An AC stub lands when the onboarding/level-design flow is authored. | onboarding / level-design flow + game-designer | When the onboarding flow is authored |
